The rendered DOM was **not** pixel-verified — the browser pane was blocked by an unresolving policy check in this session, same tooling limitation noted for Findings #89/#90 earlier.

- **"Fix all remaining" sweep — the whole audit backlog closed (2026-08-01).** Continuation of the endpoint-corpus session above; user asked to fix everything still pending. Five items done, two closed as won't-fix with reasons. Suites after: **983 Python passed / 78 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Every fix live-run against the real production DB or the real third-party endpoint.
  - **Finding #31 (regime tilts) — RESOLVED as shrinkage, not a fit, after the stated blocker turned out to be stale AND the real one turned out to be permanent.** `unified_ranker.py`'s docstring claimed no per-category history table existed; `stock_factor_breakdown_history` does exist now and even carries a `regime` column, so that claim was corrected. But measuring the actual data settled it the other way: **BULL has 3 lifetime regime episodes and CRASH 6**, and the factor history (13 distinct dates) covers **zero days of either** — while those two carry the most extreme multipliers (0.30, 1.50). Neither fitting the magnitudes nor validating their sign is possible, and the 170+ HIGH_VOL/SIDEWAYS "episodes" are day-to-day alternation, not independent samples; fitting on that reproduces exactly the overfit-then-write bug Finding #42 fixed in `strategy_optimizer.py`. So the tilts are now treated as **priors and shrunk halfway to neutral** via `shrink_tilt()`/`TILT_SHRINKAGE=0.5` — direction preserved exactly (CRASH still underweights breakout), magnitude damped (a 70% cut becomes 35%). A tilt actually *fitted* from outcomes still overrides at **full** strength, since shrinkage exists to damp intuition and a fitted value is not intuition. New `regime_tilt_fit_readiness()` reports per-regime whether enough history has accumulated to justify fitting, so the item unblocks itself instead of sitting open forever (live: every regime `ready_to_fit=False`). 6 new tests incl. one asserting shrinkage can never flip a tilt across neutral.
  - **Survivorship closed in PRICES — new `ohlcv_adjust.py`, wired into `backtester.load_ohlcv()`.** The tradability half was already done; prices were blocked on split adjustment (bhavcopy RAW vs `stock_ohlcv` split-adjusted). `corporate_actions` was checked and is unusable — 353 rows, dividends only, a six-week range — so factors are derived from NSE's own data. Live result: **delisted companies are tradeable for the first time** — MEGASOFT 1,260 bars ending exactly at its 2026-02-06 delisting, INFIBEAM 1,257, SEQUENT 1,251, all previously absent from `stock_ohlcv` entirely. 649 adjustment events persisted to a new `ohlcv_adjustment_factors` table. **Four wrong turns, each caught only by testing against reality and each of which would silently corrupt a whole price series — this is the durable lesson, not the feature:** (1) the first method assumed NSE's `PREV_CLOSE` is ex-date-adjusted. **It is NOT** (NESTLEIND: `prev_close=27116.4` alongside `close=2666.4`), so the split lives in `close/prev_close` *within one row*, not across rows. The wrong assumption made every real split invisible while surfacing clusters of artifacts — caught only by checking a known split, which is why NESTLEIND and IRCTC are now pinned in the tests. (2) Snapping off the *close* misses real actions when the ex-date has a big genuine move: IRCTC rose 10.6% that day, so `close/prev_close`=0.2212 against a true 1/5, while `open/prev_close`=0.1978. **Snap off the open.** (3) A same-date "cluster" guard added against the old method was silently discarding real events — **India averages ~3 corporate actions per trading day**, so several companies sharing an ex-date is the base rate, not an anomaly; it killed IRCTC's split *after* correctly detecting it. Removed. (4) **Circuit-locked days** trade at a single price exactly at the band, so 1.20/0.80 are indistinguishable from a 6:5 consolidation or 1:4 bonus by ratio alone — before the zero-range guard, **1.2 was the most common "ratio" in the entire history (99 events)**, i.e. the detector was mostly finding circuit locks. Also: the ratio grid must be built as two separate families (small p/q for bonuses, `1/k` and `k/1` only for deep splits) — crossing the large denominators with every numerator invented ratios like 9/25 and made the grid so dense the snap stopped filtering anything.
  - **A real data gap found along the way: `nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py` filtered weekends out of its backfill**, so NSE's live Union Budget session on **Sunday 2026-02-01** (2,955 securities, verified fetchable) was never captured — an entire missing trading day, invisible to every row-count and freshness check. The archive 404s on genuine non-trading days (verified: Saturday 2026-01-31), so probing every calendar day is correct and costs only the 404s. **The missing sessions are NOT yet backfilled**: a parallel sweep to enumerate them got the archive to rate-limit me (403 Access Denied), so the count of affected dates is unknown — artifacts on 2025-02-03 and 2023-11-13 suggest the 2025 Budget Saturday and Diwali Muhurat trading are also missing. Re-run gently (serial, delayed) once unblocked. **Also worth remembering: `archives.nseindia.com` does not honour HEAD** — a HEAD sweep reported 0 sessions where GET returned 349KB of real data.
  - **Endpoint audit §5-2 shipped — `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py`** brings `pctTransacted` (deal size as % of float) into `block_deals`, the cross-sectionally comparable field NSE's own feed lacks (qty/value are absolute: a ₹12cr deal is a control transaction in a microcap and a rounding error in Reliance). Live: 978 rows, 966 with pct, 201 symbols — e.g. EIGHT ROADS INVESTMENTS MAURITIUS exiting **27.41% of SHADOWFAX's float for ₹852cr**. Three endpoint quirks verified and pinned in tests, each a silent-failure mode: `type=block`/`type=bulk` are **ignored** (identical mixed set returned); `skip` is ignored while `offset` paginates (a loop on `skip` re-fetches page 1 forever while looking like progress); and **the feed is not only bulk/block** — it also carries ten flavours of insider filing, so the fetcher filters to Bulk/Block rather than silently changing what `block_deals` means for every existing reader. **Follow-up worth taking: those insider rows carry `pctTransacted`, which `insider_transactions_fetcher.py` (NSE PIT filings) does not** — the same endpoint could materially improve insider features, in that fetcher's table rather than this one.
  - **`live_datasource` coverage 17 → 23 of ~140 fetchers**, prioritised by blast radius rather than count: `test_live_datasource_feature_matrix_fetchers.py` covers five fetchers that write directly into `technical_signals` (delivery_volume, nt_dashboard, preopen, mc_techscanner, trendlyne_fundamentals), plus the two new fetchers above. All verified passing against real endpoints. Writing them re-confirmed the point of the exercise in a small way: two of my own assumptions about response shape were wrong (NiftyTrader returns `{indices, stocks}`, not `resultData`; MTO fields are `deliverable_qty`/`qty_traded`/`delivery_pct`) — the fetchers were healthy, my guesses were not.
  - **Two items CLOSED as won't-fix** (recorded in the intraday audit's new §9 so future passes stop re-opening them): **`adjustment_basis` consumers** — 287 of 2,608,478 rows tagged (0.011%), and backfilling means decompressing 21 of 24 Timescale chunks and permanently losing the compression; the mixed-basis problem it was meant to detect is now handled at source by `ohlcv_adjust.py`. **`screener_appearances` bitemporality** — `screener_membership_snapshot` already makes values reproducible from 2026-07-31 forward, and a bitemporal rebuild cannot recover state that was overwritten in place, so it would cost a dual-write migration to fix nothing historical; treat pre-07-31 screener-derived numbers as void.

Commits here are therefore made **by explicit path, never `-A`**. `.claude/worktrees/` already exists — running concurrent sessions in separate worktrees avoids this class of collision entirely.

- **Second backlog sweep — four more genuinely-open audit items, found by re-reading the audits rather than my own notes (2026-08-01).** User asked again for anything still pending; re-scanning `docs/audit-2026-07-28/FULL_STACK_AUDIT.md` (not just the newer docs) surfaced items neither my task list nor the newer audits' "still open" tables carried. Suites after: **1,006 Python passed / 78 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. All live-verified.
  - **THE find: `insider_buy_pct_90d` was reading the wrong column and had 0.18% coverage.** The 2026-07-30 bias audit diagnosed `insider_trades.date` as TEXT holding NSE's display format (`"05 Apr, 2022"`, 46,194 of 46,198 rows) and **added a parsed `date_iso` column to fix it — but never switched the consumer.** `insider_features.py` still did `WHERE date >= ? AND date <= ?`, a lexicographic string compare of `"05 Apr, 2022"` against `"2026-05-03"`, which matches essentially nothing: the feature was populated on **4 of 2,187 rows**. One-line fix to `date_iso` → **151 symbols** (mean 0.020, sd 0.140; TATASTEEL/RELIANCE/BEL net-buying, 148 net-selling). **Standing lesson: adding a repair column is only half a fix — grep every consumer of the broken column in the same session, or the repair sits there doing nothing while the audit records it as done.**
  - **`recommendation_log`'s "residual NULL entry_price" (audit line 2346) was never a barrier gap — it was the universe gap, in a second writer.** Measured live: 87 symbols with NULL entry_price on 2026-08-01, **all 87 had no `technical_signals` row at all**, only 4 had any price history, and **84 were absent from the `nse_stocks` master** — including `13510368`, the exact raw numeric id the bias audit purged from `unified_recommendations`. `unified_ranker` gained `_restrict_to_tradeable_universe()` then; `scoring_engine._log_recommendations()` never did. Added the mirrored filter (live: drops 86 of 363 candidates) and purged **2,521** unactionable historical rows. Today's coverage **77.8% → 99.0%**. A recommendation for a symbol with no price cannot be entered, stopped, graded or backtested — the row should not exist.
  - **`_trading_days_back` was holiday-blind in TWO files** (audit line 1161). `delivery_volume_fetcher.py` and `fno_rollover_fetcher.py` each hand-rolled "step back, keep weekdays", so `--days N` silently covered fewer than N real sessions. Measured over a 90-session window: **3 wasted fetches on real holidays** (Ambedkar Jayanti, Good Friday, Eid) and 5 calendar days of lost reach — `--days 90` actually covered 87. Both now share `as_of.trading_days_back()`, which reads the exchange's own session list from `stock_ohlcv`/`nse_universe_history`. **Deliberately NOT `market_holidays`**: that table is built from observed gaps and stops at 2026-04-14, so it cannot answer questions about the recent range these callers ask about. Also fixed the same file's None-vs-`[]` contract (404-holiday now returns `[]`, a real failure returns `None`) — collapsing both to `None` made a throttled run indistinguishable from a run of holidays, the bug already fixed once in `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`.
  - **NSE weekend sessions backfilled — but only 3 of the 25 "found", and checking that is what mattered.** The rate limit had lifted, so I re-probed serially (1.2s delay) rather than with the parallel sweep that got me blocked. 25 weekend dates returned HTTP 200 with 2,500+ parseable rows. **They are almost all fakes: the archive RE-SERVES the previous session's file for weekend dates instead of 404ing** — md5 proved Sunday 2024-10-20 byte-identical to Friday 2024-10-18, likewise four *consecutive* Sundays in Oct/Nov (NSE holds Muhurat trading once a year). Only **3 are genuine**: Diwali Muhurat **2023-11-12** and the Budget sessions **2025-02-01** and **2026-02-01** — which independently match the three artifact dates `ohlcv_adjust.py` had flagged. Backfilled those (sessions 1,378 → 1,381). Crucially this is **not** corrupting even without the check, because `parse_bhavcopy` keys every row off the file's own `DATE1` rather than the requested date, so a re-serve just upserts rows that already exist — but `run_one()` would have logged a session on a day the market was shut, so it now detects and reports the re-serve and counts 0. **Had I trusted 200 + plausible row count, I would have inserted 22 days of duplicate Friday data stamped with weekend dates into the very table built to be the point-in-time universe.**
  - **Insider filings now carry `pctTransacted`.** `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py --insider` writes the feed's insider categories to `insider_trades` with % -of-float — the materiality dimension NSE's PIT feed lacks and `insider_buy_pct_90d` (a pure quantity ratio, and near-binary in practice) cannot express. Live: NOVARTIS AG exiting **70.68% of NOVARTIND's float**, against promoter sells of 0.9% and 1.0% that a ratio scores identically. Note `insider_trades.id` is a **BIGINT PK with no default** (NSE rows occupy 9..46k), so unlike `block_deals` a `tt:` string key does not fit — ids are derived deterministically from the low 48 bits of Tickertape's ObjectId plus a 10^15 offset, so a re-run upserts rather than duplicating and the two sources cannot collide.

- **Full-stack production performance pass — frontend/backend/DB (2026-08-02).** User asked for a senior-performance-engineer sweep across all three layers ("optimize for millions of users"). Given this repo's extensive prior perf work (Round 4 audit fixes #33/#34/#37, job destagger, index additions — see memory), ran 3 parallel research-only audits first (frontend rendering, live tRPC request path, DB query/index) rather than re-discovering already-fixed ground, then implemented the findings. All changes `tsc --noEmit` clean, full vitest suite (275 tests) green, `vite build` succeeds. **No live Postgres in this sandbox — the new migration (`migrations/1785700000000_screener-appearances-symbol-and-options-oi-date-index.sql`) has NOT been applied; run `npm run migrate:up` against the real `POSTGRES_URL` before the index-dependent query fixes below see their intended benefit** (same class of gap as `migration_tool_verification_gotcha` memory — declaring/writing a migration is not the same as applying it).
  - **Backend query fixes**: `screeners.router.ts`'s `getScreenerBasket` had regressed the exact correlated-subquery-per-row anti-pattern Finding #33 fixed elsewhere (`WHERE ts.date = (SELECT MAX(date) FROM technical_signals t2 WHERE t2.symbol = ts.symbol)`, O(total historical rows)) — rewritten to the same `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY symbol ...)` pattern already used correctly in `misc.router.ts`. `getStockScreeners` ran 4 independent screener-table lookups sequentially (`await` × 4) instead of `Promise.all`. `commandCenter.router.ts`'s `getUnifiedScoreForSymbol` (likely the single highest-traffic per-symbol endpoint — every stock-detail page load) joined against `SELECT * FROM technical_signals WHERE date = (SELECT MAX(date)...)` — materializing every symbol's full ~300-column row for the latest date just to pick one — rewritten as two parallel indexed point lookups (`WHERE symbol = ?`) instead of a join; `getBuyRecommendations`'s equivalent subquery kept the join (needed, since it returns up to 200 symbols) but now projects only the ~40 columns actually used instead of `SELECT *`. Three "latest close per symbol" sites (`scoring.router.ts` `getStrategyPicks`, `ml.router.ts` `getSignalReportCard`'s `activeSignalGrowth`, `signals.router.ts` `getSignalTracking`) each did `JOIN (SELECT symbol, MAX(date) GROUP BY symbol)` or `WHERE (symbol,date) IN (SELECT symbol, MAX(date) GROUP BY symbol)` — full-table aggregation + re-scan — rewritten to the same `ROW_NUMBER()` pattern (chose this over Postgres's `DISTINCT ON` deliberately: `confluenceEngine.ts`'s own comment already documents `DISTINCT ON` as Postgres-only, not translated for the SQLite dev fallback, so `ROW_NUMBER()` is this codebase's established cross-dialect equivalent).
  - **Caching gap — the largest single finding.** `market.router.ts` (`getGlobalMarketData`/`getMarketOverview`/`getTopMovers`/`getBreakouts`/`getSectorPerformance`), `indices.router.ts` (`getAllIndices`/`getGlobalIndices`/`getIndexStocksList`/`getIndexConstituents`), `moneycontrol.router.ts` (11 of 12 procedures — only `getMcConsolidated` was already wired to `mcApiService.ts`'s internal 1h cache), and `fundamentals.router.ts` (`getRatios`/`getShareholding`/`getCorporateActions`/`getMFInvestments`/`getSmartMoneyFlow`) all called their live MoneyControl/NiftyTrader/ET upstream fetch functions directly on every single request with **no caching at all** — every dashboard poll tick (30-60s) from every open browser tab was a synchronous round-trip to a third-party API, and at real concurrency this both inflates p50 latency to the upstream's latency and risks the upstream rate-limiting/banning the app (exactly the failure mode `cacheService.ts`'s `fetchWithCache` — Redis/in-memory fallback + in-flight de-dup/cache-stampede protection — already exists to prevent). Wrapped all of the above in `fetchWithCache`, TTL chosen per data volatility (30s live indices/overview, 60s movers/breakouts, 900s intraday-ish MC price/vwap, 3600s quarterly-ish fundamentals/ratios/shareholding, 300s smart-money-flow). `ml.router.ts`'s `getModelRocDiagnostics` ran an **unbounded** query over the ever-growing `signal_outcomes` table (237,816+ rows) plus a synchronous Mann-Whitney rank-sort AUC computation **on every call, in the request handler** — added a `days` window param (default 365) and wrapped the whole computation in `fetchWithCache` (300s), since the underlying data only changes on batch-job cadence.
  - **DB index gaps** (new migration, not yet applied — see above): `screener_appearances` had indexes on `appeared_date`/`screener_id` but none on `symbol`, despite `getScreenerAppearanceHistory` accepting a symbol-only lookup — added `idx_scap_symbol(symbol, appeared_date DESC)`. `stock_options_oi` had `(symbol, date DESC)` but `getOptionsIntelligence` does `WHERE date = (SELECT MAX(date)...)` with `date` not the leading column — added `idx_soo_date(date DESC)`. `technical_signals` only had two redundant single-column `symbol` indexes and one on `date`, no composite — added `idx_tsig_symbol_date(symbol, date DESC)` to serve the new point-lookup queries above. **Deliberately did NOT add an index on `confluence_signals`** despite `ml.router.ts`/`confluence.router.ts`/`technicals.router.ts` all wrapping `computed_at` in `date(...)::text` (defeating both a plain index and, more importantly, TimescaleDB's own chunk-exclusion pruning on this compressed hypertable) — CREATE INDEX behavior on compressed Timescale chunks for a non-segmentby column carries real uncertainty this sandbox couldn't live-verify, matching this repo's own precedent of deferring risky operations on compressed hypertables (`ADD CONSTRAINT` on `confluence_signals` was similarly deferred in the 2026-07-23 URL-corruption fix). Fixed only the one site with a clean, low-risk rewrite instead (`ml.router.ts`'s `getSignalReportCard` outer `WHERE` — cast the *literal* side to `::timestamptz` instead of the column, keeping `computed_at` bare/sargable for chunk exclusion); left the other two sites (`confluence.router.ts`'s `DATE(cs.computed_at) = so.signal_date` — DATE compared to a TEXT column, possibly a **live correctness bug**, not just a perf one, unverifiable without a real DB — and `technicals.router.ts`'s `date(cs.computed_at) = ?`) untouched and flagged rather than blind-fixed.

`EODMarketScreener` was also missing the `isVisible`-gated-polling pattern its siblings (`LiveMarketScreener`/`MarketIndices`/`GlobalMarkets`/`Watchlist`) already use, so it polled even when its tab wasn't visible — added the same `document.visibilitychange` gate. `Watchlist.tsx`'s `watchlistStocks` did an unmemoized filter→map with a nested O(n·m) `.find()` per row against `liveQuotes`/`watchlistDetails` on every 10s poll — rebuilt as `useMemo` + `Map` lookups. **Deleted a genuinely dead ~558-line `Dashboard` component** (`App.tsx`, confirmed zero `<Dashboard` JSX references anywhere) along with 8 now-unused eager (non-lazy) imports it alone consumed (`MomentumIntelligence`, `IndexOverview`/`InstitutionalInsights`/`PennyStockIntelligence`, `TopMoversIntelligence`, `MarketIndices`, `GlobalMarkets`, `IntradayBreakouts`) — every real route uses the already-lazy-loaded `DashboardPage`/`V2Dashboard`/`V3Dashboard`/`MarketCommandCenter` instead, so this was pure eager-bundle weight with zero functional benefit.   - **Standing lesson for this session, matching an existing pattern in this file**: writing a SQL comment (`-- ...`) *inside* a JS template-literal SQL string that itself contains a literal backtick character (e.g. `` -- Was `WHERE ...` `` ) silently terminates the outer template literal early — caught immediately by `tsc --noEmit` (`',' expected` at the comment's position), but worth grepping for on any future inline-SQL-comment edit: never put a backtick inside a comment that lives inside a backtick-delimited query string.

Fixed with the exact `::text`-cast-the-DATE-side pattern already established in this codebase's history for this exact table/column (`ml.router.ts`'s `getSignalReportCard`) — a no-op on the SQLite fallback (cast stripped, existing native `DATE()` behavior unchanged), corrects the Postgres path.

Deliberately did NOT add a date bound here (unlike sibling fixes) — `fii_chg_qoq`/`mf_chg_qoq` are quarterly figures that can persist unchanged on a symbol's row for weeks, so narrowing the window risks silently dropping symbols whose latest value sits on an older row, an unverifiable behavior change without a live DB.

Found the actual matching root cause instead: all 13 single-symbol MC procedures in `moneycontrol.router.ts` did `getStockMapping(input.symbol)?.mcsymbol || input.symbol` — and `mapping.mcsymbol` is `""` (empty string, falsy) for a real subset of `stocklist.ts` entries, not just `undefined` for symbols missing from the file. `"" || input.symbol` silently falls through to the **raw NSE ticker**, which is not a valid MoneyControl `scId` (opaque, provider-specific — this codebase's own "Ticker Resolution Strategy" section already documents never guessing a provider ID). Fixed by routing all 13 through `resolveMoneycontrolSymbol()` (already implemented correctly in `stockMapping.ts`, already proven working in `getMcStockNews` per [mc_stock_news_endpoint_2026_07_31] — hardcoded mapping → in-memory cache → MC autocomplete fallback), returning `null` instead of a broken live call when even autocomplete can't resolve an obscure symbol (type-consistent: every `fetchMcXxx` function already returns `Xxx | null`).

Two of the 23 were tested against the real autocomplete endpoint with the query the code actually sends (`query=<raw ticker>`, not the company name): `ASMS` finds no exact match (correctly degrades to `null` — Bartronics may not be indexed under that ticker in MC's autosuggest at all) but **`MUFIN` finds an exact match** (`", MUFIN,"` present in the candidate's `pdt_dis_nm`) → real scId `APMFI54277` → confirmed against the live SWOT endpoint returns real strengths/weaknesses/opportunities/threats data for Mufin Green Finance. Not itself exploitable post-fix (since `"" || scId` correctly falls through to the caller's already-resolved value when `mcsymbol` is empty), but fragile: any future caller that reintroduces the naive `getStockMapping(...)?.mcsymbol || rawSymbol` pattern would have silently poisoned all ~19 of this function's fanned-out `fetchMcXxx` calls (technical, equity-cash, SWOT, essentials, insights, price/volume, analyst rating, earnings/price forecast, consensus, ratios, shareholding, chart patterns, etc. — this is the function every MC-backed procedure ultimately calls). Dead prop, not a live fetch bug — `MCStockInfoPanel` resolves everything itself via `symbol` through the now-fixed tRPC procedures. Left as-is rather than "fixing" working code for a cosmetic dead-prop cleanup.

The fix's own verification only called `compute_insider_features()` directly and confirmed it returns real data in isolation; it never confirmed the *scheduled run's* `UPDATE ... WHERE date=?` actually matched a row in the live table. **Root cause: `ml-daily-ops`'s step chain has grown long enough that it now regularly finishes AFTER MIDNIGHT IST** — confirmed via `job_heartbeat`: a run processing the "2026-07-31" trading day completed at **2026-08-01 01:23 IST**. `insider_features.py` used `datetime.date.today()` as the exact-match write target (`WHERE symbol=? AND date=?`); once the script executes past midnight, `today` resolves to the *next* calendar day, which has no `technical_signals` grid row yet (a trading day's row only exists once created during *that day's own* market-hours scan cycle) — so the UPDATE silently matches **zero rows, every time, no error**. `bse_event_classifier.py`'s `run_daily()` had the identical pattern, deliberately documented as a fix (2026-07-19, replacing a worse `MAX(date)` bug) that itself became wrong once the schedule changed underneath it.
  - **Fix: `as_of.logical_trading_date(cutoff_hour=4, now=None)`** — before 4am local time, "today" is yesterday (the trading day whose EOD processing is still finishing); at/after it, the real calendar date. Deliberately **not** `MAX(date) FROM technical_signals` — that would reopen the exact bug the `date=?` guard was written to prevent (silently writing into an even-older stale row if a script runs before today's grid exists for an unrelated reason, e.g. a manual daytime backfill). A fixed early-morning cutoff fixes the midnight-crossing case without reopening that one.
  - **A follow-up systematic sweep (32-file grep → ~30 candidates with an exact-match `date=?` UPDATE into a daily-grid table) found the SAME defect in 8 more files, all fixed the same way**: `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`, `screener_features_fetcher.py` (2 sites — the largest single engine weight in `unified_ranker`'s regime blend), `credit_rating_fetcher.py`, `mc_earnings_fetcher.py` (4 separate functions), `analyst_revision.py`, `avwap_features.py`, `oi_delta_features.py`, `iv_features.py`. Plus 2 more fixed defensively though **not currently reachable in production** (`relative_strength.py`, `ownership_relative.py` — the vulnerable `--date today` branch exists and is each script's own documented usage pattern, but `queues.ts` currently calls both with no args) — closed anyway since it's a one-line change removing a landmine for whenever someone adds that flag. ~20 files were checked and correctly left alone: some anchor to `MAX(date)` from source data already (`eps_surprise_fetcher.py`, `mc_broker_reco_fetcher.py`, `pead_model.py`), some write per-row `signal_date`/`scan_date` from a dataframe not a wall-clock scalar (`ml_ensemble.py`, `online_learner.py`, `cs_ranker.py`, `movement_predictor.py`, `ml_signal_scorer.py`, `ml_calibration.py`, `breakout_classifier.py`), some are plain `INSERT...ON CONFLICT` upserts which self-heal regardless of which date resolves (`pcr_fetcher.py`, `reward_engine.py`, `nifty_pe_fetcher.py`, `mc_index_oi_fetcher.py`), and some only ever run inside a market-hours-gated window that structurally cannot cross midnight (`preopen_fetcher.py`, `market_regime_fetcher.py`, `intraday_regime.py`). **Standing lesson for any new post-close script: `date.today()`/`datetime.now()` used as an exact-match write target is not safe by inspection alone — check what job chain invokes it and whether that chain's own runtime has grown past the point where "today" can still mean the trading day whose row exists.**
  - **The `date_iso` gap's real root cause, fixed at the source**: `insider_features.py` reads `insider_trades` via `date_iso`, but the table's **dominant, currently-scheduled writer is `moneycontrol_fetcher.py`** (not `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`, which writes a *different* table, `insider_transactions` — easy to confuse, don't) — and it never populated `date_iso` on insert at all, so every row it wrote after the 2026-07-30 one-time backfill was invisible to the read-side fix regardless of the date-anchor bug above. Fixed by reusing `data_integrity_repair.py`'s own `parse_loose_date()` at insert time (not reimplementing it) so both the strptime-success path (MC's `%d-%b-%y` format, already ISO by the time it's inserted) and the raw-fallback path (when MC serves an unexpected format) still get a real `date_iso`. Backfilled the 1,213 rows written since the last repair run (now 46,275/46,275 = 100% coverage) and manually re-ran the fixed pipeline for 2026-07-31 (4 → 160 of 2,187 rows populated).
  - **Three more dormant-but-structurally-live bugs found and fixed, all matching failure classes this codebase has been burned by before**: (1) `unified_ranker.py`'s position-sizing leg (`bet_size_from_probability`, `_get_win_probabilities`) had no `isfinite` guard on `win_probability`/`calibrated_win_probability` — a NaN value doesn't get caught (fails `p <= neutral`, since every NaN comparison is False) and falls through to `min(1.0, nan)`, which Python's first-argument-wins NaN comparison resolves to **1.0, the MAXIMUM bet size** — same failure shape as the 2026-07-31 `dl_score`-into-fake-'Buy' incident, just in the sizing leg instead of the ranking leg. 0 NaN in production today (dormant), fixed defensively. (2) `intraday_ranker.py`'s component blend had the same `is not None`-not-`isfinite` gap; currently benign by accident (`max(0.0, nan)` happens to keep 0.0 under the same first-argument-wins quirk, so it silently zeroes rather than fakes a Buy) but fixed to match the explicit guard used everywhere else. (3) **`regime_detector.py`'s `train_hmm()` had NO promotion gate at all** — genuinely new, never previously audited across 5 prior full-stack passes — unconditionally overwriting `hmm_regime.pkl` (which drives `REGIME_WEIGHTS`, the largest blend-switch in the whole ranker) with no held-out comparison, unlike every sibling ML file in this codebase. Added a champion/challenger gate scoring the trailing 60 days under both the active and freshly-retrained model (`_log_likelihood_per_sample`, with an explicit Jacobian correction — **two GaussianHMMs fit with different StandardScalers are not comparable via raw `model.score()`**, since each scaler's own fitted std shifts the density by a constant the naive comparison would otherwise attribute to fit quality); a regression is written to `hmm_regime.pkl.candidate` instead of overwriting the live model. `--force` skips the gate for a deliberate first train or relabeling.
  - **Added the daily data-integrity cron job requested**: `data-quality-daily` (`10 3 * * *` = 8:40 AM IST, every day — a formal `JOB_REGISTRY`-registered, heartbeat-monitored wrapper around the existing 25-check suite in `dataQualityChecks.ts`). This does **not** replace the pre-existing 15-min `setInterval` poll in `jobWatchdog.ts` (still the real-time alerting path) — it exists because that poll itself was never a monitored cron job, so a silently-dead Node process would take data-quality monitoring down with it and nothing would notice (the exact class of gap `job-digest` had before *it* was registered). Sends a pass/warn/fail Telegram summary every day regardless of outcome, and fails the job itself (visible as "late" in the digest, not just a once-daily message) if any critical check is failing. Live-verified end-to-end: 23/25 pass, 0 critical failures, 2 pre-existing non-critical warns (`insider_trades`/`bulk_deals` recency — sparse data, by design).
  - **Verification discipline**: every production-data claim was checked against the live Postgres DB before and after the fix (not just unit tests) — `date_iso` coverage, `insider_buy_pct_90d` population counts, `job_heartbeat` timestamps converted to IST to prove the midnight-crossing. The 8-file systematic sweep was delegated to a background agent, then independently spot-checked (diffs read directly, NOT-VULNERABLE verdicts re-verified against `queues.ts` invocation sites, full suite re-run independently rather than trusting the agent's own reported pass count) before being accepted — this is the discipline the project's own `live_datasource`-test mandate exists to enforce, applied to agent-produced fixes too.
- **`dl-trainer` (LSTM weekly retrain) was a live incident, not just an under-budgeted timeout — found via a routine weekend-job-status check (2026-08-01/02).** `dl-trainer`'s last recorded failure was a 6h timeout on 2026-07-27, but investigating it surfaced something worse and still active: `dl_model_config.json` pointed at `lstm_version: 19`, and **`lstm_v19.pt` did not exist on disk at all**. Root cause: `dl_trainer.py`'s `retrain_models()` reimplemented its own quality gate instead of calling `dl_engine.py`'s already-correct `_promote_lstm_version()` (built for a different call path — `dl_engine.py --mode train` CLI usage — and never wired into the actual scheduled orchestrator). That gate treated **NaN acc/roc_auc as "insufficient data, skip the check, promote anyway"** — backwards, since `train_lstm()` sets `metrics["error"]` specifically when the weights themselves diverged and correctly refuses to even `torch.save()` the checkpoint (the 2026-07-31 NaN-LSTM fix), but this gate never looked at that key. Net effect: `dl-engine-infer` had been running "successfully" (exit 0) while **silently writing zero predictions every day**, indistinguishable from a healthy day in every job-monitoring signal. Checked every checkpoint's actual weights directly (not metadata): **only v2 and v3 (mid-June) are clean — every version from v4 through v18 is ~100% NaN**, and `model_registry` had **17 of 18 BiLSTM rows simultaneously `is_active=1`** forever, since the old gate never deactivated a prior row either way.
  - **Immediate repair**: rolled `dl_model_config.json` back to v3 (backup of the broken config kept alongside, matching `_promote_lstm_version`'s own timestamped-backup convention) and repaired `model_registry` so only v3 is active. Seeded `dl_model_config.json`'s `lstm_metrics` with v3's real baseline (`roc_auc: 0.58`) so the next real training run has an actual bar to clear instead of auto-promoting via the "no prior baseline" escape hatch.
  - **Fixed the gate bypass**: `dl_trainer.py` now calls `dl._promote_lstm_version()` (the one gate for this model, mirroring `ml_ensemble.py`/`cs_ranker.py`'s promotion pattern) instead of its own logic; the model_registry write was extracted into `_record_model_registry()` (con, version, auc, acc, promoted) — a small, DB-mockable unit mirroring `cs_ranker.py`'s `_register_cs_model()` / `test_cs_ranker_promotion_gate.py` pattern exactly, since it's the identical Finding #17 bug class (unconditional promotion + never-deactivated prior row) in a file that bug class hadn't reached yet.
  - **Fixed a second silent-failure mode found while patching this**: `run_inference()`'s "model file missing" and "every prediction came back non-finite" branches used to `print()` and quietly `return`/`sys.exit(1)`. Both are now a raised `RuntimeError` instead — **`sys.exit` cannot be used here** because `python_api.py`'s `/api/infer-dl` FastAPI endpoint also calls `dl_engine.run_inference()` in-process; `SystemExit` is a `BaseException` the endpoint's `except Exception` does not catch, so `sys.exit(1)` there would have taken down the whole uvicorn worker instead of just failing that one request. A raised exception fails loudly on both call paths correctly (CLI: uncaught → non-zero exit; FastAPI: caught → HTTP 500).
  - **Found and fixed the likely root cause of the divergence itself, then validated it**: no gradient clipping anywhere in `_train_one_fold()`, and no non-finite guard on features loaded from `feature_store` — a ratio-style feature (e.g. `dist_sma200_pct`, `vwap_dist_pct`) computed off a near-zero denominator or one of this codebase's documented bad OHLCV bars (see the 2026-07-30/31 bias-audit and `ohlcv_adjust.py` notes) can be ±inf, and feeding that straight into the LSTM detonates the first matmul and poisons every subsequent batch once the hidden state carries a NaN. Added `np.nan_to_num` sanitization in `load_symbol_sequences()`/`load_inference_sequence()`, a per-batch `torch.isfinite(loss)` skip, and `clip_grad_norm_(max_norm=1.0)` in `_train_one_fold()` — with the AMP-specific gotcha that `scaler.unscale_(opt)` **must** run before `clip_grad_norm_`, or clipping bounds the wrong (still fp16-scaled) magnitude and doesn't actually protect anything. **Live-validated, not just unit-tested**: a real 25-symbol/30-epoch training run (7,448 sequences) that would previously diverge now finishes with **0 of 2,299,209 parameters non-finite**.
  - **The 6h timeout was genuinely too small, and by a lot — measured, not guessed**: that same 25-symbol validation run took **870s (14.5 min)** on this machine's shared 8GB laptop GPU (RTX 5050) under typical contention (Ollama + other jobs — `nvidia-smi` showed 85% utilization, 5.6/8.1GB used, 10 concurrent `python.exe` processes at the time). Extrapolated to the full ~2,100-symbol universe plus `walk_forward_validate()`'s own several-model-copy retraining, a full run plausibly spans hours to the better part of a day depending on contention. **User chose to bump the timeout generously rather than reduce training cost.** `queues.ts`: `dl-retrain-weekly`/`dl-retrain-emergency`'s `lockDuration` and an explicit `processDLPython(...)` timeout override raised 6h→24h (the shared `processDLPython` *default* of 6h is untouched, since `feature_engineering.py`/`dl_engine.py --mode infer`/`backfill_ohlcv.py` all rely on it staying short). `dl_trainer.py`: the stale-lock threshold that lets a second trainer start when the first is presumed dead was raised from 7200s (2h) to 25h (`STALE_LOCK_SECONDS`, named and commented so it's obviously coupled to the BullMQ timeout) — at the old 2h threshold, a real 20-hour run would have looked "stale" partway through and let a second concurrent trainer start, both writing `lstm_v{N}.pt`/`dl_model_config.json` at once.
  - **Tests**: 11 new (`test_dl_trainer_registry_gate.py` — `_record_model_registry`'s promote/reject/scoping behavior, the gate-wiring check via source inspection, and a `STALE_LOCK_SECONDS > 24h` invariant test), all passing alongside the existing 28 DL-related tests. Full suite after all changes: **969 passed, 81 skipped, 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **Still open, deliberately not attempted this session**: the LSTM has not actually been *retrained* yet (only rolled back to the last known-good v3, from mid-June — 6+ weeks stale) — the fix makes the NEXT scheduled/emergency retrain trustworthy, it doesn't itself produce a fresh model. `~2400 historical all-NaN rows in `deep_learning_predictions` (v4-v18 era) were left as-is (harmless now that the ranker/inference path sanitizes non-finite values, per the 2026-07-31 fix).
- **Telegram job-health digest audit — 6 real stale/missing `cronPatterns` mirrors in `monitorScripts.ts`, all producing false Telegram alerts (2026-08-03).** User asked to review the Telegram job-status reports and confirm all jobs are running fine. No live server/DB/Telegram in this sandbox, so this was a source-level cross-check: every `JOB_REGISTRY` (`jobRegistry.ts`) and `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` (`monitorScripts.ts`) cron mirror was diffed against its real schedule in `queues.ts` + the `src/server/jobs/*.jobs.ts` modules the 2026-08 decomposition split it into (a prior session's "every cronPattern mirror updated in lockstep" claim from 2026-07-31 turned out incomplete). Found and fixed 6 real gaps in `monitorScripts.ts`, all previously undetected because `jobPipelineOrdering.test.ts` only checks `JOB_REGISTRY`/source-cron collisions, never `MONITOR_SCRIPTS`'s `cronPatterns` mirrors:
  - `feature-engineering` and `trendlyne-midweek`: both still carried their **pre-2026-07-31-destagger** cron values (`0 10 * * 1-5` / `30 12 * * 2`) after the real jobs (`dl-feature-daily`, `trendlyne-midweek-batch`) moved to `30 11 * * 1-5` / `30 14 * * 2` that same day — the destagger session's own "6 jobs + dl-engine-infer + screener-performance" mirror-update list simply missed these two. `feature-engineering` is `critical: true`, so this was firing a real false "stale" Telegram alert every single weekday between the old and new deadlines.
  - `regime-detector` and `performance-tracker`: both had **no `cronPatterns` at all** despite being driven by Mon-Fri-only jobs (`dl-regime-daily`, and `performance_tracker.py` inside `ml-daily-ops`) — the flat `staleLimitHours: 26` fallback false-flagged 'stale' (both `critical: true`, real alerts) every Saturday/weekend.
  - `technical-scan`: same gap, but with a self-contradicting comment already in place — it explicitly reasons that a flat threshold would "false-alarm every single evening/weekend" and sets `staleLimitHours: 26` to fix it, but the underlying job (`*/30 3-10 * * 1-5`) is Mon-Fri only, so the real Friday→Monday gap is **~64.5h, not ~26h** — this was false-flagging 'stale' for ~38 continuous hours every weekend on one of the platform's most central jobs (2000+ stock technical scan), `critical: true`.
  - `financial-ratios` / `working-capital`: exhaustively computed (not eyeballed) the true worst-case gap between consecutive "first Sunday of month" occurrences across 15 years — **35 days = 840h**, next occurrence 2026-11-01→2026-12-06 — which exceeds the existing `staleLimitHours: 800`. Bumped to 900h. Both `critical: false` (digest-only, no real-time alert), so lower severity than the four above.
  - Also corrected `company-profiles-sync`'s display-only `schedule: 'Bi-weekly Sunday'` label (cosmetic — not used in any lateness computation) — the job is actually daily, all 7 days, sharding the universe by day-of-year (already correctly documented elsewhere in this file's 2026-07-31 job-destagger note; the label here had never been updated to match).
  - **Verification**: fixed values checked against the literal `repeat: { pattern: ... }` lines in `queues.ts`/`jobs/*.jobs.ts` (not against other comments, which is exactly the class of drift this catches), `computeCronLateness()`'s actual semantics in `jobHeartbeat.ts` traced by hand for each case to confirm the false-positive window rather than assumed, `npm install` run fresh in this sandbox to get a working `tsc`/`vitest` (had neither at session start), full suite **251 passed** (only the 28 pre-existing, network-dependent `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures, unrelated — this sandbox's proxy returns 403/timeout against the real MoneyControl API, already documented as a known non-blocker), `tsc --noEmit` clean except the one pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` error already on `main`.
  - **Lesson, extending the already-documented pattern**: this file's own history already tracks the "stale cronPattern mirror" bug recurring across `JOB_REGISTRY` entries and MONITOR_SCRIPTS entries lacking `cronPatterns` — this session shows the fix pattern itself (add a `cronPatterns` mirror) can drift the same way its target did, and that a mirror update done "in lockstep" during a scheduling change can still miss entries, especially ones with self-confident comments (`technical-scan`) that never got re-derived numerically. **Any time a job's cron pattern changes, grep for every OTHER file that might independently encode that same schedule (`JOB_REGISTRY`, `MONITOR_SCRIPTS`, any test asserting ordering) — a comment claiming "matches the convention" is not evidence, recompute the actual gap.** Did not build an automated mirror-consistency test for this in this session (deliberately out of scope — the task was a status review, not a refactor) — worth a follow-up: extend `jobPipelineOrdering.test.ts` (or a sibling) to parse `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.cronPatterns` and assert each pattern string literally appears in the source file its job comes from, the same way it already does for `JOB_REGISTRY.cronPattern`.
- **AI-signal Ollama gate was a near-no-op — degenerate calibration, not a bug (2026-08-02).** User asked whether the `ai-signals` job for 20MICRONS was adding value and to cut unnecessary Ollama usage if not. Job logs said "completed successfully" (it did — persisted `BUY, confidence 41`), but that says nothing about value. Checked the live universe: **2,155 of 2,264 symbols (95.2%) shared the exact same `calibrated_win_probability`, 0.40640821640387675** — just above the `processAISignal` gate floor (`app_settings.ai_signal_min_win_prob`, default 0.40) — so the gate was passing 2,169/2,264 stocks (95.8%) daily, i.e. calling Ollama against essentially the whole universe rather than a quant-endorsed shortlist. 20MICRONS itself: raw `win_probability` ranged 0.18–0.71 over 5 days but calibrated to the identical 0.4064 on 4 of them. **Root cause is not a calibration bug**: `ml_calibration.py`'s own docstring already documents the base ensemble's `win_probability` is non-monotonic outside BEAR regime (0.65–0.85 raw predictions win LESS than lower ones) — consistent with the already-known live-AUC≈0.50-outside-BEAR finding from the 2026-07-23 `regime_edge_status` work. Isotonic regression is correctly pooling a genuinely non-monotonic range into one flat value; it's exposing the base model's lack of real differentiation for ~95% of the universe on a given day, not causing it — retraining/refitting the calibrator would not fix this. **Measured consequence**: AI-sourced `unified_signals` 30-day decisive win rate is **~2.0%** (134 WIN vs 6,525 STOP_LOSS + 66 LOSS of 6,725 decisive outcomes) — the same dead level as pre-[[llm-demotion-and-retrain-2026-07-12]] (2.3%); the July 12 demotion fixed *what* gets written but not the gate's near-total lack of selectivity. **Fix (user-approved)**: raised `app_settings.ai_signal_min_win_prob` 0.40 → 0.42 via a direct row upsert — single consumer (`queues.ts:335`, 5-min cache TTL), no code/restart needed — clearing the 0.4064 plateau with margin while keeping the gate open for the 14/2,264 (0.6%) stocks/day with genuinely differentiated calibrated scores. Live-verified projection: ~2,169/day candidates → ~14/day, a ~99% cut in unnecessary Ollama calls. **Deliberately not done**: didn't touch `ml_calibration.py`, didn't disable the AI-signal pipeline outright (the residual 0.6% + their `ai_reasoning` narrative may still carry value), didn't wire the existing (flag-gated-off) `edge_adjusted_probability()` regime-shrinkage mechanism into this specific gate (it already covers `ml_ensemble`/`scoring_engine`/`unified_ranker` sizing but never this Ollama gate — worth a follow-up). **Lesson**: a job's "completed successfully" log line is not evidence the signal it wrote had value — check gate selectivity (fraction of universe actually filtered) and resolved win rate before trusting job-success as a usefulness proxy; a gate reading a *calibrated* column can silently degrade to a near-no-op when the underlying model's differentiation collapses in a regime, and the calibration behaving correctly is exactly what hides this from a naive check.
- **Weekend job-status review (2026-08-03): 3 real fixes, 1 initially-flagged "critical gap" that turned out to be a measurement artifact — worth recording so it isn't re-flagged.** User asked to confirm all weekend jobs (Sat Aug 1 / Sun Aug 2) completed successfully. Cross-checked `getLateJobs()` (JOB_REGISTRY, cron-aware) AND `getSystemStatus()` (MONITOR_SCRIPTS, DB-freshness-aware) against ground-truth queries on the live tables -- the two checkers disagree with each other by design (different registries), so neither alone is sufficient; both had real gaps.
  - **`nse-bhavcopy-fetcher` (critical) had zero heartbeat instrumentation** -- `queues.ts` called it via bare `runPython(...).catch(console.warn)` instead of `T.run('nse-bhavcopy-fetcher', ...)` like every sibling step in the same `ml-daily-ops` chain, so `job_heartbeat` never recorded a single run despite the fetcher genuinely working (`nse_universe_history` was correctly fresh through 2026-07-31). Fixed by wrapping it in `T.run(...)` to match the established pattern.
  - **`regime-detector` (critical) false-alarmed "stale" every weekend** -- its `monitorScripts.ts` entry was missing `cronPatterns` (unlike its sibling `dl-engine-infer`, registered the same way via `dl.jobs.ts`), so it fell back to the flat 26h `staleLimitHours` check, which trips every Sat/Sun regardless of Friday's actual success. `market_regimes` was correctly fresh through Jul 31 (HIGH_VOL) the whole time. Added `cronPatterns: ['15 11 * * 1-5']` matching `registerDlJobs()`'s real registration.
  - **Initially flagged, then corrected: the 15-min intraday chain (market-regime-refresh -> intraday-breadth-capture -> intraday-ranker) looked like it had a critical live gap** -- `intraday_recommendations` showed only 2 executions all Friday instead of ~25, and `intraday-breadth-capture`'s new write path had never once recorded a heartbeat since being wired in. **This is not a live bug**: `git show` on the commit that added the new breadth-capture step (`d8e8c5d`) timestamps it **2026-07-31 17:35:18 IST -- after that day's 15:30 IST market close**. The code correctly has zero data since deploy because zero trading-hours minutes have elapsed since then (Sat/Sun are non-trading); the next scheduled fire was confirmed live in Redis (`bull:market-regime-refresh:repeat`) for Monday 08:30 IST, right on schedule. **Lesson: before treating "zero activity since visible in the data" as a bug, check when the instrumenting commit actually landed relative to the market session -- a fix deployed after close on a Friday will correctly show nothing until the next session, and checking again pre-market-open on the very next trading day will look identical to a real outage.**
  - **Real, currently-active bug: `exit_policy.py --train` timed out on its 20-min budget on all 3 attempts over the weekend** (Sat 13:59, Sat 19:16, and Mon 07:31 IST -- the exit-policy MFE/MAE regressor has not retrained successfully this cycle). Traced via BullMQ/Redis job history (`bull:ml-weekly-retrain:completed`) to **two overlapping `ml-weekly-retrain` catchup runs from two separate server restarts ~30 min apart**, both queued because `addJobWithCatchup()`'s "was this missed?" check only looks at `getJobs(['completed','failed'])` -- a still-running catchup from an earlier restart is invisible to a later restart's check, so both concluded "missed" and each queued its own full retrain, doubling Python-subprocess contention. Fixed generically in `src/server/jobs/registerJob.ts`: before queuing a catchup, check `getJobs(['active','waiting','delayed'])` for an existing job with the same name and skip if found. This guard applies to every queue using this shared helper, not just ml-weekly-retrain. 3 new tests in `src/server/__tests__/registerJob.test.ts` (missed+nothing-in-flight -> catchup queued; missed+already-in-flight -> skipped; not-missed -> no catchup logic touched).
  - **Also confirmed working as designed, no action needed**: `drift_detector.py`'s EMERGENCY_RETRAIN signal (Sat 01:10 IST, severe drift from the market's BEAR/HIGH_VOL whiplash that week) correctly auto-queued and completed `dl-retrain-emergency`; the LSTM is confirmed on the clean v3 checkpoint (`is_active=1`, no new NaN predictions since the 2026-08-02 gate fix -- the Aug 1 NaN error in logs predates that fix); one transient "Postgres unavailable at startup" during a concurrent session's restart recovered on the next attempt.

- **`url_explorer` (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-23-url-data-exploration.md) built out to completion (2026-08-03).** Only Task 1 (`normalizer.py`) existed; user asked to build the remaining 6 tasks exactly as the plan specified. Added `store.py` (Postgres/SQLite schema via `db_compat`: `url_endpoints`/`url_params`/`url_fetches`/`url_fields`/`url_field_correlations`), `fetcher.py` (injectable throttled fetch with a consecutive-failure circuit breaker), `profiler.py` (JSON flattening → per-field coverage/dtype/NSE-universe-overlap), `correlator.py` (ticker-keyed numeric fields vs. return targets via `scipy.stats`), `returns.py` (trailing-return targets from `stock_ohlcv`; forward-return targets deliberately deferred — see the module's own docstring), `report.py` (markdown usefulness report), and `explore.py` (CLI orchestrator, `python -m url_explorer.explore --urls urls.txt`). This is a standalone, manually-invoked research tool (not wired into `queues.ts`/`ml-daily-ops`) for cataloging a batch of captured third-party URLs (same category of exercise as the 2026-07-31 `verify_live_urls.py`/`probe_endpoint_payloads.py` endpoint-corpus review) — point it at a `urls.txt`, it collapses near-duplicate URLs into endpoint templates, fetches once per template, profiles the JSON shape, and correlates ticker-keyed numeric fields against trailing returns. 26 tests pass (full suite unchanged at 1,103 passed / 81 deselected `live_datasource`). Implemented verbatim from the plan's own worked-out code — no design deviation, so no new gotchas to record beyond what the plan itself already documents (forward-return IC needs a later snapshot-diffing enhancement, deliberately not built in v1).
- **Data-integrity report cleanup + the trendlyne-midweek gap closed (2026-08-03), continuation of the same-day weekend job-status review above.** User pasted the actual `data-quality-daily` Telegram digest (5 warnings on a Monday morning: OHLCV/fii_dii_flow/market_regimes freshness "3+ days old", insider_trades "276.4d old", bulk_deals "76.1d old") and asked to resolve all of it, plus fix the pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` typecheck error. All 5 warnings investigated against live data — 3 were the exact same weekend-blind-threshold false-positive class documented repeatedly elsewhere in this file, and 2 were genuine bugs this check happened to be the first thing to surface.
  - **OHLCV/fii_dii_flow/market_regimes "3+ days stale" were false alarms**: `data-quality-daily` runs at 08:40 IST, hours before that day's own stock-refresh (16:00)/fii-dii-fetcher (19:30, inside ml-daily-ops)/regime-detector (16:45) jobs fire — so on any Monday, the most recent row is still Friday's, a real ~1.1-day trading-day lag inflated to ~3.1-3.4 raw calendar days by the Sat/Sun gap. `dataQualityChecks.ts`'s own header explicitly declines an NSE-holiday-calendar dependency "for simplicity," but a plain Sat/Sun subtraction is cheap and removes the whole false-positive class. Added `tradingDaysStale()` (subtracts weekend calendar days between the row's date and now, floored at 0) and wired it into `ohlcv-freshness-coverage`/`fii-dii-flow-freshness`/`market-regimes-freshness`. A genuine multi-weekday outage still fails/warns undiminished (verified with a "one week later" test case).
  - **`insider-trades-recency` was reading the wrong column — a real bug, not a false alarm.** `insider_trades.date` is NSE's raw display text ("22 May, 2026"), not lexically sortable; `MAX(date)` returned `"31 Oct, 2025"` (alphabetically late month name) while the real latest row was `2026-07-31` — a 276-day false reading. `date_iso` (added in the 2026-08-01 midnight-crossing session, now at 100% coverage and populated at insert time) is the actually-sortable column. Fixed the check's SQL to `MAX(date_iso)`.
  - **`bulk-deals-recency` was monitoring a table nothing has written to in 2.5 months — also a real, separate bug.** Git-blamed `bulk_deals`'s last real row (2026-05-19) to a feature (`fbeac6f`/`7e61549`, "add SmartMoneyFlow...") merged 2026-05-19 and reverted 2026-05-21 (`424b731`) — the "76.1d old" reading was 100% correct, it just described a table nobody uses anymore. The live equivalent is `block_deals`, fed daily by `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py` (wired into ml-daily-ops since 2026-07-31, carries `pctTransacted`) and additively shared with `block_deal_fetcher.py`'s NSE rows. Repointed the check's SQL at `block_deals` (id kept stable; label/detail wording updated). Left the dead `bulk_deals` table itself alone (its `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` in `db.ts`/`moneycontrol_fetcher.py` is harmless dead weight, not a bug) — dropping a live-DB table is a bigger, separate decision than fixing a monitoring check, flagged rather than done unprompted.
  - **Closed the `trendlyne_price_analysis_fetcher.py` gap flagged-but-not-fixed in the earlier session note today.** The app log for 2026-07-28 showed the real root cause: the script ran to completion ("execution completed") but **every single one of 1822 stocks logged "no data"** — a 100% silent failure with no error anywhere, while its sibling `trendlyne_adv_tech_fetcher.py` (run immediately before it in the same `trendlyne-midweek` batch) succeeded fine the same day. Live-probed the endpoint directly today and it returns full, correct data (`head.status="0"`) — the incident was transient (most likely a temporary Trendlyne anti-bot block against the plain, unauthenticated `requests.Session()` this fetcher uses) and had already self-resolved, so it couldn't be reproduced. Rather than chase an unreproducible one-off, hardened both fetchers with the resilience machinery this codebase already built for exactly this class of problem (`fetch_utils.py`'s `retry_get`/`FetchTracker`, rolled out to 15 other fetchers 2026-07-30 but missed by these two): `_fetch()`/`fetch_adv_tech()` now retry through `retry_get` instead of a single-attempt `session.get()`, and `main()` in both files now tracks every symbol through a `FetchTracker` that exits non-zero once the failure rate crosses 15% — so a repeat of "100% no data" fails the job loudly (visible in `job_heartbeat`/error logs) instead of logging a misleadingly clean "execution completed" with zero rows written.
  - **Found and fixed a second, independent bug while investigating: `monitorScripts.ts`'s `trendlyne-midweek` `cronPatterns` was stale.** It still said `'30 12 * * 2'` (6pm IST) but `trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts`'s actual registration moved to `'30 14 * * 2'` (8pm IST) on 2026-07-31 specifically to stop colliding with `et-marketstats-sync`'s relocation into the 6:00-6:40pm IST block — exactly the "stale cronPattern mirror produces phantom alerts" class this same file's header comment warns about elsewhere. Corrected the mirror.
  - **`MCStockInfoPanel.tsx`'s pre-existing TS2322**: `Object.entries(pv?.price || {})`'s `val` is `unknown` (loosely-typed MC API response) and was interpolated directly into JSX (`{val}%`); wrapped in `String(val)` — the adjacent `Number(val)` comparison already proved it was numeric-ish, this just makes the render side type-safe too.
  - **Verification**: 7 new Python tests (`test_trendlyne_midweek_resilience.py` — retry-wrapped success/failure parsing for both fetchers, plus source-inspection checks that `FetchTracker`/`.finish()`/`.record()` are actually wired into each `main()`, mirroring the dl_trainer promotion-gate wiring-check pattern) + 10 new TS tests in `dataQualityChecks.test.ts` (weekend-adjustment math, the exact Monday-morning regression for all 3 checks, a "still fails a real week-long outage" guard, and SQL-string assertions that insider-trades/bulk-deals point at the corrected columns/tables). Full Python suite **994 passed, 81 deselected** (`live_datasource`, unaffected); full TS suite **337 test files / 2144 tests passed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean (zero errors, including the now-fixed `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx`). `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy the TS-side fixes (the two Python fetchers need no restart — `runPython` re-reads `.py` from disk on every subprocess spawn).
- **Data-quality coverage expanded from ~25 to 62 checks + a standing mandate added (2026-08-03), same-day continuation.** User asked to "add all live datasources" to `dataQualityChecks.ts` and make it mandatory that any future datasource gets a check too. Swept every `runPython()` call site across `queues.ts` + `jobs/*.jobs.ts` (~120 distinct scripts), cross-referenced each script's own `INSERT INTO`/`CREATE TABLE` targets against the file's existing coverage, and found the ~25 hand-written checks covered a small fraction of the platform's external-data landing tables — most fetchers had zero freshness monitoring at all.
  - **Built a declarative factory instead of hand-writing ~35 more bespoke checks**: `TABLE_FRESHNESS_CHECKS` (a `{id, label, category, critical, table, dateColumn, nativeDateColumn?, tradingDayAware?, warnDays, failDays?}` config array) + `makeFreshnessCheck()`, which generates the SQL + `evaluate()` for each entry. This directly serves the "make it a mandate" half of the ask — adding a check for a new datasource is now a one-line config object, not a SQL+evaluate() block to author and get right every time. `tradingDayAware` defaults to true (uses the same-day's `tradingDaysStale()` fix); explicitly set `false` for genuinely 24/7-cadence tables. Omitting `failDays` reproduces the existing "sparse by nature" soft-warn-only style (`insider-trades-recency`/`bulk-deals-recency`) for naturally episodic data.
  - **Added 37 new checks** across every genuinely distinct external-data table found not yet covered: `nse_universe_history` (survivorship-critical, critical:true), options (`so_option_chain`, `index_option_oi`, `nt_index_pcr_ts`, `stock_option_features`), flows (`insider_transactions` — distinct from `insider_trades`, `bulk_block_deals`, `stock_delivery_volume`, `mf_stock_holdings`, `mf_sector_allocation`), fundamentals (`tl_financial_quality`, `trendlyne_dvm_scores`, `proprietary_scores_history`, `working_capital_history`, `stock_earnings_beats`, `eps_surprise_history`), macro (`macro_asset_prices` — critical:true, fed by ~8 different fetchers, `eco_calendar`, `index_valuation`, `mc_global_snapshot`, `sector_global_corr`, `historical_fno_sentiment`, `sector_fo_sentiment`, `nse_ipo_calendar`), scoring/signals (`confluence_signals` — critical:true, canonical confluence engine, `unified_signals`, `screener_appearances` — critical:true since it feeds `screener_momentum_score`, the largest single ranker weight per the 2026-07-30 bias audit, `screener_sector_rotation`, `intraday_recommendations`), and reference (`mc_broker_reco`, `mc_chart_patterns`, `market_breadth`, `preopen_snapshot`, `nt_fno_dashboard`, `trendlyne_fno_activity`, `mc_pricefeed_daily`, `extra_endpoint_responses`). Deliberately excluded pure-internal/derived tables (model registries, RL Q-tables, weight-history bookkeeping, backtest run logs, DL prediction output) — those are ML state, not datasources, and diluting the file's purpose with them wasn't the ask.
  - **The sweep immediately found 2 real, previously-invisible problems** — exactly what adding coverage is for: `mf_sector_allocation` (`mf_sector_flow_fetcher.py`'s own declared write target) is **completely empty, 0 rows**, despite the fetcher running; and `historical_fno_sentiment` (fed by `pcr_fetcher.py --gex`) is **11.4 days stale**. Neither was fixed in this pass — surfacing them via the new checks (now visible in every future daily digest) was the actual deliverable; root-causing either is separate follow-up work. `insider_transactions` (NSE PIT filings, distinct from `insider_trades`) also reads 66.4d stale but correctly soft-warns rather than failing, matching the "real transactions can legitimately be this sparse" uncertainty already documented for `insider_trades`/`bulk_deals`.
  - **Live-verified via `npm run dq:check`**: 59/62 pass, 0 critical failures, the 2 genuine gaps above correctly surfaced (one warn, one non-critical fail) rather than silently passing.
  - **Mandate added in two places**: a new "General Rules" bullet (the enforcement text) and a cross-reference added to the existing "Adding a New Data Source" section so both requirements — the `live_datasource` test AND the data-quality check — are visible together wherever a future session goes looking for "how do I add a fetcher correctly."
  - **Tests**: 6 new (registry floor-count guard against a future refactor silently dropping the generated entries, factory wiring spot-checks, and behavior tests for the empty/sparse/trading-day/24-7/native-DATE-cast code paths — including a negative check that the 24/7 `confluence_signals` check does NOT get weekend-adjusted). Full TS suite **337 files / 2153 tests passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy (Python fetchers untouched this pass, no restart needed for them).

  - **6 new market-wide entries added to `endpoint_registry.py`'s `CURATED_EXTRA_ENDPOINTS`** (28 → 34), all live-verified with plain `requests` (no TLS impersonation needed for these hosts, unlike MoneyControl/ndtvprofit): `tickertape_mmi` (Market Mood Index — confirmed via grep absent from this codebase/frontend before this addition), `mc_deals_insight_top_investor` (richer counterparty/sector/value detail than `block_deal_fetcher.py`'s NSE pull), `investsights_investors_list` (superstar-investor directory snapshot, for a future leaderboard UI), `investsights_concall_recent` (AI-generated earnings-call tone/takeaway — matches this codebase's own stated "LLM only for unstructured text, as a bounded component score" philosophy from the 2026-07-30/31 quant-strategy audit), `investsights_sector_rrg` + `investsights_sector_correlation` (a real Relative Rotation Graph and sector correlation matrix — the "fuller fix" `unified_ranker.py`'s first-order `MAX_SECTOR_EXPOSURE` cap, added 2026-07-30, was explicitly flagged as needing). Writes `(symbol, investor_slug, change_type, curr_pct_holding, pct_holding_change, period_end_date)` into a new `superstar_investor_activity` table. **Real endpoint quirk caught live**: the `symbol` field on both `/investors/{slug}` and its `/activity` sub-path is sometimes a raw BSE numeric scrip code instead of an NSE ticker (confirmed: `"517238"` for Dynavision Ltd sitting alongside a normal ticker `"APOLSINHOT"` in the same investor's holdings) — no BSE-code resolver exists in this codebase for this provider, and building one blind would repeat the exact URL/ID-as-symbol mistake this project has already been burned by twice (2026-07-23 Trendlyne corruption, the still-unresolved raw-numeric-symbol artifact in `nse_stocks`). Fixed by only keeping rows whose symbol matches an existing `nse_stocks` ticker — BSE-only holdings are silently dropped, not corrupted, an accepted coverage tradeoff. Wired into `queues.ts` right after `extra_endpoints_fetcher.py` (plain `runPython(...).catch(...)`, matching that whole block's existing style — no `T.run()`/StepTracker wrapping there). Live-verified end-to-end against the real DB: 15-investor sample → 374 stored / 434 skipped (expected, given the BSE-code mixing).
  - **`url_explorer`'s own `store.py` had a real bug, found by actually running it**: its DDL used bare SQLite `AUTOINCREMENT`, which is invalid Postgres syntax (`db_compat.translate()` rewrites query syntax, not DDL type syntax) — crashed the first live fetch/profile pass with `psycopg2.errors.SyntaxError`. Fixed by branching on `sql_translate.use_postgres()`, mirroring `moneycontrol_fetcher.py`'s `ensure_tables_exist()` — same "SQLite-schema-of-record assumed dialect-portable without ever running it against Postgres" lesson this file has recorded several times before (the `date('now')` incidents).
  - **Test coverage**: `test_live_datasource_round4_endpoints.py` (6 tests, the new registry entries) + `test_live_datasource_investsights_investor_activity.py` (4 tests, the new standalone fetcher, incl. a test pinning the BSE-code-must-be-dropped behavior) — both mandatory per "Adding a New Data Source." Two pre-existing coverage-guard tests elsewhere in the suite (`test_endpoint_registry.py`'s hardcoded market-endpoint count, `test_live_datasource_investsights_tapetide.py`'s provider-based sweep, which had unknowingly absorbed the 4 new InvestSights entries into its own scope) needed updating — exactly the kind of drift those guards exist to catch, caught immediately by running the full suite rather than just the new tests. `AI_ENDPOINT_MEMORY.md` updated with a new "Round 4" section (matching its own established per-round convention) and its status-table counts (29→35 live-fetchable, plus the new standalone fetcher noted as uncounted since it doesn't fit the registry's scope model).

- **Fixed the 2 gaps the data-quality sweep found (2026-08-03), same-day continuation.** User asked to fix `mf_sector_allocation` (empty) and `historical_fno_sentiment` (11.4d stale) from the previous message's new checks. One was a genuine internal scheduling bug (fixed); the other is a third-party endpoint that appears restructured (flagged to the user rather than guessed at, per standing feedback).
  - **`historical_fno_sentiment` — fixed. Root cause: every single scheduled call to `pcr_fetcher.py` across the whole codebase passed `--gex`** (grepped all 5 call sites in `queues.ts` to confirm) — the index-level dealer-GEX path (`run_nifty_gex()` → `save_nifty_gex()`, writes only `macro_asset_prices`). The *default* per-symbol path (`run()` → `save()`) is the ONLY thing that writes `historical_fno_sentiment` (and refreshes `stock_options_oi`'s ATM IV for the `DEFAULT_SYMBOLS` 20 large-caps) — nothing scheduled it anymore. A stale code comment at the original call site ("iv_features reads the ATM IV that pcr_fetcher just wrote to stock_options_oi") was the tell: it described behavior from before the call was narrowed to `--gex`-only, and nobody had corrected it since. The gap stayed invisible because `stock_options_oi` itself kept looking fresh — it's independently fed by `so_option_chain_fetcher.py`/`stock_option_chain_fetcher.py`, so only `historical_fno_sentiment` (with no other writer) was actually broken. Fixed by adding back a daily `runPython('pcr_fetcher.py', [], 90_000)` call (no flags) right before `iv_features.py` in the `ml-daily-ops` chain — cheap (20 symbols, ~30s at NiftyTrader's own rate limit). Live-verified: ran it directly, 20/20 symbols succeeded, `historical_fno_sentiment` now carries today's date.
  - **`mf_sector_allocation` — NOT fixed; the upstream endpoint appears to have changed, and per an existing memory entry from 2026-07-04 (feedback_failing_urls) the user wants to be asked for an alternative rather than have me research/guess a replacement myself.** Probed `mf_sector_flow_fetcher.py`'s AMFI URL (`portal.amfiindia.com/DownloadSchemeData_Po.aspx?mf=0&tp=1&fD=...&tD=...`) directly: it now returns the scheme MASTER list (AMC/scheme name/ISIN/launch date) instead of the monthly portfolio-holdings disclosure (per-stock market value / % to NAV) this parser expects — no "Total AUM" header lines, no market-value/%-to-NAV columns at all, so `_parse_amfi()` correctly finds zero rows on every run, every day, for at least the last 8+ days of production logs checked. Varying `tp` (0/2/3/4) returned byte-identical responses, and AMFI's human-facing portfolio-disclosure page 404s — the site's structure has likely changed since this fetcher was written. **What WAS fixed regardless of the endpoint question**: `run()` used to `print()` and plain `return` (exit 0) when `holdings.empty`, so this has been a silently-empty "success" on every run for weeks — same invisible-failure class this whole session's data-quality sweep exists to catch. Changed to `sys.exit(1)` so it now fails loudly (visible in `job_heartbeat`/error logs) until either the URL is fixed or the fetcher is retired.
