Full suites: Python **997 passed**, TS **338 files / 2155 tests passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. `npm run dq:check` re-run: **60/62 pass** (up from 59/62 — `historical_fno_sentiment` now passes; `mf_sector_allocation` still correctly warns, now for the right reason — a loud, visible failure instead of a silent empty success).
- **Follow-up on the 2026-08-03 job-health audit: three new mirror-consistency tests built + one more real drift found, closing the "worth a follow-up" item from that session's own note.** User asked for `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.cronPatterns` mirror-consistency to be automated, then `JOB_REGISTRY.cronPattern`/`everyMs` too, then `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.staleLimitHours` too.
  - `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsCronMirror.test.ts` (29 tests): every `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.cronPatterns` string must exist as a real `repeat: { pattern: ... }` registration somewhere in `queues.ts`/`src/server/jobs/*.jobs.ts` (general sweep), plus pinned regressions tying `feature-engineering`/`trendlyne-midweek`/`regime-detector`/`dl-engine-infer`/`screener-performance`/`outcome-resolver-5d`/`-15d` to their specific driving job by name.
  - `src/server/__tests__/jobRegistryCronMirror.test.ts` (113 tests): same audit applied to `JOB_REGISTRY.cronPattern`/`everyMs` — all 52 scheduled entries pinned to their driving job (including the 15 `ml-daily-ops`/2 `ml-weekly-retrain` StepTracker sub-steps, correctly pinned to their shared parent's schedule since they have none of their own), with a meta-test failing loud if a newly-added job has no pin. **Getting the marker-matching right took two real iterations, both found empirically against this actual codebase, not hypothetically**: (1) a window search centered on the marker (checking both directions) misattributed `mc-screener-sync`'s/`dl-feature-refresh`'s pattern to their *preceding* neighbor when that neighbor's own comment block was long enough to push the window backward across the boundary; (2) several job-id strings (`ml-daily-ops`, `intraday-fetcher`, `live-screener-collect`, ...) appear multiple times in source — once as an `export const QUEUE_X` constant, once at the real registration, again in later `.on('completed', ...)`/`recordHeartbeat(...)` calls — so a naive first-occurrence lookup can land on the wrong one. Fixed by checking every occurrence of the marker, searching only forward from each (verified true at every real call site), and taking the globally nearest (occurrence, repeat) pairing across all of them.
  - `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsStaleLimitConsistency.test.ts` (14 tests): extends the audit to `staleLimitHours` itself — monitor.router.ts's `getSystemStatus()` FALLBACK lateness check, used only when an entry has no `cronPatterns` (the rest are already covered by the cron-mirror test above). For each no-`cronPatterns` entry, locates its real driving job by the same marker technique and computes the job's true worst-case gap — via `cron-parser` for a literal cron cadence, or via an explicit first-Sunday-of-month calendar model for `working-capital`, whose real cadence (`trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts`'s `isFirstSundayOfMonth` gate) is a conditional sub-cadence of a weekly cron, not expressible as a raw cron pattern — then asserts `staleLimitHours` clears it. **Found a real, live drift while building it**: `financial-ratios` still carried its pre-2026-07-31 `schedule: 'First Sunday of month'` label and `staleLimitHours: 900` after `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` was moved out of the first-Sunday gate that same day (`processTrendlyneRatiosMonthly` now runs it unconditionally every Sunday, only `working_capital_fetcher.py`/`mf_stock_holdings_fetcher.py` remain gated below it) — not a false-alarm risk (900h > the real 168h worst case), but a genuine staleness-detection blind spot: a real break in that fetcher wouldn't have been flagged for up to 900h instead of ~200h. Corrected to `schedule: 'Weekly Sunday'` / `staleLimitHours: 200`, matching its `ml-weekly-retrain`/`trendlyne-fundamentals` siblings' convention; `working-capital` itself is genuinely still monthly-gated and keeps its correct, now cross-verified 900h.
  - **Verification discipline held throughout**: every test negative-controlled by deliberately corrupting real entries (`et-marketstats-sync`/`dl-feature-refresh` cron values; `ohlcv-backfill`'s `staleLimitHours` below its real 168h worst case; `working-capital`'s below its real 840h worst case, specifically to prove the monthly-gate model is load-bearing and not silently equivalent to a plain-weekly read of the same cron) and confirming the exact expected failures before restoring. Full suite green (395+ passed across all three new files combined) except the pre-existing, unrelated `mcapiProxy.test.ts` network failures and a pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` typecheck error already on `main`.
- **Full-suite Postgres flakiness (the follow-up flagged above) root-caused and fixed same-day, 2026-08-03.** Not actually flaky — 100% deterministic given the ambient environment, and NOT caused by the mirror-consistency test files (confirmed by reproducing identically with those files removed). Root cause: `dbAsync.ts`'s `usePostgres()` reads `process.env.USE_POSTGRES` fresh on every `dbGet`/`dbAll`/`dbRun` call (`=== 'true'`, strict), and this sandbox has `USE_POSTGRES=true` baked into the process environment itself — confirmed via a fresh `bash -c` subshell (no `.env` file, no shell rc line; it's a real container/harness-level env var, not something a test could see coming from a dotfile). 10 test files (`backtestRunner`, `commandCenter`, `createSignal`, `getFiiDiiFlow`, `getRecentCloseSeries`, `getUnifiedScoreForSymbol`, `scoringService`, `signalReaders`, `unifiedSignalsKey`, `upsertUnifiedSignal`) exercise the real tRPC router (or call `dbRun`/`dbGet`/`dbAll` directly) against a real in-memory/file-based SQLite `db` they set up themselves, but never isolated `USE_POSTGRES` — so every DB call silently routed to a live Postgres pool that doesn't exist in this sandbox, failing on `ECONNREFUSED` regardless of what was actually being tested. **Confirmed empirically before touching any code**: `USE_POSTGRES=false npx vitest run <file>` made the failures disappear pre-fix; a full run under that override matched the known-clean baseline exactly (28 failures, all in `mcapiProxy.test.ts`, unrelated real-network calls). Fixed by adding `process.env.USE_POSTGRES = 'false';` to each file, ahead of any (even transitive) import of `dbAsync.ts` — 6 files already used a dynamic `await import('../db')` specifically to guarantee env vars land first (same pattern now reused, env line added alongside their existing `DATABASE_URL = ':memory:'`); the other 4 used a **static** `import { dbRun } from '../dbAsync'`, where a plain top-level env-var statement placed before it is NOT guaranteed to run first (static imports are hoisted ahead of a module's own top-level code) — converted to the same dynamic-import pattern rather than relying on an ordering guarantee that might not hold under a different bundler/transform config. **Verified, not assumed**: ran the full suite 3 times under the *unmodified* ambient environment (`USE_POSTGRES=true` still set at the shell level, no override) — consistently 407/435 passed, identical every time, only the pre-existing `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures remain. `tsc --noEmit` clean except the one pre-existing, unrelated `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` error already on `main`. **Lesson**: a test file that sets up its own isolated fixture (in-memory SQLite) but drives it through a real, shared routing layer (`dbAsync.ts`) is only as isolated as every env var that routing layer reads — `DATABASE_URL` alone was never sufficient, since `USE_POSTGRES` is a completely separate switch read by a different module (`pgConfig.ts`) with no connection to `DATABASE_URL` at all; a test that isolates one env var a shared facade reads must audit *every* env var that facade reads, not just the one it happens to remember exists.
- **Fourth mirror-consistency test, `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes` (2026-08-03, same audit series).** `jobHeartbeat.ts`'s `getLateJobs()` waits `graceMinutes` past a cron's expected fire time before flagging a job 'late' — if `graceMinutes` is smaller than the job's own declared runtime (BullMQ `lockDuration`, or an explicit `withJobTimeout(...)` wrapper), the job gets flagged late while still running completely normally. `src/server/__tests__/jobRegistryGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` (59 tests) locates each entry's real driving job via the same marker-matching technique as `jobRegistryCronMirror.test.ts` and asserts `graceMinutes` covers it; the 17 `ml-daily-ops`/`ml-weekly-retrain` StepTracker sub-steps are checked against their parent's own (live-read) `graceMinutes` instead, per the already-documented "wider than the parent's grace" convention.
  - **Found real, live drift, three of it large.** `mc-screener-sync`: grace 60min vs a declared 90min lockDuration. `ml-weekly-retrain`: grace 180min vs a 6h lockDuration. `quant-eod-sync`: grace 45min vs a 2h lockDuration / **5.5h** `withJobTimeout` wrapper whose own comment records real measured runs of 153/157/239min — grace=45min was almost certainly false-flagging on every single run. `ml-daily-ops` (`critical: true`): grace 60min vs a 4h lockDuration / 3.5h `withJobTimeout` — and this file's own job-schedule-review note independently measured this job's real runtime at **~4.4h**, with a separate note recording a run completing at 01:23 IST off a 19:30 IST start (~5h53m), both exceeding even the source-declared 4h/3.5h ceiling. Being the platform's central, most complex ML pipeline job, running every weekday, a 60min grace was very likely producing a real "Job running late" Telegram alert most weekday evenings. Fixed each to its source-declared ceiling + a modest margin (`mc-screener-sync` 60→105, `quant-eod-sync` 45→360, `ml-daily-ops` 60→270, `ml-weekly-retrain` 180→390) — deliberately the mechanically source-derivable number, not stretched to match the less-precise CLAUDE.md anecdotal ~4.4h/5h53m figures for `ml-daily-ops`, which describe an evolving pipeline-growth concern already flagged elsewhere as unresolved and worth its own dedicated look, not folded into this fix. Cascaded to the sub-steps (`ml-daily-ops`'s 15 × 120→280min, `ml-weekly-retrain`'s 2 × 240→400min) to preserve the "wider than parent" relationship the fixed parents' new values would otherwise have broken.
  - **Building the extraction logic surfaced two more real, empirically-found bugs on top of the marker-matching lessons already recorded for the cron-pattern test**: (1) the lockDuration regex didn't handle this codebase's `60_000` JS numeric-separator style (only `60 * 1000`), silently dropping several real matches; (2) checking every occurrence of a marker and taking the globally nearest forward match (the technique that correctly handles cron patterns) picked up an *unrelated later job's* lockDuration for `closed-day-early-batch` — a `recordHeartbeat(...)` call reusing its name sat closer to the *next* queue's (`dl-retrain-emergency`'s) lockDuration than its own. Fixed by searching forward only from the marker's FIRST (registration) occurrence, since lockDuration always sits relative to a job's own registration in this codebase, never scattered oddly relative to a later reference call.
  - **Negative-controlled**: reverted `jobRegistry.ts` to its pre-fix state and confirmed the exact same 4 failures reproduce with the exact lockDuration/withJobTimeout numbers in the failure messages, then restored. Full suite: 466/494 passed (was 407/435 before this file), same pre-existing `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Fifth mirror-consistency test, `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.graceMinutes` (2026-08-03, same audit series).** `monitor.router.ts`'s `getSystemStatus()` uses `computeCronLateness(..., graceMinutes, ...)` for any entry with `cronPatterns` — the same "wait `graceMinutes` past the expected fire time" mechanism as `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes`, just for the DB-freshness-check registry instead of `job_heartbeat`. `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` (19 tests) locates each entry's real driving job(s) via the same marker technique and asserts `graceMinutes` covers the largest of them (`outcome-resolver-5d`/`-15d` are fed by two independent jobs via two `cronPatterns` and must cover whichever runs longer).
  - **Found a large, systematic drift**: 9 of the 16 `cronPatterns`-bearing entries are steps inside the `ml-daily-ops` chain (`cronPatterns: ['0 14 * * 1-5']`) — the exact job the `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes` test above already found needed 270min, not 60 — but every one of these 9 `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` entries (`outcome-resolver-5d`, `outcome-resolver-15d`, `performance-tracker`, `fii-dii-fetcher`, `finbert-scorer`, `ml-ensemble-score`, `reward-engine`, `rl-agent-update`, `signal-type-stats`) was *also* still at `graceMinutes: 60`, completely independently: these are two separate registries tracking the same underlying jobs through different mechanisms (`job_heartbeat` vs DB-freshness), so fixing one never touched the other. Four of the nine are `critical: true`, meaning this was very likely producing real "Critical engine stale" Telegram alerts on top of the already-fixed JOB_REGISTRY "Job running late" ones for the *same* chain. Fixed all 9 to 280min, matching the corresponding JOB_REGISTRY sub-step fix for consistency between the two registries. The other 7 `cronPatterns`-bearing entries were already consistent; `intraday-breadth-capture` (setInterval-driven, no lockDuration exists) is explicitly exempted with its own reasoning documented inline.
  - **Negative-controlled**: reverted, confirmed the exact same 9 failures reproduce with the exact real-ceiling numbers, then restored. Full suite: 485/513 passed (was 466/494 before this file), same pre-existing failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Sixth audit: coverage completeness for JOB_REGISTRY's everyMs jobs (2026-08-03, same series).** User asked to extend the audit to whether `everyMs`-driven jobs (`news-sentiment`, `trendlyne-intraday`, `confluence-compute`) should also have a `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` DB-freshness entry, not just the `job_heartbeat` "did it run" signal already covered.
  - **Found a concrete structural gap for `confluence-compute`**: `confluence.jobs.ts`'s processor returns `{ computed: 0, ... }` — a successful, non-throwing return — on every tick where `isMarketOpen()` is true (a deliberate skip: this positional signal intentionally doesn't run during market hours). BullMQ fires `'completed'` identically whether real work happened or the run was a no-op, so `job_heartbeat` reports "success" every 30 min forever even if `confluence_signals` silently stopped updating entirely — only a DB-freshness check on the real output table can catch that.
  - **`news-sentiment`**: a different but real case — no deliberate skip, but a silent partial failure (e.g. every RSS source returning empty) would report "success" with `fetched_at` never advancing — the same "job succeeded but wrote nothing new" pattern this codebase's incident history keeps recording.
  - **`trendlyne-intraday`**: deliberately given **no** entry. `runIntradayScreenerScan()` only writes on a genuine new high-scoring breakout with no existing active signal, so zero new rows on a quiet day is normal — a naive freshness check would false-alarm. Worse, its write target (`unified_signals`, `signal_source='screener'`) is shared with several unrelated writers, so even a "recent rows exist" check would be misleadingly reassuring about this job specifically. A real check needs a scan-attempt log, out of scope here.
  - **Shipped**: two new `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` entries (`confluence-compute` — `staleLimitHours=10h`, since the real worst-case gap is the ~6h15m weekday-market-hours-skip window, not one clean 5-field cron pattern; `news-sentiment` — `cronPatterns=['*/15 * * * *']` + `graceMinutes=45`, genuinely 24/7 so an exact cron works), each wired to a real `getLastRunAt`/`getScriptStats` case in `monitor.router.ts` (without the former, the switch's `default: return null` would silently show "never run" forever — worse than no entry). Both ids added to `jobRegistryVsMonitorScripts.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_SAFE_OVERLAPS`. `monitorScriptsCronMirror.test.ts`, `monitorScriptsStaleLimitConsistency.test.ts` (new `marketHoursSkipGated` model, fixed 6.25h constant), and `monitorScriptsGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` all extended to cover the two new entries. New `everyMsMonitorCoverage.test.ts` (6 tests) encodes the finding directly — re-verifies `trendlyne-intraday`'s exemption reasoning against actual source (its write is still conditional, `unified_signals` still has multiple writers) rather than just asserting it, so a future refactor that changes either premise fails loud instead of leaving a stale exemption.
  - **Negative-controlled**: removed `confluence-compute`'s `getLastRunAt` case, corrupted `news-sentiment`'s `cronPatterns`/`graceMinutes`, corrupted `confluence-compute`'s `staleLimitHours` below 6.25h — all four caught, then restored. Full suite: 495/523 passed (was 485/513), same pre-existing failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **`edge_adjustment_enabled` turned on, re-verified as a live no-op (2026-08-03).** Following the gap-analysis roadmap's #1 sequencing item, checked `app_settings.edge_adjustment_enabled` and found it **already `'true'`** — flipped by a concurrent session sometime after the 2026-07-23 no-op verification (that entry's "Flag currently OFF" is now stale; corrected here). Re-ran the read-only `scripts/diff_edge_adjustment.py` against live data rather than trusting the old check: **still a complete no-op** — BEAR AUC 0.630 and SIDEWAYS AUC 0.567 (moved from 0.618 since 07-23) both still clear the 0.55 trust floor with sufficient history (26-31 distinct days, 4 episodes each), so no shrinkage fires; 0 of 0 `recommendation_log` ACTIVE rows flip (that table now has 0 `technical_scan`-sourced ACTIVE rows, down from 45 in July — reflects normal turnover, not a bug), 0 of 21 `scoring_engine` win-probs changed, 0 of 2,259 `unified_ranker` bet sizes changed. HIGH_VOL still isn't `ready` (only 3 days of history). No code/restart needed — the flag is read live from the DB on every call, so it's already active in production. The mechanism will only start doing something the moment a regime with enough history drops below 0.55 AUC, which is exactly the point of it.

- **Score & signal table consolidation, phased execution of the previously-deferred "Phase 3" governance work (2026-08-03).** User asked to consolidate the 3 score tables + 6 (really 5 -- signals was already dropped) signal tables flagged in the gap-analysis roadmap; scoped via plan mode into a Score track (S1/S2) and a Signal track ("Cluster B-lite", continuing 2026-06's Cluster A) after a full consumer inventory (3 parallel research passes) found the naive interpretation was architecturally wrong -- `stock_scores`/`quant_scores` are `unified_ranker.py`'s *inputs*, not duplicates of `unified_recommendations`, so they cannot be physically merged into it.
  - **S1 (real correctness bug, not just cleanup):** `quant_scores` had three writers with a confirmed-live ordering conflict, verified directly against `queues.ts`'/`screeners.jobs.ts` cron patterns (not just claimed) -- `institutional_quant_engine.py` (ml-daily-ops, 7:30 PM IST, full `DELETE`+re-`INSERT`) was always clobbered same-night by `quantScoringService.ts`'s own upsert (11 PM IST, identical columns); live-verified via a seed-then-rerun smoke test that a real sibling column (`mf_quality_score`) survived a second engine run only after the fix. Retired as an automatic writer, kept upsert-safe on-demand. `multi_factor_scorer.py` claimed in its own comment to depend on `quantScoringService` already having run, but ran 3.5h *before* it -- moved into the `quant-scoring` job, after `runQuantScoring()`.
  - **S2:** `getStrategyStocks` gained `unified_recommendations` context columns (`ur_unified_score`/`ur_classification`/`ur_conviction_level`) + an opt-in `requireUnifiedCoverage` gate with cold-start fallback -- deliberately NOT a table swap like `getTopRatedStocks`'s existing UR-first/`stock_scores`-fallback, since UR and `quant_scores` share zero filterable columns and a swap would have silently broken every filter.
  - **B1-B7 (Cluster B-lite):** found and retired a second, behaviorally-*drifted* dead copy of the technical-analysis engine (`backend-python/app/technical_analysis_engine.py` -- missing two bug-fixes the live `src/server/` copy already had, and its only route had zero callers anywhere). Folded `technical_analysis_signals` into `unified_signals` (`signal_source='technical'`) using the exact mapping precedent from Cluster A's own deferred-but-fully-spec'd Task 5/6/7 -- `rsi`->`technical_score` (kept numeric, not buried in text, since `strategySignalsService.ts`'s `qualityOversoldScanner` filters on it with `<= ?`), `patterns`->`ai_reasoning` (still JSON-parseable). User confirmed scheduling the engine nightly (it had *never* run automatically before -- only reachable via `mcpServer.ts`'s on-demand MCP tool), taking the ml-daily-ops slot `institutional_quant_engine.py` vacated. Repointed all 3 non-UI readers, dropped the table (177 live rows, all pre-migrated) via a new SQLite migration `074_drop_technical_analysis_signals` + live `DROP TABLE` on the Postgres container. **End-to-end live-verified with a real production run, not just a dry pass:** `technical_analysis_engine.py` actually executed against all 2,450 stocks, wrote 2,429 real `signal_source='technical'` rows, and all three repointed readers were confirmed reading real data afterward.
  - **B5/B6 (independent cleanups):** caught up `signal_outcomes`'s `label_definition`/`is_suspect` columns in both tracked schema files (previously added only at runtime by `data_integrity_repair.py`, invisible to a fresh bootstrap) -- pure documentation, live DB unchanged. Renamed the misleadingly-named `getUnifiedSignals` tRPC procedure (it read `technical_signals`+`confluence_signals`, never `unified_signals`) to `getTechnicalConfluenceSignals`.
  - **Deliberately out of scope, with reasons written into CLAUDE.md's own governance section:** full Cluster B (`technical_signals`->`technical_features` rename, dropping `signal_outcomes`) -- ~35 writer files/~69 readers across the entire Python ML stack, plus `signal_outcomes`'s mixed fixed-barrier-vs-path-based label definition needs to be *resolved*, not just documented, before any training join through it is safe. This mirrors the project's own established discipline (see the 2026-07-30 audit's repeated “deserves its own pass with a full consumer inventory” language) of not doing high-blast-radius consolidations as a drive-by.
  - **Process note:** followed the exact TDD-per-task methodology Cluster A itself established (failing test -> implementation -> passing test -> live-Postgres smoke -> commit, one task at a time) rather than inventing a new approach -- this consolidation is a continuation of a real team plan, not a fresh design.

- **signal_outcomes signal_source fix, closing the real "mixed label definition" mechanism (2026-08-03).** User asked to fix the full "Cluster B" (technical_signals rename + signal_outcomes drop + its data-quality issue) flagged as future work in the prior session. Investigated via plan mode rather than assuming the earlier framing was correct — and it was NOT: the "mixed label definition across horizons" finding from the 2026-07-30 audit undersold the real mechanism. `signal_outcomes` had no `signal_source` column, so THREE independent writers — `outcome_resolver.py` (h1/5/15, grades `technical_signals`), `confluence_outcome_tracker.py` (h1/3/7/14/30, grades the unrelated `confluence_signals` table), and a genuinely undiscovered third writer found only by grepping every remaining `signal_outcomes` reference at the end of the sweep, `signalOutcomesService.ts` (its own separately-scheduled `signal-outcomes-daily` cron, 9 AM IST weekdays, also grading `technical_signals` with yet a different methodology) — all guarded their dedup checks against "does ANY row exist for this key", regardless of writer/source. Whichever wrote first silently won a given `(symbol, signal_date, horizon_days)` forever; the horizon split the audit observed (h1/5/15 vs h3/7/14/30) was a byproduct of this collision-avoidance, not a designed labeling scheme. Verified this empirically against live data (NEUTRAL-band width per horizon, `computed_at` distribution) before designing the fix — an early hypothesis ("the resolver code was rewritten since the audit, historical rows are just stale") was tested against real data and refuted; the real mechanism was confirmed by tracing the actual cron schedules in `queues.ts`.
  - **Fix**: widened the key to `(symbol, signal_date, horizon_days, signal_source)`, mirroring `unified_signal_outcomes` (which already had exactly this column for exactly this reason — direct precedent, not a new pattern) and reusing Cluster A's exact SQLite rename→recreate→copy→drop migration dance. Stamped all three writers; `technicalSignalsService.ts`'s PENDING-seed row also needed the same stamp — without it, a seeded row at the column default (`unknown`) would never be matched by any resolver's `ON CONFLICT` target and would orphan permanently while a second row got created alongside it (found by reasoning through the seed/resolve lifecycle, not by a test failure). Backfilled 267,976 existing rows by inferring source from the already-correct `label_definition` split (sound, not a guess — the one-writer-per-horizon-bucket mechanism made the inference exact: 145,412 → technical, 122,564 → confluence, zero remaining unclassified). Audited and fixed ~20 consumer files across the ML stack — `ml_ensemble.py`'s core training query (both dialect branches, both label modes) was the single most important fix, arguably more consequential than anything else in this pass, since it's the platform's primary model. Two files needed the OPPOSITE filter (`confluence_ml_engine.py`, `confluence.router.ts`'s `getConfluenceOutcomes`) — confirmed by reading each query's actual JOIN logic rather than assuming every consumer wants 'technical'.
  - **Scope decisions, both confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before proceeding**: fix `signal_outcomes` now (real, active, ongoing correctness bug — not deferred); skip the `technical_signals`→`technical_features` rename (confirmed via exhaustive grep to touch 141 files, not the ~35/~69 originally estimated — purely cosmetic, zero functional benefit, not worth 141-file mechanical risk alongside a real bug fix). Also investigated and REJECTED merging `signal_outcomes`/`unified_signal_outcomes` into one table (the original "collapse to one outcome table" target) — `unified_signal_outcomes` is FK'd to a specific `unified_signals` row instance; `signal_outcomes` grades events that mostly have no corresponding `unified_signals` row at all. These answer genuinely different questions; forcing a merge would require dropping the FK or writing a `unified_signals` row for every graded event. A closed question, not a deferred one.
  - **Live-verified, not just unit-tested**: ran BOTH `outcome_resolver.py` and `confluence_outcome_tracker.py` for real against production (43 + 510,754 outcomes respectively); confirmed real symbols (e.g. `ADANIPORTS`/2026-08-02/h1) now genuinely carry both a technical- and confluence-sourced row simultaneously — structurally impossible before this fix; spot-checked `ml_calibration.per_regime_auc` (plausible AUCs 0.56-0.81) and `confluence_ml_engine.build_training_data` against the fresh data. New regression test for the `signalOutcomesService.ts` writer gap, negative-controlled: reverting its `ON CONFLICT` target throws `SQLITE_ERROR` against the now-widened schema (a harder failure than the original silent-collision bug, since the schema itself changed underneath the old target).
  - **Process note**: the Agent tool hit a session usage limit mid-investigation (three parallel research agents all failed simultaneously) — confirmed direct tool calls (Grep/Read/Bash) still worked, and completed the rest of the investigation via direct greps/reads instead of retrying the Agent tool, rather than blocking on it.
- **Audit-backlog sweep, live_datasource coverage (2026-08-04).** User asked to complete remaining pending audit items. Cross-checked all 4 audit docs + CLAUDE.md's own session notes end-to-end: nearly everything closeable had already been closed across prior sessions (Finding #31 resolved via shrinkage, survivorship closed in prices, endpoint-corpus P0-P3 shipped, 6 job-monitoring mirror-consistency test suites built, `edge_adjustment_enabled` live-verified as a no-op). The one item every pass kept re-flagging without ever fully closing is `live_datasource` test coverage — explicitly named in the fifth-pass audit as "the single largest open gap in this codebase" (~6% of ~140 DB-writing fetchers at that pass, drifting up to ~17-23% across several later ad-hoc sessions, still nowhere near complete). Added 2 more this session, prioritizing the audit's own explicit recommendation list rather than picking arbitrarily: `test_live_datasource_moneycontrol_fetcher.py` (the single broadest scraper in the platform — ~10 endpoints across 8 tables in one `fetch_stock_data()` call — previously entirely untested despite being named first in every priority list going back to the fifth pass) and `test_live_datasource_mc_earnings_fetcher.py` (feeds 11 `technical_signals` columns `ml_ensemble.py` reads directly: `days_to_next_results`, `earnings_category_yoy/qoq`, `earnings_np_growth_yoy/qoq`, `earnings_shocker_flag/gain`, `eps_beat_last_q`, `mc_eps_vs_cons`, `positive/negative_turnaround`). Both live-verified against real MoneyControl endpoints (RELIANCE across 5+ moneycontrol_fetcher.py endpoints; 807 real earnings-calendar items + 781 real price-shocker items from mc_earnings_fetcher.py's two market-wide endpoints on a live run). The `mc_earnings_fetcher.py` test resolves whichever `scid`s the live market-wide feed returns *today* against the real production `nse_stocks` table rather than a synthetic mapping, so the DB-round-trip half stays deterministic without needing to guess which tickers will appear in a given day's feed. `live_datasource`-marked test files: 23. Full Python suite after both additions: 981 passed, 85 skipped, 0 failed. **Still open, not attempted this session** (each needs its own dedicated pass, not a drive-by): the remaining ~115 untested fetchers (`nt_change_oi_fetcher.py`/`nt_oi_snapshot_fetcher.py`/`nt_pcr_ts_fetcher.py`/`nt_vix_fetcher.py` checked and confirmed NOT to need the DB-stored NiftyTrader Bearer token this file previously flagged as blocking — they use plain public endpoints — but their `execute()`/`executemany()` calls route through `db_compat.get_engine()` directly with no DB-injection point, unlike the `con`-parameter style `mc_earnings_fetcher.py`/`mc_pricefeed_fetcher.py` use, so testing them safely needs a `monkeypatch` on the module-level DB functions rather than the pattern used here); actually retraining the LSTM (still rolled back to v3, 6+ weeks stale); the 3 unbuilt endpoint-corpus fetchers (superstar investors, event calendar, the `fno/market/filter` options surface beyond `so_option_chain_fetcher.py`); `screener_appearances` bitemporality (won't-fix, mitigated forward via `screener_membership_snapshot`).
- **GNews integrated as a news source tier, then redesigned twice in the same session based on live results + user feedback (2026-08-04).** User asked to integrate GNews for stock/market/global news. First cut was one `runGNewsCycle()` (3 calls, flat 3h cadence) gated only on `GNEWS_API_KEY` being present. User then gave a real key (added to `.env`, gitignored) and asked to keep interval choices appropriate per use-case and to also cover per-stock news specifically. Final shipped design in `newsSentimentService.ts`:
  - Split into 3 differentiated cycles instead of one flat job: `runGNewsMarketCycle()` (India business headlines + NSE/BSE/Nifty/Sensex search, every 2h), `runGNewsStocksCycle()` (one OR-joined search over a rotating batch of 5 names from the top-60-by-market-cap universe, cursor persisted in `app_settings.gnews_stocks_rotation_offset`, full universe rotates every 12h, every 1h), `runGNewsGlobalCycle()` (world business headlines, every 6h) — combined 52 req/day against the 100/day free-tier cap, weighted toward the cycles that actually feed trading decisions.
  - **Live-verified against the real endpoint and found a real bug before shipping**: 3 concurrent GNews calls via `Promise.all` got 2× HTTP 429 (a burst/concurrency limit on the free tier, not the 100/day quota — a lone call immediately after succeeded with real articles). Fixed by pacing `runGNewsMarketCycle`'s 2 calls 1.2s apart instead of running them concurrently.
  - **Added an explicit `app_settings.gnews_enabled` gate, default OFF, on top of the API-key gate** — the live response itself carries `information.realTimeArticles: "Real-time news data is only available on paid plans. Free plan has a 12-hour delay."`, a materially different freshness guarantee than every other source in this pipeline (RSS ~15-30min, MC/BSE genuinely live); shipping it enabled-by-default would have silently mixed 12h-stale data into what's presented as live market/stock news. Flip on with `UPDATE app_settings SET value='true' WHERE key='gnews_enabled'` once that tradeoff is accepted for a given use case.
  - **Added `runMcStockNewsCycle()`** — a genuinely live per-stock source (no delay caveat) reusing `fetchMcStockNews()`/`resolveMoneycontrolSymbol()` already proven working for the per-stock news tab (`McNewsCard.tsx`, 2026-07-31). No metered quota (`mcFetchJson`'s own `Semaphore` throttles concurrency platform-wide already), so it runs denser than Google News: top-100 by market cap, every 2h. Live-verified: 10 companies → 61 real, correctly-tagged articles in one run.
  - **While live-verifying, found (not fixed) a real pre-existing bug**: `mcApiService.ts`'s `decodeMangledEscapes()` requires a non-alphanumeric character immediately before a mangled `uXXXX` sequence to repair it, so `"Coforge's"` mangled to `"Coforgeu2019s"` is NOT repaired (the `e` before `u` blocks the match) while `"...Pick u2013 Why..."` (space before `u`) is. Live-confirmed via a real ingested MC headline stored uncorrected. The function's other two guards (requires a digit in the 4 hex chars; requires a non-ASCII decoded codepoint) likely already provide enough disambiguation that the prefix-boundary requirement is over-cautious and blocks the single most common real case (mangled possessive apostrophes) — flagged as a follow-up, not fixed this session (found via a different task than what was asked).

Fixed by switching both tests' marker for this job to `newsSentimentWorker = new Worker`, the same override pattern already established for `intraday-fetcher`.

Full suite 2,936/2,936 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean — both verified after every redesign, not just at the end.
- **Closed the 2 items the urls.txt field analysis had deferred (2026-08-04) — one turned out already done, the other exposed a real cross-provider bug.** User said "do whatever is needed" in reply to the report's two "not done, needs more work" items.
  - **MC's historical Ohlson/Dupont/Altman/Graham scores: already fully built, one day before this analysis even ran.** `mc_stockvitals_history_fetcher.py` (committed 2026-08-02, `0f09168`) already does exactly the "cross-validate before adding a second copy" thing the report asked for — it writes into the SAME `proprietary_scores_history` table/`score_type` keys the existing daily widget scrape (`moneycontrol_fetcher.py`) already populates, deepening `altman_z`/`ohlson_o`/`graham_number`/`dupont_score` coverage from ~14-16% to ~81-85% with zero query changes needed anywhere, and wired Graham/DuPont into `ml_ensemble.py`. Scheduled monthly in `trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts`, live-datasource tested, live-verified again this session (real data 2000-2026, 1980-2018 stocks per metric). **Lesson: the "not done" recommendation was already stale the moment it was written — should have grepped for an existing fetcher before flagging a gap, exactly the kind of check this file's own audit sessions repeatedly say to do and this one skipped.**

What genuinely was new: diffing urls.txt's 178 captured `(catId,scanId)` pairs against the 77 already in `MC_SCREENERS` (after deduping by scanId alone — MC reuses the same scanId across several `catId` "category" tabs, and `moneycontrol_screeners`/`screener_master` are keyed on scan_id alone regardless of catId, so re-listing an already-covered scanId under a second catId would just double the live fetch for no new data) found **24 genuinely new scanIds**. Each live-verified (real `scannerName`/`scannerDescription`/stock counts) before adding — notably, every one of the 77 pre-existing pro-scanner entries is `is_positive: true`; this batch adds the **first bearish/distress fundamental scanners this codebase has ever ingested into the MC screener source** (Falling Profits/Sales/Margins, Rising Promoter Pledge, Deteriorating Cash Conversion Cycle, FII/Promoter Selling, Profit-to-Loss) — until now that side of MC's own screener catalog only ever surfaced bullish screens, the same one-sided-screener-weighting bug class the 2026-07-31 intraday audit flagged in a different source. Live-ran the full sync end-to-end against the real DB (101 scanners, all fetched successfully, real stock counts matching the live-verification pass exactly).
  - **THE finding, caught only because the sync was actually run for real rather than just added and left**: `screener_master.scan_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` has **no source disambiguation** — it's a single global key shared across MoneyControl/Trendlyne/ETnow/et_marketstats, all of which assign their own scanId/screenpk/screener_key independently. `syncMoneyControlScreeners()` also had its own bug that made this worse: it only ever `UPDATE`s `screener_master`, never `INSERT`s (the "Update screener_master sync time" step is a bare `WHERE scan_id = ?`, no upsert) — so a genuinely brand-new MC scan_id got **zero** `screener_master` row at all, silently falling back to `confluenceEngine.ts`'s generic neutral/weight-5 classification default instead of a real inferred sentiment/category. Fixed the missing-INSERT bug (`ON CONFLICT(scan_id) DO NOTHING` seed added right after the existing upsert, so it never clobbers classification work `screenerClassifier.ts` has already done). **But fixing that surfaced the deeper bug**: 7 of the 24 new MC scanIds (166→ no, specifically 173/428/519/520/521/522/525) are **numerically identical to existing, live, actively-populated ETnow scanIds** — e.g. MC's "Double Dhamaka" (scan_id 173) collides with ETnow's "Warren Buffet Screener" (also scan_id 173, 9 real stocks in `etnow_screener_stocks` today). Confirmed via direct query: 0 scan_ids in the table currently show >1 distinct `source` (first-writer-wins, ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING silently drops the loser) — meaning **ETnow's classification metadata is silently applied to these 7 MoneyControl screeners** in `confluenceEngine.ts`'s scoring (`ensureScreenerMeta()`/`classifyScreener()` both key purely off bare `scan_id`, no `source` parameter anywhere in the lookup path). Quantified precisely (not estimated): checked ALL 133 distinct MC scan_ids against `screener_master`, all 133 have *some* row (0 missing after the fix), but exactly 7 are ETnow-owned, not MoneyControl-owned. Trendlyne (numeric `screenpk` in the thousands-to-hundred-thousands, and increasingly slug-based) and et_marketstats (hash-based `screener_key`) don't share MC/ETnow's small-integer ID space, so this collision risk is specifically an MC-vs-ETnow problem, not a 4-way one.
  - **Deliberately NOT fixed further this session** — a real fix means migrating `screener_master` to a `(source, scan_id)` composite key and updating every consumer (`confluenceEngine.ts`'s `ensureScreenerMeta()`/`classifyScreener()`, `screenerClassifier.ts`, and all 4 provider syncs' `UPDATE ... WHERE scan_id = ?` sites) to pass `source` through the whole lookup chain — a genuinely separate, wider-blast-radius change than "add MC's new scanners," and one that touches confluenceEngine's live cross-provider scoring for the whole platform, not just these 7 IDs. Left as a flagged, quantified, reproducible finding rather than either silently shipping a bigger unrequested migration or silently leaving it undocumented. The 24 new MC scanners' own raw data (`moneycontrol_screeners`/`moneycontrol_screener_stocks`/`screener_appearances`) is unaffected by this bug — only the `screener_master` NLP-classification enrichment layer is wrong for these 7, and only in the direction of falling back to a real-but-wrong (ETnow's) classification rather than MC's correct one.
  - **Verification**: full MC screener sync run twice against the real production DB (once to add the 24, once after the screener_master seed fix), confirmed all 24 real fetches + correct `is_positive` values + correct `screener_master` seeding for the 17 non-colliding ones. Full suites: TS `tsc --noEmit` clean, vitest **438 files / 3,443 tests passed** (including every session's worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` — confirms no cross-session interaction issues from the concurrent activity visible in this repo this week). Docs corrected: `docs/url_explorer/DATA_CATEGORIZATION_AND_USAGE.md`'s two "not done"/"not wired" claims fixed to reflect what's actually true.

Findings (not all acted on, recorded for a future pass): (1) 8 overlapping "what to buy" nav tabs (`Buy Recs`/`Alpha`/`Best Picks`/`Top Rated`/`Signal Intel`/`Signal Report Card`/`Signals`/`Today's Picks`) with no visual hierarchy signaling which is authoritative relative to the backend's documented `unified_recommendations` canonical ranking; (2) no freshness/as-of indicator anywhere on `TradeDecisionCockpit.tsx`, the actual "should I trade now" screen; (3) decision tables poll via React Query (10–60s) rather than the existing price WebSocket; (4) `PriceAlertsPanel.tsx`/`AlertsToast.tsx` are toast-only, no `Notification` API or sound; (5) no command palette beyond the existing `/`-to-search-a-stock shortcut; (6) no single reverse-chronological "today's activity" feed merging signals/alerts/news; (7) `SignalTracking.tsx`'s ledger has no clickable column sort (can't rank by worst-PnL-first for risk triage); (8) provenance labeling (model score vs. derived heuristic, already correctly done in `TradeDecisionCockpit`'s factor breakdown per a past audit) is inconsistent across other list pages. **Scope-checked before implementing #3**: the existing WebSocket (`websocketService.ts`) is an alert/event bus (`new_signal`/`price_cross`/`technical_breakthrough`/`portfolio_move`, threshold-gated), not a continuous price-tick stream — and `liveStockData.ts`'s own bulk price cache only refreshes every 5 min (`BULK_TTL`) regardless of transport, so switching the frontend's polling to WebSocket would not have actually improved freshness; the real, honest, immediately-valuable fix was surfacing the true cadence, not disguising it as faster than it is.
  - **Shipped**: `TradeDecisionCockpit.tsx` and `Watchlist.tsx` now show an explicit freshness readout (`relativeFromNow`/`formatISTWithLocal` from the pre-existing `lib/timeFormat.ts`, matching the exact convention already used in `CommandCenterDashboard.tsx`/`BuyRecommendationsPage.tsx` — reused rather than inventing a new pattern) plus an amber warning when the last successful fetch is older than ~2–3x the poll interval (browser-fetch-recency only, deliberately NOT a trading-day staleness threshold, since this codebase has been burned repeatedly by weekend/holiday-blind staleness checks — see the look-ahead-bias-audit memory). `misc.router.ts`'s `getTradeDecisionCockpitData` now also returns `marketOverview.asOfDate` (the most recent `technical_signals.date` any candidate is grounded in), so a trader can distinguish "the browser just fetched" from "the underlying scan itself is from today."
  - **Live-verified in-browser, and in doing so hit a real environment gotcha worth recording**: this sandbox's `npm run serve`/`npm run dev` scripts hardcode a *relative* path (`node_modules/tsx/dist/cli.mjs`), but this worktree's own `node_modules` is nearly empty (only a stray `.vite` cache dir) — TypeScript/Vitest work fine via `npx` because bare-specifier imports (`import 'react'`) resolve up the directory tree to the parent repo's `node_modules` (this worktree lives *inside* `bharat-stock-intelligence/`, not beside it), but a literal relative path like `node_modules/tsx/dist/cli.mjs` does not get that treatment and fails `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Worse: `npx tsx server.ts` launched via a backgrounded Git-Bash subshell repeatedly ended up with its *actual* OS-level cwd resolving to the **main repo**, not the worktree (confirmed via each zombie process's sourcemap output pointing at `D:/Github/bharat-stock-intelligence/src/...` with no `.claude/worktrees/...` segment) — meaning several verification attempts were silently checking the *unedited main-repo file*, not this session's change, until caught by explicitly diffing served-module content against the on-disk worktree file. **While chasing stray processes on port 3000, accidentally `Stop-Process`'d a pre-existing PM2-managed `bharat-server` instance** (`pm2 status` revealed it, previously mistaken for one of my own zombie test processes) — PM2 auto-restarted it within seconds (restart counter ticked up by one), no lasting effect, but it's exactly the kind of shared-infrastructure disruption the safety rules warn about; resolved by running the worktree's verification server on an isolated port (3055) instead of fighting for :3000. **Lesson for any future session needing to boot this worktree's dev server**: don't trust `npm run serve`/`dev` as-is here, and don't assume a Git-Bash `(cmd &)` background launch's cwd matches the shell's `cd` — verify via the served module's inline sourcemap or an explicit `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` command-line check before trusting what's being served, and if `pm2 status` shows a process you didn't start, treat it as shared infrastructure, not a leftover to kill.
  - Both pages confirmed rendering correctly end-to-end (populated `TradeDecisionCockpit` showing `fetched just now` in the correct non-stale slate color, tooltip showing `formatISTWithLocal`; empty-state `Watchlist` correctly suppressing the freshness line via its existing `watchlistStocks.length > 0` guard) — `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite (56 files / 581 tests) green throughout.

Ran the non-forcing `npm audit fix` (package-lock.json only, no package.json range changes) — resolved `brace-expansion`, `fast-uri`, `hono`, `ip-address`, `postcss`, `undici`, `@hono/node-server`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` (8 packages, 6 of the 7 highs) down to 12 remaining (1 critical, 3 high, 8 moderate). **Deliberately did not force the other 2**, re-confirming the exact same call this repo already made on 2026-07-23: `vitest@4.1.10` (fixes the one critical, dev/test-tooling only, not shipped to users, but a major bump risking this suite's custom `singleFork` pool config) and `firebase-admin@10.3.0` — a **downgrade**, not an upgrade, and firebase-admin is what `protectedProcedure`/`adminProcedure` depend on for ID-token verification; downgrading risks breaking auth, not just tests. **New finding this pass**: `react-router`'s "safe" fix bumped it forward but a residual high-severity CSRF-bypass CVE remained even after that — npm's only proposed remedy is `react-router-dom@7.11.0`, which is actually *older* than what the safe fix had just installed (the vulnerability was introduced in 7.12.0+ and hasn't been patched forward yet as of this session) — left as-is rather than downgraded, since react-router is real routing infra for the whole app, not dev tooling, and this wasn't part of the user's explicit "safe fixes only" scope. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 605/605, and a live-browser routing smoke test (client-side nav to `/screener` correctly updated the URL and rendered real content) confirmed the react-router bump itself didn't break anything.
- **Closed the remaining UX-audit backlog + both flagged follow-ups in one pass (2026-08-04), same-day continuation.** User asked to finish the 4 remaining audit items (nav consolidation, sortable Signal Ledger, alert notifications, command palette) plus fix the 2 previously-deferred findings. All 6 shipped, live-verified, full suite green throughout.
  - **Duplicate screener id, root-caused not patched over**: `trendlyneScreener.ts`'s `getTrendlyneScreenerList()` merges 3 independent sources (Trendlyne `screener_id`, MoneyControl `scan_id`, ETnow `screener_id`) into one list with a raw, unprefixed `id` field — since each source hands out its own numeric sequence, ids collide across sources (confirmed live via the React "duplicate key" warning). Deliberately did NOT frontend-dedupe (that would silently drop one of two genuinely distinct screeners sharing a coincidental numeric id) — namespaced each source's id (`tl-`/`mc-`/`et-` prefix) at the source instead, the only correct fix since `id` is purely a display/key field (the real data fetch keys off `screenpk`, untouched). **Also found, deliberately NOT fixed this pass**: `screener_master`'s primary key is `scan_id` alone (not `(scan_id, source)`), so `build_screener_metadata()`'s `ON CONFLICT(scan_id)` upsert in `scoring_engine.py` can silently misattribute one source's NLP-inferred sentiment/category onto an unrelated screener from a different source that happens to share a raw numeric id — a real scoring-pipeline data-integrity risk, but a Python-schema-migration-sized fix, out of scope for a nav-bug pass. Flagged for a dedicated follow-up.
  - **F&O Intel's misleading empty-state copy fixed with a new reusable `isNseMarketOpen()` helper** (`lib/timeFormat.ts`, IST-aware, Mon-Fri 09:15-15:30, no holiday calendar — same documented limitation as `AppShell.tsx`'s existing `getMarketStatus()`). The message now correctly distinguishes "no signals for this filter" from "market is genuinely closed" instead of always hedging with "may be closed or data loading" regardless of actual state (that branch is only reached once `isLoading` has already resolved false, so "or data loading" was never even accurate). `FnOHeatmap.tsx`'s adjacent "Sectoral heatmap currently unavailable" was checked and left alone — already accurate (confirmed live: `getTrendlyneFnoHeatmap` genuinely returns `{data:[]}`, a real empty upstream response, not a broken request).

Growth/PnL defaults **ascending** (worst performer first), inverted from every other numeric column's "biggest first" default — deliberate, since the audit's whole point was "no way to see which open positions are bleeding right now" for risk triage. **A real hook-ordering bug caught before it shipped**: the first draft called the new `useMemo`/hook after the component's existing `if (isLoading) return` / `if (error) return` early returns — a Rules-of-Hooks violation that would throw "Rendered fewer hooks than expected" on every loading→success transition. Caught by `tsc`, not by testing (TypeScript doesn't catch this class of bug either, in general — this one happened to surface via a leftover duplicate-declaration compile error that led to inspecting the diff more carefully).
  - **Alert notifications**: new `lib/browserNotify.ts` (localStorage-backed prefs, both default OFF; a synthesized two-tone Web Audio chime — no bundled asset — debounced 800ms so simultaneous alerts don't stack; browser `Notification` fired **only while the tab is backgrounded**, since the in-app toast already covers the foreground case and firing both would be noisy) wired into **both** of this app's independent alert pipelines: `useAlerts.ts` (SSE, `/api/stream` — price-alert triggers, via `queues.ts`'s `checkPriceAlerts()` → `broadcastAlert()`) and `AppShell.tsx`'s separate WS toast (`/signals` — new-signal/price-cross events). These are genuinely two different delivery mechanisms built at different times; a single wiring point wouldn't have covered both. New `AlertPreferencesToggle.tsx` (bell icon, always visible, mounted inside `AlertsToast.tsx`) gives an opt-in toggle + permission-request flow for both prefs.
  - **Command palette**: new `CommandPalette.tsx`, Cmd+K/Ctrl+K from anywhere, extending (not replacing) the existing `/`-to-search-a-stock shortcut — searches both nav pages and stocks in one list, arrow-key navigation, shows all nav items when the query is empty (so it doubles as fast keyboard-driven nav browsing, not just a filter). Reads `NAV_GROUPS` generically as a prop, so the nav-consolidation restructuring below flows through automatically with no palette changes needed. **A real verification trap hit while testing this**: the automated browser tool's `computer.key` action for `ctrl+k` never reached the page at all (Chrome reserves Ctrl+K for the address bar in real usage too — a genuine cross-browser conflict worth knowing about, not just a test artifact) — `window.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent(...))` was needed to test it programmatically instead. That in turn hit a second false negative: the modal WAS mounting and receiving the event (confirmed via the handler firing and the DOM node appearing with correct content — 40 real nav results, correct placeholder text), but `document.body.innerText` reported it as absent because Framer Motion's enter animation was stuck at `opacity:0` (a RAF/compositor throttling artifact specific to this headless test harness, not a real bug — a real user's active tab animates normally). Querying the DOM node directly (`querySelectorAll('button')` inside the palette root) rather than trusting `innerText` was what actually confirmed it worked.
  - **Nav consolidation**: split the old flat 18-item "Intelligence" sidebar group into three, verified against each page's actual backend query and on-page copy rather than assumed from labels alone — "Alpha" turned out to self-label "Canonical model" in its own UI text, and "Today's Picks" turned out to read `getTechnicalConfluenceSignals` (confluence-based), not the canonical ranking, despite sounding like it might be. **Top Picks** (`buy-recs`/`alpha`/`top-rated`/`trade-cockpit` — all `unified_recommendations`-backed or the decision cockpit itself) placed right after Markets, the most prominent slot. **Alternative Screens** (`best-picks`/`strategy`/`strategy-builder`/`signal-intelligence`/`screener-intelligence`/`early-spotter`/`todays-picks` — independent scoring models, several of which already self-disclose "not the unified cross-engine model" in their own copy) and **Signal Tools** (`signal-tracking`/`signals`/`signal-report-card`/`sentiment`/`backtest`/`builder`/`research` — logs, diagnostics, and market context, not ranked "buy this" lists at all) follow after Analysis. Embedding was mechanical (`DashboardPage.tsx` now renders `<ActivityFeed onSelectStock={onSelectStock} />` in a new "Activity Feed" card between the Index Overview row and Top Movers row, matching the page's existing `glass`-card wrapper convention).
  - **Methodology**: rather than guess, drove the actual pm2-managed `bharat-server` instance (the real main-repo checkout on :3000, real Postgres, real live data — not this worktree's empty SQLite dev DB) through all 41 nav tabs via a scripted sweep (`nav button` click + wait + capture `main.innerText` + `read_network_requests`), looking specifically for HTTP 500s/400s and 207 Multi-Status batches with embedded per-procedure errors. **A real methodology trap hit and corrected along the way**: the first two rapid-fire sweep attempts (1.1-1.4s per tab) produced flaky, contradictory results (e.g. "Screener" showing a generic "Sign in..." auth-gate flash on one pass, full real content on a slower re-check) — this app does heavy client-side data fetching per tab, and rushing the sweep produced false positives. Slowing to ~2.2s/tab made every result reproducible. Also note for any future session: `window.fetch` monkey-patching after the app has already booted does NOT intercept trpc's own requests (the client captured its own `fetch` reference at init) — `read_network_requests` (the browser-extension-level capture) is the reliable way to catch trpc call failures, not an in-page patch.
  - **Found (already fixed same day, just not yet deployed to `main`/pm2): `sig.growth_pct.toFixed is not a function` still crashing the live Signal Ledger** — confirms the earlier `pgClient.ts` NUMERIC-parser fix (this file's next entry below) is real and actively affecting production right now, pending merge.
  - **New Finding 1 — `getSignalReportCard` 500'd on every single call, permanently stuck on "Loading signal report...".** `ml.router.ts`'s `activeSignalGrowth` query did `SELECT ts.id, ...FROM technical_signals ts` — but `technical_signals` has **no `id` column at all** (its natural key is the composite `(symbol, date)`, per `db/schema.postgres.sql`), so Postgres threw `column ts.id does not exist` on every request. Fixed by synthesizing a stable composite id: `ts.symbol || '-' || ts.date AS id` (the frontend's `key={`${row.id}-${index}`}` in `SignalReportCard.tsx` already had an `index` tiebreaker, so this was never a hard requirement for uniqueness — just needed a real, non-crashing value).
  - **New Finding 2 — `getAdvanceDecline` silently 400'd inside a batched 207 Multi-Status response whenever called with no input.** `indices.router.ts`'s schema was `z.object({ ex: z.string().optional().default('N') })` — the FIELD is optional, but the OUTER object is not, so Zod rejects `undefined` entirely ("expected object, received undefined"). `TradeDecisionCockpit.tsx` calls `trpc.getAdvanceDecline.useQuery(undefined, ...)` (no input) — exactly the shape that fails. Because this ran inside a `Promise.all`-batched tRPC call alongside 3 other procedures that succeeded, the failure surfaced only as an easy-to-miss `207 Multi-Status` HTTP status with the real error buried in one array position — the Trade Cockpit's "ADV / DEC" figure just silently rendered "—" forever, with nothing in the browser console pointing at why. Fixed by wrapping the whole input schema in `.optional()` and defaulting inside the handler (`input?.ex ?? 'N'`) — a common Zod trap (`.optional()` on a field ≠ the object itself being omittable) worth checking on any other `publicProcedure` a frontend calls via `useQuery(undefined, ...)`.
  - **New Finding 3 — `mf_composite_score`/`mf_risk_adj_score` (from `multi_factor_scorer.py`) can be a genuine Postgres NaN, and NaN rows were ranking #1 in "top stocks by multi-factor score" instead of being excluded.** Unlike JS, Postgres treats float NaN as a valid, non-null value that sorts GREATER than every real number — so `risk.router.ts`'s `WHERE mf_composite_score IS NOT NULL` / `ORDER BY mf_composite_score DESC` let NaN rows through and put them at the very top of the ranking, and the frontend's `value != null` guards in `RiskMetricsDashboard.tsx` don't catch NaN either (`NaN != null` is `true` in JS) — so a stock with a broken score would render "Composite: NaN" instead of being hidden. Fixed by wrapping every `mf_*` column read (both `getRiskMetrics` and `getMultiFactorScores`) in `NULLIF(col, 'NaN'::float8)` (Postgres considers NaN = NaN true, so this converts it to a real SQL NULL) — the same defensive class of fix as `unified_ranker.py`'s `_finite_engine_map()` from the 2026-07-31 NaN-poisoning incident, just at the TS/SQL layer instead of Python. **A real cast-syntax gotcha hit while writing this**: the first draft used `'NaN'::double precision` (the natural Postgres spelling), but `sqlTranslate.ts`'s `stripPgCasts()` regex (`/::[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:\[\])?/g`) only matches single-token type names — `double precision` is two words, so on the SQLite dev-fallback path only `::double` would get stripped, leaving a dangling ` precision` as broken literal SQL text. Switched to `::float8` (Postgres's single-token alias for the same type), which the regex handles correctly and which is what every other `::type` cast in this codebase already uses for this exact reason.
  - **Also investigated, not fixed — recorded so it isn't rediscovered from scratch**: a `TrendlyneScreenerPanel.tsx` "Encountered two children with the same key" React console warning (`key={screener.id}` inside `filteredCategories.map()`) — traced to the two `key={screener.id}` usages in the file being in mutually-exclusive ternary branches (not the cause), so the real duplication is almost certainly `filteredCategories` itself containing the same `screener.id` more than once (a screener matching multiple category filters without dedup) — a real but lower-severity "duplicate row in a grid" bug, not investigated further this pass. `F&O Intel`'s "SECTORAL HEATMAP CURRENTLY UNAVAILABLE" / "NO SIGNALS DETECTED — MARKET MAY BE CLOSED OR DATA LOADING" was traced to `getTrendlyneFnoHeatmap`/`getTrendlyneFnoScanners` genuinely returning `{data: []}`/`tableData: []` for the default `long-build-up` filter at the moment checked (200 OK, not a broken request) — real empty upstream data, not a frontend bug, though the "MARKET MAY BE CLOSED" copy is misleading when the market is actually open (a copy fix, not a data fix). `Best Picks`'s "0 setups" and `Signal Intel`'s "Last: Never" label were seen but not root-caused this pass.
  - **Verification**: 3 new backend tests (`signalReportCard.test.ts`, `riskRouter.test.ts` — the latter includes both a live-query happy-path test and a source-string-pinning test for the NaN guard, since genuine Postgres NaN storage/comparison semantics can't be reproduced against the SQLite test DB this suite runs against), full suite 589/589 passing, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Both fixes were then re-verified live against the real worktree server on an isolated port (3056, not :3000 — same pm2-collision avoidance as the earlier freshness-indicator session): `getAdvanceDecline` with no input now returns real advance/decline data instead of a validation error, `getSignalReportCard` now returns real `sourceSummary`/`outcomeSummary` data instead of a 500, and the Dashboard's new Activity Feed card renders real live news ("just now", real headline/summary) end-to-end.
- **`pgClient.ts` was missing a NUMERIC/DECIMAL type parser — silently returned Postgres `numeric` columns as strings, crashing the Signal Ledger and masquerading as a backend outage (2026-08-04).** User reported two symptoms that turned out to be the same bug: "Service temporarily unavailable" on a tab, and `sig.growth_pct.toFixed is not a function` in the console. Root cause: `signals.router.ts`'s `getSignalTracking` (and `ml.router.ts`'s `getSignalReportCard`) compute `growth_pct` via `ROUND(<double precision expr>, 2)` — but Postgres only has a 2-argument `round()` overload for the `numeric` type, so it silently upcasts the double-precision expression and the column comes back typed `numeric` (OID 1700) even though every input column is `DOUBLE PRECISION`. `pgClient.ts` already had custom `setTypeParser` overrides for `INT8` and `TIMESTAMP` (both documented gotchas from earlier sessions) but never covered `NUMERIC`, which `node-postgres` leaves as a raw string by default (to avoid float precision loss on bignums — not a concern here, every NUMERIC column in this schema is a ratio/percentage/score). `SignalTracking.tsx`/`V2SignalTracking.tsx` called `sig.growth_pct.toFixed(2)` directly on that string and threw during render; the throw was caught by `TabErrorBoundary` in `App.tsx` and rendered as "Service temporarily unavailable" — a frontend crash, not an actual backend/API outage. `SignalReportCard.tsx`'s `formatPct` helper had the identical latent bug (its `Number.isNaN(value)` guard doesn't catch a numeric-looking string). **Fixed at the driver level**, matching the existing `INT8`/`TIMESTAMP` precedent in `pgClient.ts`: `types.setTypeParser(types.builtins.NUMERIC, ...parseFloat...)` — fixes this occurrence and every other current/future `ROUND(...)`/`AVG()`/`SUM()`-over-numeric result across the whole app in one place, rather than patching each frontend call site individually. New regression test in `pgClient.test.ts` pins the parser returns a real number `.toFixed()` can be called on. Could not visually verify in this sandbox — no live Postgres reachable (`.env` absent, `:5433` unreachable), and the SQLite fallback path doesn't reproduce this bug at all (better-sqlite3 already returns real JS numbers for arithmetic), so verification stopped at the unit-test layer; **needs `pm2 restart bharat-server` on the real deployment to take effect** (`.ts` edits aren't hot-reloaded — see `pm2_restart_required.md` memory). **Lesson, extending the existing INT8/TIMESTAMP precedent**: any SQL expression that mixes a floating-point/double-precision column into a function whose only multi-arg overload is `numeric` (not just literal `NUMERIC`/`DECIMAL` column declarations) can silently change the wire type of a result column — check the actual OID of anything arriving from a `ROUND(expr, n)`/`CAST`/aggregate expression before assuming a `DOUBLE PRECISION` input column guarantees a `DOUBLE PRECISION` (auto-number-parsed) output.
- **"Implement all improvements" sweep from the 2026-08-04 signal-accuracy review, 8 tasks, all live-verified against production (2026-08-04).** User asked to act on every improvement the accuracy review recommended, with signals nav capped at 1-2 pages. Full detail split across new memory files (indexed in MEMORY.md); summary here.
  - **Nav consolidation**: `/alpha` (CommandCenterDashboard) is now the single primary signals page; `/trade-cockpit` is the 2nd (execution companion). `/buy-recs` and `/alpha-cockpit` (both ran the literal same `getBuyRecommendations`->`unified_recommendations` query as `/alpha` under different names) now `<Navigate to="/alpha" replace />` in `App.tsx` -- old bookmarks still work, there's no longer a real choice between 3 near-identical pages. `Top Rated` (legacy `stock_scores`-only) moved out of the "Top Picks" nav group into "Alternative Screens" alongside Strategy/Strategy Builder, in both `AppShell.tsx` (v1) and `V2AppShell.tsx` (v2/v3) -- `V2AppShell.tsx`'s nav hadn't been touched by the 2026-08-04 UX-audit session that restructured v1's, so it still had `alpha-cockpit`/`buy-recs` as separate entries. `V4QuickNav.tsx`/`DashboardPage.tsx`/`TopPicksWidget.tsx` updated to point at `/alpha`/`/trade-cockpit` directly instead of the now-redirected paths. Verification gap, disclosed rather than hidden: this sandbox's curl and the Browser-pane tool both failed to reach locally-launched dev servers (reachable via live DB queries fine, but not HTTP) despite the servers reporting ready in their own logs -- relied on a clean `tsc --noEmit` plus careful diff re-reading for this specific change instead of a rendered screenshot.
  - **Telegram entity-parse fix, root-caused not just patched.** Two live failures that day (08:40, 15:04 IST) traced to `jobWatchdog.ts`'s `checkAndAlertDataQuality`/`buildDailyDigest` and `queues.ts`'s `data-quality-daily` worker interpolating `dataQualityChecks.ts`'s `detail` strings raw -- which routinely embed literal snake_case table names ("mf_sector_allocation is empty"), an unbalanced-underscore entity that kills Telegram's whole legacy-Markdown message. `data-quality-daily`'s own cron is `10 3 * * *` = 08:40 IST, an exact match. Moved `telegramRecommendations.ts`'s existing `sanitizeMarkdown()` (strip, not escape -- legacy Markdown's escaping is inconsistent across clients) down into the base `telegramService.ts` module and applied it at 7 previously-unsanitized interpolation sites across `telegramService.ts`, `jobWatchdog.ts` (3 sites), `jobs/agents.jobs.ts` (2 sites -- LLM-generated `narrative` text, never sanitized before), `routers/monitor.router.ts`, and `queues.ts`. New regression test pins the exact underscore-in-detail failure mode.
  - **`screener_master`'s (source, scan_id) collision -- the largest single item, migrated live.** Full detail in the screener_master_cross_provider_scanid_collision_2026_08_04 memory. MC and ETnow independently hand out overlapping small-integer scan_ids (7 confirmed collisions); the table's PK was `scan_id` alone, so whichever provider synced first for a given number silently owned the row forever and the other's screener got its classification. Migrated to `PRIMARY KEY (source, scan_id)` (`migrations/1785900000001_...sql`, applied live via `npm run migrate:up`) and threaded `source` through ~15 TS/Python consumers (`confluenceEngine.ts`, `scoring_engine.py`'s `build_screener_metadata`, `screener_features_fetcher.py` -- the file feeding `screener_momentum_score`, the ranker's largest weight in most regimes -- plus 4 provider syncs, `screeners.router.ts`, `quantScoringService.ts`, `strategySignalsService.ts`, `strategy_optimizer.py`, `screener_performance.py`, `trendlyne_screener_discovery.py`, `screener_catalog_enricher.py`, `screener_signal_generator.py`). Found and fixed 2 more bugs along the way: `confluenceEngine.ts`'s per-stock screener-membership dedup was ALSO bare-scan_id-keyed (would have silently dropped, not just misclassified, a stock's second membership when it collided); `scoring_engine.py` tagged et_marketstats 'ETMarketstats' while the DB's real stored value is 'et_marketstats' -- a casing mismatch that silently missed `SOURCE_WEIGHTS` (fell back to 0.9 instead of the intended 0.8) and would have produced duplicate rows under the new composite PK. Live-verified: re-ran `build_screener_metadata()`'s incremental path against production post-migration -- all 7 previously-missing MoneyControl rows now exist alongside their ETnow counterparts with correct, independently-classified sentiment (e.g. scan_id 522: ETnow "Hinduja Group"=neutral vs MC "Falling Quarterly Profits"=bearish). Row count 1649->1669. `screener_performance_history` and `screener_reliability` have the identical bare-scan_id gap and were deliberately left open (documented, not silently skipped).
  - **8th `unified_ranker.py` engine: smart money.** New `_get_smart_money_scores()` blends insider buying (`technical_signals.insider_buy_pct_90d`) + institutional block-deal accumulation (`block_deals.pct_transacted`, buy-side, capped at 100 for 10%+ of float bought in 90d) -- previously only shown on the standalone `/smart-money` page, never fed into the canonical score. Wired into `REGIME_WEIGHTS` at a flat 0.05 in every regime (same treatment as `cs`), with `ml`/`confluence`/`technical`/`dl` proportionally shrunk per-regime to absorb it -- all 5 regimes re-verified summing to exactly 1.0. Deliberately excluded from the ranker's symbol union (should raise conviction of a stock another engine already surfaced, not introduce one on its own). Live-verified: full `python unified_ranker.py` run against production succeeded, 1548 stocks scored, `engine_coverage_count` now maxes at 8 (was 7).
  - **Survivorship fix extended to `breakout_classifier.py`'s training data.** `_load_ohlcv()` read exclusively from `stock_ohlcv` (backfilled by iterating the *current* `nse_stocks` master, structurally excluding ~306 since-delisted companies) -- meaning the model's own headline "5yr purged-OOF AUC 0.61" was measured on a survivorship-biased universe. Fixed by reusing (not reimplementing) `backtester.py`'s already-proven `Backtester.load_bhavcopy_adjusted()` to fill in `nse_universe_history` symbols missing from `stock_ohlcv`. Live-verified: +385 delisted symbols, +14,578 real split-adjusted bars. - **Three enforcement mechanisms shipped from a system-identification review's own recommendations (2026-08-04)**, converting "keep this in mind" into repo-enforced state rather than trusting memory:
  - **`live_datasource` coverage +3 (26→29 marked test files)**, prioritized by blast radius per the established method: `asm_gsm_fetcher.py` (the fetcher with the single worst live-confirmed bug in this repo's history — a WHERE-clause-less UPDATE that nulled surveillance flags platform-wide — had never had a live test despite that), `market_regime_fetcher.py` (feeds `macro_asset_prices`, `critical: true`, gates which `REGIME_WEIGHTS` set the entire ranker blends under), `mc_index_oi_fetcher.py` (the file whose call/put OI weighting was swapped until Finding #51 of the 2026-07-30 audit — the new test asserts the computed max-pain strike falls within the real fetched strike range, the exact check that would have caught that swap). All 3 live-verified against real NSE/MC endpoints. `asm_gsm_fetcher.py`'s `upsert_flags()` has no `con` injection point and closes its own connection — a shared `:memory:` sqlite handle loses its data before read-back, so the write-path test uses a real temp-file DB instead.
  - **`feature_coverage_gate.py`** (new, `src/server/`) — a pre-flight tool for wiring a new engine into `unified_ranker.py`'s blend. Reuses `densify_feature_matrix.py`'s `sparse_columns()` methodology but generalized to report per-column coverage on the busiest recent date (never `date.today()`, never pre-densification — both documented traps that make a real feature look falsely empty or a genuinely empty one look falsely fine). Pinned via a new `TestEngineCountIsPinned` class in `test_unified_ranker_regime.py` (exact 8-engine key set × exact 5-regime set, parametrized) — adding a 9th engine now requires a conscious edit to that test, whose docstring points at running the gate tool first. Live-verified the tool against real data: correctly resolved 2026-07-27 (2,228 rows) as the busiest recent date over more-recent partial-collection days, and correctly flagged a real schema typo instead of crashing.
  - **`scripts/check_load_bearing_constraints.py`** (new) — makes "is this still true" a runnable question instead of a fact to trust from memory, for the two findings most at risk of being silently treated as stale caveats: the intraday emission gate's live open/closed state (via `IntradayRanker()._emission_allowed()` — live-run confirmed still CLOSED at -0.491%/trade over 1,566 trades in the 10d window, with an explicit "this changed, re-verify" banner if it ever flips open) and `REGIME_WEIGHTS`' `breakout` weight per regime against a pinned ceiling derived from the 2026-07-30/07-31 negative-momentum findings (live-run confirmed all 5 regimes exactly at ceiling — `BULL=0.15, SIDEWAYS=0.13, HIGH_VOL=0.10, BEAR=CRASH=0.05` — no silent re-inflation). Both the gate's `EMISSION_GATE_MIN_TRADES`/`MIN_AVG_PNL`/`LOOKBACK_DAYS` constants and the breakout-weight ceiling are pinned by new regression tests (`test_intraday_ranker_gate.py::TestEmissionGateConstantsArePinned`, `test_unified_ranker_regime.py::TestBreakoutWeightCeilingIsPinned`) — **negative-controlled**: corrupting the real `REGIME_WEIGHTS['BULL']['breakout']` value and `EMISSION_GATE_MIN_TRADES` in the source file and re-running confirmed both pinned tests fail with the exact corrupted value in the message, then reverted via `git checkout`.
