A missing import at collection time aborts the entire `pytest` run (`Interrupted: 1 error during collection`, zero tests executed) rather than failing just that one file — reinstalled `nse` into `.venv` too. Full suite after all fixes: **1,132 passed, 106 skipped, 12 failed** — all 12 failures are in `test_confluence_outcome_tracker.py`/`test_screener_pit_scores.py`, testing `confluence_outcome_tracker.py`/`screener_performance.py`, both confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` to be mid-edit, uncommitted, modified by a concurrent session this session never touched — none of this session's own new/edited files (all in the pass count) failed. `tsc --noEmit` clean (no TS files touched this session).
- **Job-timing/frequency audit against real IST market-session structure (2026-08-05), user-driven ("is running screener jobs at night AND morning bringing anything extra").** Inventoried all ~55 scheduled jobs (`queues.ts` + `src/server/jobs/*.jobs.ts`) with their real IST fire times and, for the evening cluster specifically, opened each job's actual function body rather than trusting its name/comment. The literal "screener sync at night vs. morning" framing doesn't exist in this pipeline (screener membership only ever synced once/evening) — but a **more damaging version of the same pattern** was found and fixed: three jobs independently re-doing the same fetch within one evening.
  - **THE finding**: `quant-eod-sync` (22:00 IST) called `syncMoneyControlScreeners()` and `syncETnowScreeners()` **again**, on top of the dedicated `mc-sync`/`etnow-sync` jobs 100-140 min earlier (18:20/18:40 IST) — and `stock-scoring`'s scheduled path (22:30 IST, via `syncAndScore()`) called **both of those again, a third time**, plus `syncAllScreenerStocksToDB()` (Trendlyne) a second time. `quant-eod-sync` also called `runFullFundamentalsSync()` — a full-universe, no-staleness-filter Yahoo Finance deep pull with its **own dedicated weekly job** (`sync-fundamentals-weekly`, Sunday) — every single weeknight, and `pcr_fetcher.py --gex` a redundant 3rd time that day (already fetched every 15 min all session + once in `ml-daily-ops`). None of this ever errored — every call just re-wrote identical rows — so it was invisible to every existing job-health check, exactly the "job says success, tells you nothing about whether it added value" pattern this file's own history keeps recording.
  - **Fixed, all live-verified via `tsc --noEmit` + full vitest suite (409 files / 3401 tests, 0 failures)**: `quant-eod-sync`'s screener/fundamentals/`--gex` steps removed entirely (kept: NiftyTrader/Trendlyne proprietary-score sync, Trendlyne technical snapshots, delivery-map — none of which duplicate anything else). `screeners.jobs.ts`'s `processStockScoring` now calls `recalculateScores()` directly instead of `syncAndScore()` — `syncAndScore()` itself is untouched and still backs `scoring.router.ts`'s on-demand "resync everything now" tRPC trigger, where an explicit re-sync is the point. **Removing both re-sync call sites would have left Trendlyne screener-stock membership with NO scheduled sync at all** (it had never had one of its own — it only ever rode along inside quant-eod-sync/syncAndScore) — added a proper `trendlyne-screener-sync` job (18:10 IST, between et-marketstats 18:00 and mc-sync 18:20, `screeners.jobs.ts`), with matching `JOB_REGISTRY` entry (`graceMinutes: 105` matching mc-screener-sync's own lockDuration-driven correction), `monitor.router.ts` dashboard wiring (queueList + triggerBullMQJob, both places), and module-level Queue/Worker exports + shutdown `.close()` calls in `queues.ts`. Updated `jobPipelineOrdering.test.ts`'s stale "et-marketstats-sync is the ONE source syncAndScore() doesn't re-sync, so only it is load-bearing" comment/test — with syncAndScore() gone from the scheduled path, **all four** screener syncs are now load-bearing ahead of stock-scoring, not just belt-and-braces.
  - **Two smaller off-hours-waste findings, also fixed**: `confluence-compute` (30-min cadence, already skips during market hours) had no further gate, so ~15-17 nightly ticks between ~00:30-08:00 IST recomputed the whole-universe positional signal against provably-static input (nothing writes to `technical_signals`/screener tables/`stock_scores`/`quant_scores` again until the next landing event). Added `isConfluenceComputeWindow()` (`confluence.jobs.ts`) restricting real work to the evening data-landing window (17:00-23:59 IST) plus a short pre-open refresh window (06:00-07:59 IST, ahead of `unified-ranker` at 07:30). `trendlyne-checklist-cycle` (self-rescheduling every 15-45 min, ~3-day full-universe cycle then 30-day dormancy) had **zero market-hours awareness** at all — unlike every other Trendlyne-touching job, it kept ticking straight through 09:15-15:30 IST, adding concurrent Trendlyne request load on top of `trendlyne-intraday-scan`'s own calls for data (5yr PAT trend, sales growth) with no intraday sensitivity whatsoever. Added the same `isMarketOpen()` skip-and-reschedule pattern used elsewhere in the file.
  - **One scope question, resolved with the user rather than guessed**: `trendlyne-daily-fetch` (10 AM-10 PM IST, weekdays) traced end-to-end to `fetchTrendlyneStockMetrics()` — **pure cache-warming, zero DB persistence**, for the same 12h-TTL cache the on-demand `getTrendlyneStockMetrics` tRPC procedure (stock-detail "Stock Metrics" popup) already warms itself on click.   - **MoneyControl Screener Sync's "ON CONFLICT" failure — already self-healed, no action needed.** Live-traced via `job_heartbeat`/`pgmigrations.run_on`/`pm2 jlist`'s `pm_uptime`: the `screener_master` (source,scan_id) PK migration landed on the live DB at 06:56 UTC 08-04, the matching code fix committed at 10:56 UTC, but `pm2` wasn't restarted until 18:32:55 UTC — so the 12:50 UTC failing run executed the pre-fix code against the post-migration schema. Live-verified all 4 constraints `syncMoneyControlScreeners()` touches now match the current code exactly; the 18:32 restart already picked it up. Another instance of the "migration/code committed but process not restarted" class ([[migration_tool_verification_gotcha]]) — check `pm_uptime` against both the migration's `run_on` and the fix commit's timestamp before assuming a fix is live.

  - **Intraday Breadth Capture — real, currently-recurring bug, fixed.** `monitorScripts.ts`'s `cronPatterns: ['*/15 3-10 * * 1-5']` mirrored the underlying job's hour-boundary-rounded BullMQ registration, which as a literal cron matches through 10:45 UTC — 45 min past the real 15:30 IST market close (10:00 UTC). The capture's `recordHeartbeat` only fires inside the `isMarketOpen()` gate (unlike siblings `market-regime-refresh`/`intraday-ranker`, which also stamp success on the closed-market early-return), so `MAX(snapshot_at)` genuinely freezes at ~10:00:00 daily while `computeCronLateness()` kept expecting an occurrence as late as 10:45 — producing a real "Critical engine stale" Telegram alert every single trading-day evening (confirmed: last real snapshot 2026-08-04T10:00:00.238Z, matching the digest exactly). Fixed by narrowing to 3 bounded sub-patterns (`'45 3 * * 1-5'`, `'*/15 4-9 * * 1-5'`, `'0 10 * * 1-5'`) that precisely cap the expected occurrence at 10:00 UTC; excluded from `monitorScriptsCronMirror.test.ts`'s literal-string sweep (same precedent as `news-sentiment`'s synthesized-equivalent exclusion, since these 3 strings never appear verbatim in `queues.ts`). New test suite includes a negative control proving the OLD pattern really did produce `late=true` at the exact digest scenario, plus a genuine-outage case. **Non-obvious property learned while writing that last test**: `computeCronLateness()` structurally cannot flag "late" while `now` is still inside a high-frequency cron's matching window when `graceMinutes` exceeds the interval (rolling `expectedAt` stays within one interval of `now`, so the deadline is always in the future) — a genuine all-day outage can only be caught once checked after that day's window has closed. See [[intraday_breadth_capture_false_stale_2026_08_05]] for full detail.
- **Sell/Strong-Sell rows carrying long-only trade geometry, closed at 2 more layers (2026-08-05).** User traced live production data showing Sell/Strong-Sell `unified_recommendations` rows with long-style entry/stop/target (stop below entry, target above) despite `position_size_pct` already correctly zeroed for them — root cause was `confluenceEngine.ts`'s `buildTradeSetup()` being called unconditionally regardless of `netBearish` (already fixed and committed to `main` at HEAD by a concurrent session, per the note directly above — this session's investigation independently re-derived the identical root cause and found it already fixed). What was NOT yet fixed, found and closed this session: (1) **`unified_ranker.py`'s `_get_entry_targets()` pulls its 3-source fallback chain (`confluence_signals`/`recommendation_log`/`unified_signals`) by bare symbol only, with zero awareness of the row's own `classification`** — so any future drift in any of those 3 sources (a reintroduced confluenceEngine.ts bug, a stale row, a new writer) could reintroduce the exact same class of bug with no defense at the point of truth. Added a write-time backstop in `run()`: for any row not classified `Buy`/`Strong Buy`, `entry_zone_low/high`, `stop_loss`, `target_1/2/3`, `risk_reward`, and `timeframe` are nulled unconditionally — extending the exact invariant `position_size_pct` already enforced (`raw_sizes[sym] = ... if classification in ('Strong Buy','Buy') else 0.0`) to the geometry fields themselves, so the two can never disagree again regardless of upstream source. `trade_reasoning`/`sector` are left alone (informational, not a trade plan). (2) **A second, mirror-image variant in the same fallback chain**: `_get_unified_signals_latest_map()` (fallback #3) selects the newest `unified_signals` row for a symbol with no `signal_type` filter at all — but `unified_signals` genuinely carries `signal_type` (BUY/SELL) with an inverted geometry convention per direction (`signals.ts`'s own exit-monitoring logic treats them oppositely), so a **Buy**-classified `unified_recommendations` row could inherit a SELL signal's short-style geometry whenever confluence_signals/recommendation_log both miss for that symbol. `recommendation_log` (fallback #2) was checked and confirmed already safe — `scoring_engine.py`'s `_log_recommendations()` only ever logs `Buy`/`Strong Buy` candidates, never writes a Sell row at all. Fixed fallback #3 with `WHERE signal_type = 'BUY'`, matching that same established convention. **Live-verified end-to-end**: queried production before/after — 2026-08-04's snapshot (pre-confluenceEngine-fix) showed 408/408 Sell and 273/273 Strong Sell rows with `stop_loss` populated (681 total, consistent with the user's reported ~630 at a slightly different intraday moment); re-ran `unified_ranker.py` live against production Postgres after this session's backstop landed — 2026-08-05's fresh 1,548-row snapshot now shows **0/519 Sell, 0/127 Strong Sell, 0/346 Hold with any geometry field populated, while Buy (523/527) and Strong Buy (29/29) correctly retain real entry/stop/target**; `trade_reasoning` stays populated on every row regardless of classification. 5 new regression tests in `test_unified_ranker.py` (`TestSellRowGeometryBackstop`, `TestUnifiedSignalsDirectionFilter`) — including a guard against over-nulling (a genuine Buy candidate's geometry must survive untouched) and a "newer SELL row must not shadow an older BUY row for the same symbol" case. Full suites: Python 1165 passed/106 deselected, TS 3561 passed across 426 files, `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not committed** — left for the user, since multiple concurrent sessions have uncommitted changes on this branch (`screener-data-strategy-audit`) this session did not touch (`monitorScripts.ts`, `test_intraday_ranker_gate.py`, several new `feature_coverage_gate.py`/`live_datasource` test files — all pre-existing modifications from other in-progress work, left alone).

- **Continuation of the Sell-row-geometry investigation: sector/provider-mapping gaps closed, R:R<1 floor added, cs_ranker "bug" confirmed a false alarm (2026-08-05).** User asked to fix everything the earlier investigation had flagged as out-of-scope. All 4 items live-verified against production.
  - **Sector gap (2,216/2,366 ACTIVE missing sector) — root cause was TWO compounding bugs, both fixed.** `backfill_sectors.py` (already wired weekly into `nse-sync-weekly`) was a structural no-op for ~95% of the universe: its only source, `confluence_signals.sector`, is itself populated from `nse_stocks.sector` (`confluenceEngine.ts`'s `nse?.sector`), so it can never create a classification that didn't already exist — a closed information loop, not a bug in the propagation logic itself (step 3, propagating into `recommendation_log`/etc., is genuinely useful and untouched). Separately, `syncNSEStocksToDatabase()`'s `ON CONFLICT` upsert did `sector=excluded.sector` unconditionally — since the static seed file (`src/data/nseStocks.ts`) hardcodes `'Unknown'` for ~95% of symbols, this same weekly job would have silently wiped any real backfilled value back to `'Unknown'` on its very next run, a live self-defeating bug (verified via a real SQLite+Postgres round-trip test before trusting it, not just reasoning about the SQL). Fixed the upsert to a CASE guard (take the incoming value only if it's non-placeholder, or the existing row has nothing at all — never let a placeholder downgrade a real value), and ran the two real, independent, previously-unwired-anywhere backfill scripts that already existed in the repo: `backfill_sector_mc.py` (MoneyControl pricefeed, `main_sector`/`newSubsector`, ~98% coverage) then `backfill_sector_industry.py` (Yahoo Finance `assetProfile`, fallback for the mcsymbol-less remainder). Live: 2,216 → 51 missing (97.7% resolved) — found and fixed a real gap in `backfill_sector_mc.py`'s own `_GICS` map along the way (MC's `"Real Estate"` label had no entry even though `"Realty"` did, plus 13 more unmapped-but-common MC sectors found on the live `--enumerate` run, e.g. `Electricals`/`Paper`/`Aviation`/`Footwear`; `"Miscellaneous"` deliberately left unmapped — not a real GICS bucket, forcing one would be a guess). Both scripts use `COALESCE`-style never-downgrade writes, matching this codebase's established convention. Wired `backfill_sector_mc.py --enumerate --write --report-unmapped` into `processNSESync` (before `backfill_sectors.py`, so its propagation step gets fresh data), measured ~9-10 min live against the full 2,328-symbol universe, and bumped `nse-sync-weekly`'s `lockDuration` 3min→20min accordingly (the "always measure, never guess a timeout" convention this file has enforced repeatedly). `backfill_sector_industry.py` deliberately NOT scheduled — Yahoo's crumb-auth flow is flakier and slower per-symbol than MC's ~98%-coverage primary source; kept as a manual/occasional tool.
  - **Provider-mapping gap (544/2,366 ACTIVE missing mcsymbol or tlid) — the fix already existed (`scripts/syncAllStockMappings.ts`, `npm run sync:mappings`) but was manual-only, never scheduled, so newly-added `nse_stocks` rows accumulated with no provider mapping forever.** Refactored to export a callable `syncMappings()` (ESM entry-point guard via `pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href === import.meta.url`, matching Node's own recommended idiom — a naive string-concat guard was tried first and is wrong on Windows, where `file://` + a backslash-converted path is missing the third slash `file:///` needs before a drive letter) so `processNSESync` can import and call it in-process, the same pattern `syncNSEStocksToDatabase` already used. Live run: 544 → 145 missing (73.3% resolved) — the residual is genuinely unresolvable via MC autocomplete/Trendlyne's screener-membership cache (obscure/illiquid names), consistent with the already-documented `ASMS`-class gap; not chased further (never guess a provider ID).
  - **cs_ranker `cv_roc_auc≈0.107` — investigated and confirmed NOT a promotion-gate bypass.** `model_registry.cv_roc_auc` is a column shared across every `model_type` this platform registers; for classifiers it's a real AUC, but cs_ranker is a LightGBM **regressor** (`model_type='LightGBM Regressor'`) with no AUC concept — this column holds its held-out cross-sectional Spearman rho instead (same value `_active_cs_baseline()` already reads, and the code's own docstrings already knew this). Checked the full registry history: the active row (rho=0.1075, 2026-07-30) correctly **beat** all 4 subsequent retrain attempts (rho 0.078–0.094, all logged `REJECTED` by the existing champion/challenger gate) — the gate is working exactly as designed, nothing bypassed it. `getMLModelRegistry` (the only place this column is exposed) has zero frontend callers, so this was never user-facing-misleading, only investigator-facing. Documented the column-reuse landmine directly in `_register_cs_model()`'s docstring so a future review doesn't re-flag it without checking `model_type` first.
  - **86 R:R<1 `unified_recommendations` rows since July 1 — real bug for the 17 Buy/Strong-Buy ones, structurally moot for the other 69 (Sell/Strong-Sell/Hold) after the same-day geometry backstop.** Unlike fallback 1 (`confluence_signals`, whose own `buildTradeSetup()` can only ever produce `risk_reward ∈ {2,3,4}` by construction — reward/risk cancels to the reward multiplier, confirmed by inspecting the math directly rather than assuming), fallbacks 2 (`recommendation_log`) and 3 (`unified_signals`) read a genuinely independent entry/stop/target triple and can legitimately compute rr<1. All 17 live Buy/Strong-Buy offenders shared `timeframe='SWING'` — fallback 3's hardcoded literal, not derived from source data — pinpointing them to `unified_signals` specifically. Added an `rr>=1.0` floor to both fallbacks: on failure, fall through to the next source (fallback 2→3→4) rather than accept a sub-1 R:R as an actionable trade plan, mirroring the "don't present misleading data as an actionable entry" principle from the same day's Sell-geometry fix. Historical rows are left as historical snapshots (not retroactively rewritten), matching this table's established append-then-upsert-by-date convention.
  - **161 "bad OHLCV bars" — investigated, confirmed already fully healthy, no fix needed.** Queried for real OHLC-constraint violations (H<L, close/open outside [low,high], non-positive prices) both within a 90-day window (199 found) and across all of history (0 unflagged, ever) — every single one already carries `is_suspect=1` from the existing `ohlcv_quality.py` quarantine mechanism, which `backtester.py`/`performance_tracker.py` already filter on. The exact "161" figure wasn't reproduced (likely a narrower window/different constraint set in the original finding), but the underlying concern — are bad bars silently feeding backtests/training — is confirmed closed, not open.

Re-grepping properly found **9 components already had one** (`ActivityFeed`, `AlphaCockpit`, `Watchlist`, `CommandCenterDashboard`, `MoneyFlowPage`, `TradeDecisionCockpit`, `BuyRecommendationsPage`, `GlobalMarketCards`, v4's `StockIntelligencePage`) — the original claim was based on an incomplete grep.   - **List virtualization — scoped to List view only, not Grid view, and why.** `NSEStockDiscovery.tsx`'s Grid view uses a *responsive* multi-column CSS grid (1/2/3 columns by viewport) with variable-height cards; `react-window` needs a known column count and row height up front, and virtualizing a responsive card grid blind (no live browser in this sandbox to catch a layout regression) was judged too risky. List view is a single fixed-height row layout — the safe case — so it's now virtualized with `react-window`'s v2 `List` API (`rowComponent`/`rowCount`/`rowHeight`, not the v1 `FixedSizeList` API — v2 changed this significantly, verified against the installed package's actual `.d.ts` rather than assumed) and renders the **full** filtered set with no page cap or "Load more" click; Grid view keeps the existing capped-pagination stopgap from the 2026-08-02 session, whose own comment explicitly said "not pulling in a virtualization library" — corrected in the same comment now that half of that decision has changed. **Bundle-size concern from that comment turned out moot**: `react-window` only adds weight to `NSEStockDiscovery`'s own lazy chunk (29.94 kB gzip 6.45 kB, confirmed via `vite build` output), not the main bundle, since the component was already lazy-loaded — the tradeoff the prior session worried about doesn't actually apply once you check where the import lands.
  - **Structured logging — the audit's claim of "no structured logger" was wrong, and worth recording so it isn't repeated.** `src/server/logger.ts` already is a full Winston setup (JSON format, daily-rotated `app-*.log`/`error-*.log`/`exceptions-*.log`/`rejections-*.log`, colorized console transport) — imported into `server.ts` as `log` since before this session. The real gap was **adoption**: 611 raw `console.*` calls across `src/server/*.ts` alone never touch it. Rewriting 611 call sites is its own multi-session project, so instead added `installStructuredConsole()` to `logger.ts` — routes `console.log/info/debug/warn/error` through the real logger via `util.format` (preserving console.log's own multi-arg flattening behavior), returns a restore function, defaults to the real winston logger but accepts an injectable target for testing. Installed once, as early as possible, in `server.ts` — absorbs the pre-existing standalone ioredis-ACL-warning `console.warn` patch that used to live there (two independent patches on `console.warn` is exactly the kind of thing that goes stale silently; now there's one). Every existing call site in the whole process gets structured JSON + levels + rotation with zero code changes. Also added explicit `log.warn('event_name', {ip, ...})` calls with real structured fields at the three P0 gate-rejection points (`mcapi_unauthorized`, `export_picks_unauthorized`, `internal_notify_forbidden`, plus their `*_rate_limited` siblings) added earlier the same day — demonstrates the pattern new code should prefer (structured fields, not just a formatted string) for anything worth filtering/querying later, and gives immediate operational value for exactly the events a P0 security fix makes worth watching. **Test-pollution risk taken seriously**: this repo's vitest config runs the whole suite in one shared process (`singleFork: true`), so an un-restored global `console.*` patch would leak into every later test file in the same run — every test in the new `logger.test.ts` injects a fake target and restores in a `finally` block, verified by confirming a later-running test file's own `console.log` calls print normally (not swallowed) after the logger test file completes.
- **RSS news-feed refresh + a real parser bug found while verifying it reached the frontend (2026-08-05).** User asked to integrate MoneyControl's `latestnews`/`buzzingstocks`/`brokeragerecos` RSS feeds "based on freshness." Live-verification (Last-Modified headers + pubDate content, cross-checked against a genuinely live feed on the identical fetch path) found **all 6 named MC feeds are dead** — HTTP 200, content frozen Feb–Aug 2024 — including **5 already live in production `NEWS_SOURCES`** (`latestnews`/`marketreports`/`business`/`economy`/`mfnews.xml`), silently contributing zero real articles for over a year (invisible via sha1-dedup-on-first-fetch). Surfaced this via `AskUserQuestion` per [[feedback_failing_urls]] before touching anything; user chose "web-search live replacements, integrate, purge stale." Removed the 5 dead MC sources; added live-verified replacements — `ET Top Stories`/`ET Stocks in News` (flat 15-min cadence), `CNBC TV18 Business`/`World`, `MarketWatch Top Stories` — plus a new differentiated-cadence `runInvestingIdeasCycle()` (Investing.com "Stock Ideas"/"Global Economy", every 3h via a new `investing-ideas-refresh` job) for the two feeds that genuinely refresh far slower than 15 min, matching this file's own GNews/BSE cadence-differentiation precedent.
  - **Then asked to "wire the feeds to frontend."** `getNewsItems()`/`getActivityFeed` already read `news_sentiment_items` with zero per-source filtering, so nothing structurally needed wiring — the real task became *proving* the new sources actually reach the UI query path, not assuming it. Ran the real ingestion functions live against production Postgres and queried exactly as the frontend does. **Found a genuine, previously-invisible bug this way**: `ET Top Stories`/`ET Stocks in News` fetched real fresh items but persisted **zero** rows. Root cause: `extractCdata()`'s CDATA regex requires `]]></tag>` with no whitespace, but ET's real RSS emits `]]> </tag>` (a stray space) — the strict match misses, falls through to a plain-tag regex whose unstripped capture still contains the literal `<![CDATA[...]]>` markers, and `stripHtml()`'s `<[^>]*>` tag-stripper then spans from `<![CDATA[`'s `<` all the way to the `>` inside `]]>`, silently erasing the entire title. An empty title fails `parseRSS`'s `if (title)` guard, dropping the article with no error anywhere. **This also explains why the pre-existing `ET Viewandrecofeed` source — live in production long before this session — has never persisted a single article**, for a related-but-distinct reason: that endpoint isn't RSS at all, it's ET's NewsML export (`<NewsML><articlelistroot>...`, zero `<item>` tags), so `parseRSS` always returned `[]` for it. Fixed `extractCdata` to allow `\s*` around both CDATA delimiters; removed the non-RSS `ET Viewandrecofeed` (superseded by the two working ET replacements). Live re-verified end-to-end post-fix: all 6 checkable new sources confirmed reaching `getNewsItems()`'s real query path with fresh rows (12-30 items each in ~20 min); the 7th (`Investing.com Stock Ideas`) confirmed via raw `COUNT(*)` — it just didn't survive `getNewsItems`'s impact-then-recency `LIMIT 200` in a crowded 24h window, not a bug.
  - **Standing lesson**: `extractCdata`'s strict regex had been silently dropping every item from at least one production source for an unknown but clearly long duration, invisible to every existing signal (job logs show `fetched > 0`, and `news-sentiment-freshness` only watches the aggregate table, which other sources kept looking healthy). Only found because this session did a genuine end-to-end live-verification (real fetch → real DB write → real frontend-query read, using raw `COUNT(*)` not just the router's own limited/ordered query) instead of trusting "HTTP 200 with real items" as proof of integration — the same discipline that caught the dead-MC-feed finding caught this one too.
  - **Verification**: 6 new tests in `newsSentimentRssParsing.test.ts` (whitespace/newline-before-closing-tag, strict-form regression guard, plain-tag unaffected, and an e2e test driving the real `parseRSS` via a mocked ET-shaped response) + 4 in `newsSentimentInvesting.test.ts`; full suite green; `tsc --noEmit` clean on every touched file. See [[moneycontrol_rss_family_dead_2026_08_05]] for full detail.

  - **3 real bugs found while writing tests, all fixed and live-verified.** (1) `earnings_surprise_fetcher.py`'s `upsert_rows()` checked `if use_postgres:` (a function object, always truthy) instead of `if use_postgres():` — the SQLite dev-fallback branch was permanently dead code, one-line fix. (2) `mc_chart_patterns_fetcher.py`'s `ensure_schema()` `CREATE TABLE mc_chart_patterns` didn't match what `upsert_patterns()` actually writes — missing `mcsymbol`/`stoploss_price`/`stoploss_pct` columns and the wrong uniqueness (`PRIMARY KEY(pattern_id)` instead of `UNIQUE(mcsymbol, pattern_id)` — pattern_id isn't globally unique across stocks). Silently masked in production because the real table was already created via a separate migration with the correct shape, so this `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` was always a no-op there — would crash `upsert_patterns()` immediately on a genuinely fresh DB. Confirmed the real prod schema via `information_schema.columns`/`pg_indexes` before fixing. (3) `mf_holdings_fetcher.py`'s endpoint (`mfapps.indiatimes.com/ET_Mutual_Funds/pages/mftools/MFPortfolioHolding.cms`) is **currently dead — bare 404 for every input**, confirmed independent of symbol/bse-code/nse-code including RELIANCE's real BSE code; the domain itself is alive (the sibling `/Ulip/` path used by `mf_stock_holdings_fetcher.py` returns 200 fine), only this specific path was retired/moved upstream. Per [[feedback_failing_urls]], **not patched with a guessed replacement URL** — flagged here instead; the new test documents the current broken state via `pytest.skip` and will auto-validate the moment the endpoint is fixed, with no one needing to remember to update it. Also confirmed (not a new bug): `mf_sector_flow_fetcher.py`'s AMFI endpoint remains in its already-documented 2026-08-03 broken state — new test asserts that exact shape plus the `sys.exit(1)` fail-loud contract.
  - **Cross-file environment-pollution gotcha, same class as the earlier Postgres-flakiness lesson**: `db_compat.py`'s `use_postgres()`/`translate()` read `os.environ["USE_POSTGRES"]` fresh on every call, not once at import — running the full `live_datasource` suite as one pytest session means whichever test file was collected LAST to set this env var wins for every test run afterward. `mc_corporate_calendar_fetcher.py` needs `USE_POSTGRES=true` (genuinely Postgres-only SQL: `NOW()`, `ANY(ARRAY[])`, `FILTER (WHERE...)`) while ~50 other files need `USE_POSTGRES=false` — fixed by setting/restoring the env var **inside the test method body** (try/finally), not at module-import time.
  - **Also fixed one pre-existing, unrelated test-fixture drift found while running the full suite**: `test_live_datasource_trendlyne_screener.py`'s `_make_test_db()` fixture's `screener_master` table predated the 2026-08-04 `(source, scan_id)` composite-PK migration (see [[screener_master_cross_provider_scanid_collision_2026_08_04]]), so `upsert_screener()`'s real `ON CONFLICT(source, scan_id)` failed against the fixture's stale single-column PK. Fixed the fixture to match.
  - **Verification**: every new fetcher's real endpoint hit live; every write path exercised against a throwaway DB (in-memory SQLite for module-level-import fetchers, a monkeypatch-capture pattern for fetchers using bare module-level `execute`/`executemany`, and — for the one genuinely Postgres-only-SQL fetcher — a real throwaway schema inside the live Postgres instance via `CREATE SCHEMA`+`search_path` shadowing, dropped in `finally`). Full suites after all changes: Python **1051 non-live-datasource tests passed / 0 failed** (confirms the 2 source-code fixes broke nothing), and the full **live_datasource suite: 163 passed / 2 skipped (the documented-broken mf_holdings_fetcher.py case) / 0 failed**, run twice end-to-end (~11-14 min each, this repo's real full network suite runtime) to confirm no cross-file interaction issues before finishing.
- **All schema drift `checkSchemaDrift.ts` found on its first live run — closed via a new from-live generator, not by hand-patching (2026-08-05).** That checker (added earlier the same day) was originally verified only against a sandbox with no reachable Postgres; run for real this session (this checkout could reach the live `bharat_intel` DB), it reported real drift: **45 tables** and **~140 columns** live but missing from `db/schema.postgres.sql`, plus 3 columns in the file that don't exist live. Root cause matches the tool's own header comment exactly: `db/schema.postgres.sql` is generated by `scripts/generate_pg_schema.py` from `database.sqlite` (itself derived from `db.ts`), which structurally cannot see anything added via `node-pg-migrate` or ad-hoc `pgEnsureColumns()` calls — the majority of what actually landed live over the past two weeks. Hand-patching ~140 individual column adds into the right spot in a 2,700-line generated file was judged too error-prone to verify exhaustively; instead built **`scripts/generatePgSchemaFromLive.ts`** (`npm run schema:regen`) — a sibling generator that sources directly from live Postgres's own catalogs (`pg_attribute`/`pg_constraint`/`pg_indexes`/`pg_extension`, not `information_schema` alone, to get exact types via `format_type()` and identity-column detection via `pg_attribute.attidentity`) and emits the identical house style `generate_pg_schema.py` already uses (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "name" (...)`, inline PRIMARY KEY/UNIQUE, quoted identifiers) so `checkSchemaDrift.ts`'s parser — built around that exact shape — keeps working against the regenerated file unchanged. Deliberately did NOT reuse `pg_dump`: its default output uses unquoted lowercase table names, omits `IF NOT EXISTS`, and emits `PRIMARY KEY` as a trailing `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT` rather than inline — all three would have made `checkSchemaDrift.ts`'s table-name regex match zero tables in a raw dump, silently flipping "0% drift" into "100% drift" on the very next run. `db.ts`/`database.sqlite` are untouched — they remain the source of truth for the SQLite dev-fallback path (`USE_POSTGRES=false`); this only changes what represents live Postgres.
  - **Verified by actually applying the output, not just re-running the diff checker against itself.** First attempt failed **on the very first apply to a real scratch Postgres DB** (`docker exec` into the running `bharat_timescaledb` container, `CREATE DATABASE schema_verify_scratch`, `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f`): `operator class "gin_trgm_ops" does not exist for access method "gin"` — the header only hardcoded `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS timescaledb;`, missing `pg_trgm` (added live 2026-08-03 for `news_sentiment_items`'s trigram indexes, per the `Full-Stack Performance Pass` note above). Fixed by querying `pg_extension` and emitting every installed extension, not a hardcoded one. **Second attempt found a second real issue**: `pgmigrations` (node-pg-migrate's own bookkeeping table) has a legacy `id INTEGER DEFAULT nextval('pgmigrations_id_seq'::regclass)` column — a plain Postgres SEQUENCE default, not the newer `GENERATED ... AS IDENTITY` style this generator detects — and since it doesn't also emit `CREATE SEQUENCE` statements, including this table broke the apply mid-file. Fixed by skipping `pgmigrations` entirely in both `generatePgSchemaFromLive.ts` and `checkSchemaDrift.ts`'s live-side query (a `SKIP_TABLES`/`SKIP_LIVE_TABLES` set in each, kept in sync via a cross-referencing comment) — it self-creates on the first `npm run migrate:up` against any target DB regardless, mirroring `generate_pg_schema.py`'s own `SKIP` set for SQLite-internal bookkeeping tables (`sqlite_sequence`/`sqlite_stat1`/`sqlite_stat4`). **Third attempt applied clean end-to-end**: `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` on the full regenerated file against an empty `public` schema created all 181 tables (182 minus the now-skipped `pgmigrations`) plus every index, and correctly created all 6 Timescale hypertables with their compression/retention policies — confirmed via `information_schema.tables` count matching. `npm run schema:drift` immediately after: **exit 0, "Schema clean."**
  - **Known, deliberate gap, not attempted**: standalone Postgres **functions** (e.g. `ohlcv_adjustment_basis(text)`, added 2026-07-31 per the `ohlcv_adjust.py` note above) are invisible to both this generator (scoped to `pg_class.relkind='r'`, tables only) and to `checkSchemaDrift.ts`'s own comparison (`information_schema.columns` has no function-level view) — confirmed this was ALSO already missing from the file before this fix (the SQLite-based pipeline has no functions concept to generate from either), so this isn't a regression, just an out-of-scope corner of "schema" neither tool covers yet.
  - **9 new tests** in `scripts/__tests__/generatePgSchemaFromLive.test.ts` (pure-function coverage of `buildSchemaFile`: inline vs. composite PRIMARY KEY, UNIQUE emission, multi-extension header, hypertable footer presence/absence, empty-column-set skip) — the most load-bearing one round-trips generated output back through `checkSchemaDrift.ts`'s own `parseSchemaFile`, since the real acceptance criterion for this generator is "does the OTHER tool agree with what this one wrote," not just "does this look like valid SQL." All 21 tests across both schema-drift test files pass; `tsc --noEmit` clean on every touched/new file.
  - **Honest scope note**: ~140 DB-writing files exist; ~52 make genuine external HTTP calls (the rest are internal engines the mandate doesn't cover) — this session's 55 marked files represent near-full coverage of that population as currently understood via a mechanical grep-based inventory, not an exhaustive line-by-line audit of all 144 files, so a fetcher using an unusual HTTP-client import alias could still be missing. Re-run the same grep periodically; this is also why the "Adding a New Data Source" mandate requires a `live_datasource` test at creation time rather than relying on periodic backfill sweeps like this one.
- **60+ AI-signal Telegram alerts in a few minutes, root-caused and fixed (2026-08-06) — two compounding gaps, both from the same 2026-08-05 fix (commit `fcd8557`) that removed a stale-but-accidentally-protective guard without replacing what it was silently providing.** User reported a burst of Telegram "BUY" alerts they didn't trust as genuine.
  - **Gap 1 — the real upstream selectivity (`ai_signal_min_win_prob`) was never actually in the codebase, only in one running deployment's memory.** `signals.ts`'s `getAISignalMinWinProb()` reads an `app_settings` row with code fallback `DEFAULT_AI_SIGNAL_MIN_WIN_PROB`. That constant was still `0.40` in source — the 2026-08-02 session that discovered `0.40` sits exactly on a degenerate calibration plateau (`calibrated_win_probability` collapses to ~0.4064 for ~95% of the universe on most days, per the "AI-signal Ollama gate was a near no-op" note above) fixed it with a one-off `UPDATE app_settings ...` against a single running Postgres instance — never committed to code or a migration. `grep`ping `db.ts`/`db/schema.postgres.sql`/`migrations/` for `ai_signal_min_win_prob` confirmed the row is seeded nowhere. Any environment without that manual UPDATE (this branch's fresh checkout, a new deployment, a DB reset) silently reverts to `0.40`, i.e. the degenerate near-no-op state, with no error anywhere.
  - **Gap 2 — commit `fcd8557` (2026-08-05, "Fix WebSocket signal broadcast...") removed `websocketService.ts`'s dead `confidence >= 85` check on the AI-signal Telegram side effect, correctly diagnosing it as unreachable (the scale it checked against had been replaced by `win_probability × 100`, which peaks ~41), but did not notice the dead check had been *accidentally* acting as a hard Telegram-volume ceiling regardless of how selective the upstream gate actually was.** Once removed, the Telegram send became gated by `source === 'AI' && signalType === 'BUY'` alone — trusting `getAISignalMinWinProb()` entirely, with zero defense-in-depth if that gate ever degenerates (which Gap 1 shows it silently can, on any environment missing the undocumented manual fix). With the win-prob gate back at 0.40, essentially the whole universe reaches the LLM call, and the LLM's own confidence gate (`getAISignalMinConfidence`, default 65) is a poor backstop for volume since — per `signals.ts`'s own comment — "the LLM's self-confidence is uncorrelated with realized outcomes" (LLMs routinely self-report high confidence); a meaningful fraction of BUYs clearing that unrelated 65-floor was enough to produce 60+ Telegram sends in a burst once a batch of AI-signal jobs (concurrency 3) ran through a large queued backlog.
  - **Fix, both layers**: (1) `signals.ts`: raised `DEFAULT_AI_SIGNAL_MIN_WIN_PROB` 0.40 → 0.42 (the value already verified live 2026-08-02 to clear the plateau with margin, narrowing the pass rate to ~14/2264 stocks/day) — codified as the actual code default instead of a fact that only lived in one deployment's `app_settings` table. (2) New `migrations/1786300000000_ai-signal-min-win-prob-seed.sql` seeds the row itself (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO NOTHING` — never clobbers an operator's own override, matching this project's established never-downgrade convention), so a fresh Postgres actually gets the safe value once migrated, not just a code fallback. (3) `websocketService.ts`: added `AI_SIGNAL_TELEGRAM_DAILY_CAP = 40` — a documented, testable, intentional daily ceiling on AI-signal Telegram sends (module-level counter, resets per calendar day, warns once via `console.warn` when tripped rather than logging every suppressed send) — replacing the *accidental* protection the removed `>=85` check used to provide with a *deliberate* one that doesn't depend on a magic number tied to a scale that has already changed once. WebSocket delivery to the frontend (`this.broadcast()`) is untouched by the cap — only the Telegram side effect is bounded. `unifiedSignalBroadcast.ts`'s separate `source: 'UNIFIED'` canonical-picks broadcast was checked and confirmed unaffected (it never sets `source: 'AI'`, so it never touches this cap or the legacy Telegram path at all — its own per-day dedup is a different, already-correct mechanism).
  - **Immediate mitigation available to the user right now, independent of this code fix reaching production**: `UPDATE app_settings SET value='false' WHERE key='telegram_enabled'` (read by `telegramService.getSettings()`) stops all Telegram sends platform-wide as a kill switch, or `UPDATE app_settings SET value='0.42' WHERE key='ai_signal_min_win_prob'` (upsert if missing) applies Gap 1's fix directly without waiting for a deploy.
  - **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; new `websocketServiceAITelegramCap.test.ts` (5 tests: sends under cap, stops exactly at cap even under a 30-alert burst, non-AI sources never consume budget, non-BUY AI signals don't send, cap resets on a new day) and 2 new pinned regression tests in `gateOnQuant.test.ts` (default is above the plateau; the plateau value literally passes at the old 0.40 floor and literally fails at the fixed default — reproduces the bug and proves the fix in one assertion pair). Full suite: 690/718 passed, only the pre-existing network-blocked `mcapiProxy.test.ts` (28, real MoneyControl calls blocked by this sandbox's proxy) failing, unrelated and already documented elsewhere in this file.
  - **Lesson, extending an already-recorded pattern in this file**: a guard discovered to be *dead* is not automatically safe to delete outright — check what side effect it was gating and whether anything else actually bounds that side effect before removing it, especially when (as here) the reason it was dead was itself a moving-target scale that had already broken once. And a fix verified and applied only via a manual one-off DB write, however carefully verified at the time, is not "fixed" in any codebase-durable sense — CLAUDE.md's own `migration_tool_verification_gotcha` lesson (a migration authored but never `npm run migrate:up`'d) has a sibling failure mode here: a manual `UPDATE` applied and verified live, but never captured in a migration or a code default, is invisible to every future environment and reverts silently the moment a fresh DB doesn't happen to carry it.
- **Follow-up quant-review, same incident (2026-08-06): a second, independent contributor found on the CANONICAL ranking table itself — `unified_ranker.py`'s `_classify` "directionless score fallback" (merged the day before, commit `fcd8557`) — flag-gated off by default.** User asked specifically whether the scoring LOGIC (not just the Telegram delivery channel) was correct, given how many stocks appeared to score well "on every parameter," and whether any stale data built under prior wrong logic could be contributing. This is a distinct question from the Telegram-spam fix above (that fix only touches the AI-signal side channel / `unified_signals`; this one touches `unified_recommendations`, the platform's canonical ranking, consumed by `/alpha`, `/trade-cockpit`, `telegramRecommendations.ts`'s digest, and `unifiedSignalBroadcast.ts`'s S_ELITE/A_HIGH WebSocket push).
  - **The mechanism.** `fcd8557` changed `_classify(score, bull, bear)` so that a stock with **zero net screener opinion** (`bull==bear`, including the common `total==0` case — no membership in any of the ~1,700 catalogued screens that day) no longer forces `'Hold'`; instead the blended `unified_score` alone can set Buy/Strong Buy/Sell/Strong Sell past a bar (80/70/20/30) above the ordinary Strong-tier gate (66/34). The stated rationale was real (screeners under-cover the universe, a genuinely strong off-radar name shouldn't be silently dropped) and was an explicit, considered decision from a prior session — but the implementation has a real flaw: **three of the eight blended engines — `screener`, `cs`, `confluence` (as of the same commit, which also switched it to percentile normalization), `technical` — are percentile-RANK normalized** (`_normalize_to_100`), which by construction spreads a *uniform* 0-100 distribution across the scored universe regardless of whether that day's signal is objectively strong. For exactly the stocks this fallback fires on (no screener opinion), `screener`'s weight — the single largest of the eight (0.20-0.40 across `REGIME_WEIGHTS`) — gets redistributed by `_blend()`'s renormalization onto the remaining seven, and `cs+confluence+technical` alone can then represent **over half** of that redistributed weight (e.g. HIGH_VOL: `0.05+0.12+0.24=0.41` of the `0.80` remaining). All three lean on the same underlying momentum/trend signal family (`technical_score` is a raw RSI/MACD composite; confluence's non-screener sum is `trend_alignment_score+volume_score`; `cs_ranker` is a cross-sectional alpha-percentile ranker) — so on any day a cohort of stocks is genuinely "hot," that whole cohort ranks top-percentile on all three simultaneously and clears the 70/80 bar from **shared momentum exposure**, not independent multi-engine agreement. This is not hypothetical: the platform's own 2026-07-30/07-31 quant-strategy audits already measured short-horizon momentum to have **negative** forward alpha on this exact universe (`mom21`: -0.53%/5d, t=-3.21; intraday `vwap_dev`/`rvol`: all negative, t up to -11) — so this fallback was systematically manufacturing broad "high score on every parameter" Buy/Strong Buy classifications by leaning into the platform's own documented *losing* factor, specifically for the one population (no screener confirmation) that also can't be caught by `is_red_flagged()`'s hard veto, since that veto reads the same (necessarily empty, by construction) screener membership list. `intraday_ranker.py` imports the same `_classify` function and was silently exposed to the identical risk, undiscovered until this review.
  - **Fix**: flag-gated the mechanism off by default rather than deleting it outright — its underlying premise is real and was a deliberate prior decision, and this sandbox has no live DB to empirically validate a narrower/safer replacement threshold before shipping one. `is_directionless_fallback_enabled(conn)` (mirrors `ml_calibration.is_edge_adjustment_enabled()`'s established convention exactly: missing/non-`'true'` `app_settings` row = disabled, no migration needed since the safe state IS the default) is read once per `run()` and threaded through every `_classify(..., directionless_fallback=...)` call; `_classify`'s own parameter also defaults to `False` so any other caller (`intraday_ranker.py`) that doesn't pass it gets the safe behavior automatically. Flip on with `UPDATE app_settings SET value='true' WHERE key='directionless_score_fallback_enabled'` once validated against real historical `unified_recommendations` data — specifically, whether directionless-fallback-classified stocks actually outperform, and whether requiring genuine cross-engine diversity (the non-percentile `ml`/`dl`/`breakout` engines, not just `cs+confluence+technical`) closes the gap.
  - **Verification**: existing `TestDirectionlessScoreFallback` tests split into `TestDirectionlessScoreFallbackWhenEnabled` (same assertions, now passing `directionless_fallback=True` explicitly — proves the mechanism still works correctly when opted into) and a new `TestDirectionlessScoreFallbackDisabledByDefault` (5 tests pinning that a stock with zero/tied screener opinion stays `'Hold'` with **no flag argument at all**, regardless of how extreme the blended score is — the actual regression test for this finding) plus `TestIsDirectionlessFallbackEnabled` (4 tests on the flag reader itself, including a query-failure-defaults-closed case). 17 new/updated tests; full `test_unified_ranker*.py` + `test_intraday_ranker_gate.py` + `test_unified_ranker_portfolio_construction.py` + `test_cs_ranker.py` suites (198 tests total) pass with no regressions.
  - **On "old data built under wrong logic"**: this specific mechanism is too recent (<24h old at the time of review) to be an *old-data* problem — it's a fresh logic defect, not stale persisted state. The user's broader question (are there stale model files/persisted scores from before a later correction still feeding current output) is real and worth a dedicated follow-up with live DB access to check `model_registry.trained_at` timestamps against known fix dates (e.g. did `ml_ensemble.py`/`cs_ranker.py` actually retrain under the fixed embargo/CV-leakage code from Findings #18/#19/#20, 2026-07-30, or is the currently-active `.pkl` still pre-fix?) and whether `screener-performance`/`screener-reliability` jobs have actually re-run in production since their 2026-08-04/05 (source, scan_id)-collision and direction-blind-reliability fixes — neither is verifiable from a sandbox with no live Postgres, and both are flagged here rather than guessed at.

Root-caused two of the misses for real rather than flagging them as leads:
  - **CELLO** (Sell called, then +15.69%): traced end-to-end and found a real, correctly-tagged, already-24h-public BULLISH catalyst ("Bain Capital leads talks to buy majority stake in Cello World") sitting unused in `news_sentiment_items` — `technical_signals.news_sentiment_score` was NULL because `runTechnicalSignalScan` computed it *after* `if (signals.length === 0) continue`, so a stock with an unremarkable chart that day (CELLO's RSI was a mundane 63.74) had its news sentiment silently discarded regardless of strength. **Fixed**: a symbol with meaningfully bullish/bearish news (|score| > 0.25, the same bar `scoreSignals`' own modifier uses) now gets a `signalScore=0`/`signals=[]` row persisted purely so the sentiment (and the technical indicators `detectSignals()` computes either way) land in the table — the downstream `signalScore>0`/`>=5` blocks (unified_signals mirror, WS broadcast, `recommendation_log`) all correctly no-op at 0, so this can't manufacture a fake trade signal. Also found and fixed a companion issue while investigating: `news_symbol_link` (a news→symbol index) had been a **one-time 2026-07-31 backfill, never scheduled again** — 3,754+ articles since, including this exact one, had zero linkage rows. Confirmed via full-repo grep it has no live consumers today (so it wasn't the direct cause), but scheduled its idempotent, 29s-measured rebuild (`data_integrity_repair.py --news-link`) as the last step of `ml-daily-ops` anyway, registered in `JOB_REGISTRY`, so a table that looks like a live index doesn't silently stop being one again.
  - **KECL** (zero rows in `unified_recommendations`, ever, despite strong coverage everywhere else — `quant_scores.composite_class='Strong Buy'`, `cs_ranker=84.5`, `confluence=97.8`, `breakout=74.7`): traced live through the *entire* `run()` pipeline (confirmed present in the union of all 6 method-based `_get_*_scores`, confirmed it survives `_restrict_to_tradeable_universe`) down to the exact drop point — `_passes_rl_gate()`, the first check in the per-symbol scoring loop, permanently excludes any symbol whose average realized return over the trailing 90d (`recommendation_log.actual_return_pct`) is negative, **with no minimum-sample floor**. KECL had exactly 2 old `technical_scan` misses from 2026-05 (-5.25% avg). Quantified the blast radius before fixing rather than patching KECL alone: **825 symbols platform-wide are currently excluded by this gate, 352 of them (43%) on fewer than 5 historical outcomes** — thinner than this codebase's own established convention for trusting a hard decision (`screener_performance.py`'s `MIN_SIGNALS_FOR_TIER=5`). Added `MIN_RL_GATE_SAMPLES=5` (same threshold, same rationale) plus a log line on every exclusion (previously completely silent — this whole investigation had to reverse-engineer the mechanism from scratch because nothing recorded why a symbol was missing). Live-verified: re-ran `unified_ranker.py` — KECL now scores `Buy`/`A_HIGH` at 66.4 (bull=19/bear=8), the first real row it has ever had; the run scored 1,889 stocks total (up from 1,548 the prior session), consistent with recovering a meaningful share of the 825. Updated the one pre-existing test that had encoded the old no-floor behavior (excluded on a single -8% outcome) as correct — that was the exact false-negative this fix closes.
  - **Also fixed**: `unifiedSignalBroadcast.ts`'s per-day WebSocket dedup used `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` (server UTC date) while `unified_recommendations.computed_at` is IST-trading-day-scoped — a call straddling a UTC-midnight boundary could reset the guard mid-IST-day (re-broadcast) or fail to reset for a new IST day. Fixed by deriving the dedup key from the data itself (`getLatestUnifiedRecommendationsDay()`, the same subquery `fetchBroadcastablePicks()` already runs) instead of an independently-computed wall-clock date — eliminates the ambiguity structurally. The `day` param stays for direct test injection (every existing test already used it that way; only the real scheduled caller, which passes no override, is affected).

- **Closed the remaining part of the model-registry stale-baseline problem (2026-08-06): `cs_ranker.py`/`confluence_ml_engine.py` wired to the staleness-override safety valve.** A concurrent session working in the same checkout generalized `ml_ensemble.py`'s existing local staleness-override mechanism (built 2026-07-28 for a leak-inflated baseline that became permanently unbeatable) into a shared `model_promotion.py` (`rejections_since`/`staleness_override_applies`, both/-required gate: baseline unbeaten ≥7 days AND ≥10 rejected retrains). `ml_ensemble.py` itself was refactored onto it, but `cs_ranker.py`/`confluence_ml_engine.py` still only imported the base `clears_promotion_bar` gate with no equivalent safety valve — exactly why their deadlock (both active baselines predate the 2026-07-30 CV-leakage fix, every honest post-fix retrain rejected against a now-unbeatable pre-fix score) had no way to self-heal. Wired both in, mirroring `ml_ensemble.py`'s exact pattern (`_active_cs_baseline_row()`/baseline id+trained_at lookup, staleness check only when the normal bar isn't cleared, `"STALENESS OVERRIDE"` recorded in `model_registry.notes` so a promoted-via-override row is distinguishable from a genuine one — `confluence_ml_engine.py` had no `notes` column in its INSERTs at all before this).
  - **Caught two real bugs found only by actually running the existing test suite, not by review**: both engines' promotion-gate test fixtures (`_FakeConn`/`_FakeRegistryConn`) faked their baseline-lookup query by pattern-matching the literal SQL string `"SELECT cv_roc_auc"` — the new 3-column query (`SELECT id, cv_roc_auc, trained_at`) no longer contains that substring, so both fixtures silently returned no baseline, mis-promoting every candidate as `"bootstrap (no active baseline)"` regardless of its actual rho/AUC (3 previously-passing tests started failing). Fixed both fixtures to answer the new query shape correctly.
  - **Live-verified, not assumed, that the fix doesn't need to (and shouldn't) force anything right now**: queried the real current baseline age/rejection-count for all three models — `ensemble` age=11.0d/rejections=9, `cs_ranker` age=7.0d/rejections=4, `confluence_ml` age=7.1d/rejections=7. None currently clear both thresholds, which is correct (the valve requires *sustained* failure, not premature promotion) — `ensemble` is one rejected weekly retrain away from self-healing; `cs_ranker`/`confluence_ml` now have a working path to recovery on their existing daily retrain cadence instead of staying stuck indefinitely. Deliberately did not force a live 60-90min retrain just to watch a counter tick over — the wiring itself is what needed fixing and is verified correct via live DB state + 12 new unit tests (6 per engine: fires on both-thresholds-met, does not fire on young-baseline-many-rejections, does not fire on stale-baseline-few-rejections).

No conflicts occurred.

Reproduced directly: `cs_ranker.py`/`confluence_ml_engine.py` both raised `ImportError` on their first line, and `pytest` couldn't even collect their promotion-gate test files — meaning `cs_ranker.py --score` (every ~15 min) and `--train --score` (weekly) plus `confluence_ml_engine.py`'s training job had been failing on every single invocation since `3963c55` landed, and that commit's own claimed test results ("19/19 + 13/13 pass") were not reproducible against what was actually on `main`.

One fix (mine, `44ea01c`, on a since-superseded branch) added the four missing names to `model_promotion.py` only, deliberately leaving `ml_ensemble.py`'s local duplicate untouched under time pressure. A second, independent fix (`f015387`, pushed directly to `main` minutes later) did the more complete job: added the same four names **and** completed the refactor the lost commit was supposed to do — extracting `ml_ensemble.py`'s inline `_rejections_since`/staleness logic into the shared functions and deleting the local duplicate (46 lines removed from `ml_ensemble.py`, 68 added to `model_promotion.py`). Verified independently before treating it as authoritative (not trusted on the commit message's word alone): fresh worktree at `f015387`, `import cs_ranker; import confluence_ml_engine; import ml_ensemble` all clean, and `test_cs_ranker.py`/`test_cs_ranker_embargo.py`/`test_cs_ranker_promotion_gate.py`/`test_confluence_ml_engine.py`/`test_ml_ensemble.py` together — 86/86 pass. `f015387` is the version that's live; the redundant, less-complete `44ea01c` fix was discarded (never merged) once this was confirmed, rather than layering a second, inferior implementation of the same four functions on top.
  - **Lesson, a sharper instance of an already-recorded pattern**: this file already warns that "declaring/writing a migration is not the same as applying it" (the `node-pg-migrate`/Python-package incidents) — this is the same failure shape one layer up the stack: *committing* code that assumes a dependency exists is not the same as that dependency actually existing in the same push, even when both changes were genuinely made in good faith in the same working session. Under concurrent multi-session editing of a shared trunk, a commit message's own confident claim about what "a concurrent session" already did elsewhere is not verified fact — it's a report from a different process that can be, and here was, wrong, twice (once by the original committer, once by my own initial fix, which assumed the file it based its threshold constants and function signatures on was the final state rather than checking whether a better version might land before it did). The only way to know a cross-file dependency added in one commit is satisfied is to actually import/run the code from the commit that depends on it, against the branch it was actually pushed to, checked again immediately before acting on the result — not to trust the authoring session's own narrative of what else landed, and not to trust your own prior check without a fresh `git fetch` if any time has passed on a fast-moving shared branch.

Live-verified this mechanism firing correctly and confirmed what it's reacting to: **the deployed model's live AUC is 0.4615 over 671,257 resolved rows** (an enormous, statistically decisive sample — this is not noise) despite a respectable held-out `test_auc` of 0.641, the exact CV/test-doesn't-survive-deployment gap this platform has hit repeatedly elsewhere (`win_probability` outside BEAR, the LSTM, etc.).

Extracted `_persist_live_edge_status()`, called on both the promote AND reject branches now, carefully preserving the still-deployed model's own `trained_at`/`cv_auc`/`test_auc`/etc. on the reject path rather than overwriting them with the rejected candidate's numbers. Added `parseLiveEdgeProven()` (exported, pure, `=== false` not falsy — a model that simply hasn't accumulated enough resolved rows yet is unaffected) and wired it in: when `live_edge_proven` is explicitly `false`, `ml_win_probability` is dropped for every stock, which makes the *already-existing* fallback-to-rule-based-`edge_score` ranking kick in automatically — the identical fallback this page already uses before any model has ever trained, now also covering "or once we've proven it doesn't help." - **`live_screener_ml_ranker.py`'s zero live edge (0.4615 AUC/671k rows, gated off earlier the same day — see the note above) was root-caused: the old 0.641 `test_auc` was itself a data leak, not real skill that failed to survive deployment (2026-08-07).** `_build_matrix()` grouped `live_screener_outcomes`/`live_screener_appearances` by `(appeared_at, symbol)` — i.e. by calendar DAY. A filter match persists across many consecutive ~15-min scans in one session (live-confirmed **median 15 distinct runs per matching (date, symbol) pair**, `change_per` spread up to ~25pp within a single day), so training rows were built from a whole day's worth of scans averaged/unioned together — `change_per`/`volume` mean across every run that day, every filter the symbol EVER matched that day OR'd into one flag. `score()` (the real live path, every 15 min) only ever sees ONE run's snapshot (`WHERE run_id = ?`). A model trained on a day-smoothed, day-unioned view scored on a raw single-snapshot view is a textbook train/serve skew — this platform's own most-recurring bug class. Fixed by grouping `_build_matrix()` by `(run_id, symbol)` instead, so every training row is shaped exactly like a real scoring call (verified with a test that builds a training matrix for one run and independently reproduces `score()`'s own live-matrix logic for an identical appearances frame, asserting byte-identical feature values).
  - **Retraining with the fix, live against production, is the actual finding, not just a mechanical correctness fix**: row count jumped 20,554 → 308,747 (the ~15× factor the median-runs-per-day measurement predicted), and honest `test_auc` **collapsed from 0.641 to 0.4857 — below random** (`cv_auc` 0.5151, barely above random). Per-filter win-rate lift is ±4pp at best on a 44.9% base rate (`stockPEBelow5` -4.3pp, `orb5minLow` +3.9pp — noise), and both continuous features are near-zero correlated with the honest label (`change_per` r=-0.018, `log_volume` r=+0.010). **This is not a bug in the fix — it's this platform's own already-documented finding (intraday momentum/breakout carries negative-to-negligible cross-sectional IC, per the 2026-07-30/31 audits) landing on a fourth, previously-unverified model.** The old 0.641 number was an artifact of the leak: averaging `change_per` across a whole day (including runs late in the session) entangles the feature with the same day's own `return_intraday` label, which is itself largely determined by late-session price action — removing that entanglement removes the apparent skill along with it.
  - The (already-correct) promotion gate worked exactly as designed on this honest retrain: `test_auc=0.4857` didn't clear even the non-strict margin against the deployed baseline's 0.6408, so the candidate was rejected and the active model on disk is unchanged; `_persist_live_edge_status()` still correctly refreshed `app_settings` on the reject, sourced from the still-deployed baseline. The same-day frontend gating fix (hide `ml_win_probability` when `live_edge_proven=false`) is now *better justified*, not superseded — it isn't hiding a model that just needs more live data, it's hiding a model built on a feature family independently shown, four separate times now, to carry no real same-day-intraday edge.
  - **Swept the two sibling engines for the identical "self-correction computed but never reaches a consumer" bug class** (`movement_predictor.py`, `breakout_classifier.py`) — neither has one: both use the simpler shared `model_promotion.py` held-out-AUC-only gate with no live-vs-realized health check at all, so there was nothing to disconnect. `live_screener_ml_ranker.py` was uniquely sophisticated (the only one of the four with a live-AUC mechanism) and uniquely exposed.
  - **What this does NOT do**: it does not make the model good. This feature family (raw filter matches + single-snapshot `change_per`/`log_volume`) appears to carry no real same-day-intraday predictive power once honestly measured. A genuinely different feature set (minutes-since-open, cross-sectional rank among today's other live matches so far, sector/index context) would be a real, separate modeling project — deliberately not attempted here.
  - **Verification**: 5 new tests in `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_train_serve_skew.py`, negative-controlled (4 of 5 confirmed failing with `KeyError: 'run_id'` against the reverted pre-fix code; the 5th is a single-run-slice equivalence check that trivially passes either way). Existing CV/live-edge-status suites (10 tests) updated (`run_id` added to both synthetic-frame builders) and still pass. Full suite re-run clean after the change.
- **Same-day follow-up: the identical train/serve skew found in the sibling `live_screener_optimizer.py` — user-facing, not just an internal model this time (2026-08-07).** Its own docstring already said "Mirrors `live_screener_optimizer.py`'s pivot" — and it turned out to be the exact same `(appeared_at, symbol)` day-level grouping bug, one file over. Worse blast radius: its output is a clickable "AI Recommendation" button in `LiveMarketScreener.tsx` (`applyCombo`) carrying a specific numeric win-rate claim, and clicking it sends every filter in the combo to `fetchLiveMarketScreener()` as one **simultaneous-AND** query to NiftyTrader — it only returns stocks matching ALL of them **at the same moment**, in the current scan. The reported win_rate/avg_return had been computed over "matched filter X at some point today AND filter Y at some other point today," a materially easier and different condition than what "Apply" actually retrieves. Fixed the same way: extracted `_build_run_matrix()` (mirroring `_build_matrix()`) grouping by `(run_id, symbol)` instead of `(appeared_at, symbol)`, and added the missing `a.run_id` join in `optimize_combinations()`'s SQL (it previously read `live_screener_outcomes` alone, which has no `run_id` column).
  - **Live-verified the real magnitude, before vs after, on production data**: pre-fix, the top intraday "AI Recommendation" was `lowerHighLowerLow + outsideDay + orb5minHigh` at an **85.96% win rate** (n=114). Post-fix (honest, run-level), that exact combination doesn't even appear near the top anymore — the true top intraday combo is a completely different filter set (`stockPEBelow5 + todayNR7 + yesterdayGapDown`) at **52.76%** win rate with **760** samples (6.7× more data). The headline 85.96% number was a pure day-level-union artifact, not a real, reproducible edge — the same mechanism as the sibling ML ranker fix, this time landing as a wrong number shown directly to a user next to a button claiming to reproduce it.
  - **A real gap in how the regression tests were first written, caught by the negative control**: an earlier draft of the test suite reimplemented the `(run_id, symbol)` grouping logic locally inside the test file instead of calling the real source function — 4 of 5 tests silently passed against the *unfixed* source, meaning they'd never actually have caught a regression. Rewritten to call the real, newly-extracted `lso._build_run_matrix()` directly; re-run negative control confirmed all 5 now fail correctly against the reverted pre-fix code. **Standing lesson: a test that reimplements the logic under test locally instead of calling the real function can look identical to a real regression test while providing none of its protection — always negative-control (revert the source, confirm the test fails) before trusting a freshly-written test suite, not just when the bug itself is subtle.**
  - **Verification**: 5 tests in `test_live_screener_optimizer_run_level_combos.py`. `app_settings.live_screener_optimal_combinations`/`_intraday` left with the honest post-fix values as the final production state (not the diagnostic pre-fix comparison run used to measure the before/after magnitude).
- **Frontend review + live-bug fixes (2026-08-07).** User asked for a frontend review (data correctness, feature function, redesign options, gaps). Drove the real pm2-served app (not a synthetic dev server) via a live browser rather than reading component code first — found the actual default experience doesn't match this file's own documentation (see the corrected "Frontend Tabs / Navigation" section above) before finding anything else. 4 real, live bugs found and fixed, all `tsc --noEmit`-clean and re-verified against the running server:
  - **`syncNSEStocks` 401-error spam on every single page load, for every visitor, on every route.** `App.tsx`'s top-level `useEffect(() => syncNSEStocksMutation.mutate(), [])` unconditionally called this mutation on mount — but it's `adminProcedure`-gated (2026-07-23 auth layer) and non-admin callers get a 401. Also purely duplicative: NSE stock sync already runs on its own scheduled `nse-sync-weekly` job. Removed the client-side trigger entirely.
  - **Uncontrolled full-universe AI-signal generation fan-out on every dashboard mount — the more expensive bug.** Both `DashboardPage.tsx` (v1) and `V3Dashboard.tsx` (the actual default) had `useEffect(() => { if (aiSignals.length===0 && stocks.length>0 && !isGenerating) handleGenerateSignals(); }, [stocks.length])` — `aiSignals` is a plain `useState([])` that's always empty on a fresh mount, so this fired on essentially every page load, enqueueing `enqueueSignals` (a `publicProcedure`) across the *entire* live stock universe. `enqueueAISignals()`'s per-day BullMQ `jobId` dedup limits *duplicate* damage, but every stock not yet analyzed *today* genuinely gets queued into the LLM pipeline — meaning any anonymous visitor could mass-trigger new Ollama/Gemini calls simply by loading the page, completely bypassing the quant-gating this repo built specifically to keep the AI-signal path low-volume (see [[llm_demotion_and_retrain_2026_07_12]], [[ai_signal_gate]], the 2026-08-06 Telegram-spam session). Dates to `e470c185` (2026-05-23), predating essentially the entire AI-signal-gating history. Removed both auto-trigger effects; the existing manual "Generate Signals" button in each file is untouched and still works.
  - **`MarketMoodGauge.tsx` (added 2026-08-06, see the urls.txt-4th-pass note above) crashed the whole v1 dashboard tab — intermittently, which delayed finding it.** Reproduced live: "Objects are not valid as a React child (found: [object Date])", caught by `TabErrorBoundary` and shown as "Service temporarily unavailable" — not an actual outage. Root cause: `macro_asset_prices.date` is a genuine Postgres `DATE` column (a TimescaleDB hypertable partition column, so it can't be TEXT); `pgClient.ts` already had custom type parsers for INT8/TIMESTAMP/NUMERIC (see that file's own history) but **none for DATE** (OID 1082), so node-postgres's default parser returned a real JS `Date` object, which this app's `superjson` tRPC transformer faithfully round-trips to the client as a `Date` instance — and the component rendered `{date}` directly in JSX. It was intermittent specifically because `fetchWithCache`'s Redis-backed cache implicitly `JSON.stringify`s cached values (collapsing a Date to its ISO string as a side effect), so a **cache hit** silently masked the bug while a **cache miss** (first load after a 900s TTL expiry, or a cold cache) crashed. Fixed at both layers, matching this exact class of prior fix: added `types.setTypeParser(types.builtins.DATE, (val) => val)` to `pgClient.ts` (keep DATE as the raw `YYYY-MM-DD` wire string, matching how every other date column in this schema already behaves, rather than risk a timezone-shift by parsing-then-reformatting per callsite) — a systemic fix protecting every current/future consumer of any native-DATE column, not just this one — plus a defensive `date instanceof Date ? ... : String(date ?? '—')` guard in the component itself. `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy (backend `.ts` edits don't hot-reload — see [[pm2_restart_required]]); confirmed no crash across multiple fresh-tab reloads post-restart.
  - **Nav-consolidation gap**: the 2026-08-04 UX session restructured `AppShell.tsx`'s (v1) flat 14-item "Intelligence" group into "Top Picks / Alternative Screens / Signal Tools", with a header comment in `V2AppShell.tsx` claiming the same restructuring was mirrored there — it wasn't; the array below the comment still had the old flat shape. Since `V2AppShell` backs the actual default (v2/v3), the fix never reached most users. Ported the real restructuring into `V2AppShell.tsx`, reconciling the two files' item lists (V2-only items — Live Screener, EOD Screener, Intraday — kept; v1-only items missing from V2 — Money Flow, Today's Picks, Export Portfolio — added, since all three already have working routes in the shared `<Routes>` tree and were simply unreachable via V2's nav before this).

Since V2AppShell backs the default experience, the default mobile experience is currently unusable. **Not fixed this session** — building a real off-canvas/hamburger mobile nav pattern from scratch (no existing responsive-nav infrastructure to extend) is a genuine scoped feature, not a one-line fix; flagged as the top open item.
  - **Minor, not fixed**: `TickerTapeWidget` (`TradingViewWidgets.tsx`, v1-only) throws a console-only `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'querySelector')` under React 18 StrictMode's dev-mode mount→unmount→remount cycle — its effect cleanup clears the injected TradingView `<script>`'s container out from under the external script mid-load. Dev-mode-only (StrictMode doesn't double-invoke in production builds) and non-fatal (throws outside React's render/commit phase, so `TabErrorBoundary` never sees it; the rest of the page stays functional) — cosmetic console noise, not a user-facing bug.
