Headline items at the time: build V2AppShell mobile responsiveness (fixed same session, see below); reconcile the fact that 3 competing "next-gen" dashboards (v3 terminal, v4 MarketCommandCenter, v5 workbench) now coexist with no stated deprecation path or migration signal to users — **deliberately not touched**, since picking one as canonical and redirecting/removing the others is a real product decision, not a bug fix, and doing it unilaterally would be a large, hard-to-reverse restructuring; the 2026-07-23 in-place-rebuild docs' framing of v4 as "the direction" is now stale given v3/v5 didn't exist when they were written.
  - **Same-day follow-up ("go ahead and fix all"): closed both items flagged as "not fixed this session" above.** (1) **`V2AppShell.tsx` mobile responsiveness** — ported v1's proven hamburger + off-canvas-drawer + backdrop pattern (`AppShell.tsx` already had `hidden md:flex` + a working mobile drawer; V2AppShell had neither), adapted to V2's terminal visual style rather than pulled in framer-motion (which this file didn't already use) — plain CSS transitions instead. Extracted the sidebar body into a shared `renderSidebarBody(onNavigate)` render function so desktop and mobile render identical content from one source, with the mobile copy's nav clicks additionally closing the drawer. Also narrowed the floating toast's fixed `w-80` to `w-full` below `sm:` (was borderline-safe at 375px, would've overflowed a 320px device). Live-verified at 375px: sidebar correctly `display:none`, hamburger opens a 256px drawer, clicking a nav item both navigates and closes the drawer, clicking the backdrop closes it, and at 1280px the drawer machinery is inert and the normal always-on sidebar renders exactly as before. (2) **TradingView widget StrictMode race** — the same "script injected into a container, cleanup immediately clears the container" pattern (vulnerable to React 18 dev-mode's synchronous effect→cleanup→effect double-invoke ripping a still-loading external script's container out from under it) existed identically in *5* widgets in `TradingViewWidgets.tsx`, not just `TickerTapeWidget` — `TechnicalAnalysisWidget`, `EconomicCalendarWidget`, and `MarketOverviewWidget` share the exact same shape; `AdvancedChartWidget` has a materially different (shared single `tv.js` + `TradingView.widget()` constructor) structure with its own version of the same race (a stale `onload` firing after a later effect has already reassigned the container's `id`, causing `TradingView.widget()` to target an id no longer in the DOM). Fixed all 5 with a `cancelled` flag: the 4 script-injecting widgets defer actual injection past a `setTimeout(0)` and bail if `cancelled` was set by an intervening cleanup (so the throwaway StrictMode first-pass never touches the DOM at all, only the surviving second pass does); `AdvancedChartWidget` checks the same flag inside `initWidget()` before calling the TradingView constructor, covering both the synchronous-if-already-loaded and the async-onload paths. Confirmed clean console across 3 independent fresh page loads of v1's dashboard (where `TickerTapeWidget` lives) post-fix — dev-mode-only issue, so this doesn't affect production builds, but removes real console noise on every dev session.
  - **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean after every edit (checked incrementally, not just at the end); every interactive claim (drawer open/close, nav-click auto-close, backdrop-click-close, desktop unaffected) re-verified live against the running pm2 server, not assumed from reading the diff. One environment gotcha hit while verifying: this session's browser-automation tool's rapid same-script DOM re-queries can read stale pre-render state (a `.click()` followed immediately by a state check *in the same eval call* sometimes observed the DOM before React's setState had flushed) — splitting "act" and "verify" into separate tool calls (letting the natural round-trip act as a yield point) resolved it consistently; don't trust a same-call before/after DOM diff as proof either way without that separation.
- **Two new free corporate-action sources integrated end-to-end — fetchers, an accuracy application, and frontend (2026-08-07).** Follow-up to the same-day urls.txt open-source sourcing pass (a standalone artifact, not itself a code change): user asked to actually integrate the two new finds from that pass into the codebase and use them wherever they'd improve accuracy, not just document them.
  - **`mc_corporate_actions_fetcher.py`** (new) → `stock_corporate_action_history`. MoneyControl's `stock/corporate-action?section=all` turned out to return dividends+bonus+splits+rights+board-meetings in ONE call — a real improvement over the originally-scoped 6-separate-calls plan, found by live-testing the endpoint before writing the fetcher. Live-verified field shapes (RELIANCE `scId=RI`, NESTLEIND `scId=NI` for the split case): dates are a genuine MIX of ISO and "DD Mon, YYYY" within the SAME payload (bonus's own `announcement_date` is display-format while its `record_date` is ISO) — reused `data_integrity_repair.parse_loose_date()` rather than writing a new date parser, per this project's own "grep before reinventing" precedent. `record_date` column prefers the true ex-date field (`exbonus_date`/`exsplit_date`/`exrights_date`) over MC's own `record_date`, since the ex-date is what the bhavcopy price actually moves on, not the settlement record date. Ratio-factor convention deliberately excludes rights issues (`ratio_factor` stays NULL) — a rights adjustment is price-dependent (theoretical ex-rights-price formula), not a clean multiplicative ratio the way a split/bonus is, and storing a fabricated one would silently corrupt any consumer that trusts the column the way it trusts bonus/split factors. Weekly (dividends/bonus/splits are declared a handful of times a year per stock — a daily 2000+-stock crawl would be pure load for no new data), scheduled in `trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts` right after `financial_ratios_fetcher.py`.
  - **`investsights_corporate_actions_fetcher.py`** (new) → `nse_filed_corporate_actions`. Market-wide, ONE call (`days_back`/`days_ahead`), `"source":"nse_filings"` — every row links to a real `nsearchives.nseindia.com/corporate/*.pdf` filing, not a third party's derived guess. PK is `source_url` itself (already globally unique by construction, no fabricated key needed). Daily and cheap, added to the existing InvestSights `Promise.allSettled` cluster in `queues.ts` (same block as `investsights_sector_intel_fetcher.py`/`investsights_concall_fetcher.py`, 2026-08-06) — the completeness cross-check for the weekly MC per-stock crawl, so it stays fresher than the thing it's checking.
  - **The accuracy application — `ohlcv_adjust.cross_validate_with_mc_actions()`.** Deliberately additive to the existing price-ratio heuristic (`detect_adjustment_events()`), never overrides it — three outcomes per authoritative bonus/split event: *confirmed* (an existing heuristic factor within `RATIO_TOL` sits within a ±5-day window — no write), *filled* (no heuristic factor exists anywhere in the window — a genuine gap, safe to insert with `source='mc_corporate_action'`), *disagreement* (a heuristic factor DOES exist nearby but the value doesn't match — **never auto-resolved either way**, since overwriting risks double-adjusting a bar the heuristic's own factor was already correctly applied to; reported only, matching this module's own stated "report, don't guess" philosophy). New `--cross-validate` CLI flag alongside the existing `--report`/`--persist`.
  - **Live-verified real result on production, not hypothetical**: found 3 confirmed + **6 genuinely NEW factors filled** — RELIANCE bonus issues from 1997/2009/2017 and NESTLEIND bonus issues from 1989/1993/1996, none of which the bhavcopy heuristic could ever find (`nse_universe_history` only starts 2021). Zero disagreements. These 6 factors are now in `ohlcv_adjustment_factors` with `source='mc_corporate_action'`, immediately usable by `backtester.load_bhavcopy_adjusted()` for any backtest trading a long historical window on these names.
  - **Also fixed while wiring this in: `ohlcv_adjust.py` itself had NO scheduled job at all before this session** — its own docstring only ever documented manual `--report`/`--persist` invocation, meaning `ohlcv_adjustment_factors` was frozen at whatever a one-off manual run last left it, indefinitely. - **Regime-calculation audit (2026-08-09): the HMM regime detector had a real, live labeling defect, fixed; then all regime-consuming models were retrained, surfacing a real Python-interpreter/sklearn-version mismatch.**
  - **The bug**: `regime_detector.py`'s 5-state Gaussian HMM (`covariance_type="full"`, 8 features, ~1200 training rows — overparameterized for the data volume) had overfit two states into a near-duplicate pair connected by a literal **0.0 self-transition probability**, forcing the Viterbi path to alternate between them every single trading day regardless of actual market behavior. Live-verified against real NIFTY50 OHLCV: 2026-07-27→08-07 (11 straight sessions, NIFTY actually up ~2.4% with sub-1.6% daily moves, VIX≈12 — a calm grind-up) was labeled BEAR/HIGH_VOL alternating at 73–99% confidence. This degenerate pair dominated 410 of 686 days (60%) of the model's entire history. **Fixed** with a sticky Dirichlet transition prior (`STICKY_PRIOR_WEIGHT=15`) plus a hard promotion-gate floor (`MIN_SELF_TRANSITION=0.5`, expected regime duration ≥2 days) that rejects any future retrain with a degenerate state — a held-out-likelihood comparison alone can't catch this (the broken model scored *better* on a holdout window contaminated by the exact same overfit alternation it had memorized), so promotion required an explicit `--force` override justified by external ground truth, not the model's own metric. Retrained, backfilled `market_regimes` for the full history (2023-11-08→2026-08-07, all 681 trading days, back up saved first), republished `current_nifty_regime`, propagated the corrected label into the denormalized `technical_signals.nifty_regime` column (44,669 of 62,616 rows were stale), and re-ran `unified_ranker.py` live.
  - **Secondary labeling fix**: `_assign_state_labels`'s SIDEWAYS/HIGH_VOL assignment was purely return-rank-based with no volatility check (unlike the existing BEAR/CRASH vol-based swap) — a real retrain had assigned "SIDEWAYS" to a state with *higher* vol (11.4%) than "HIGH_VOL" (8.5%), exactly backwards. Extended the same swap logic to this pair. Known remaining limitation, not fixed: `BULL`/`HIGH_VOL` naming still doesn't verify volatility ordering against every other state (in the corrected model, "BULL" has the highest vol of all 5 states, 22.8%) — only the two swaps (BEAR↔CRASH, SIDEWAYS↔HIGH_VOL) are vol-checked, not a global "is this state actually the most volatile" check. See [[regime_hmm_degenerate_states_2026_08_09]].
  - **Retrained every model that consumes `nifty_regime`/`market_regimes.regime` as a training feature**, found via `grep -rl "nifty_regime\|market_regimes" *.py`: `ml_ensemble.py` (promoted, cv_auc 0.7664 beat 0.7609+margin), `cs_ranker.py`, `exit_policy.py`, `online_learner.py`, `ml_calibration.py` (a calibration recompute, not a champion/challenger model).
  - **`ml_ensemble.py` retrain first tripped the already-documented [[weekly_jobs_and_closed_day_scheduling_2026_07_12]] gotcha** (run training from repo root, not `src/server` — `MODELS_DIR = os.path.join(os.getcwd(), ...)` is cwd-relative, so running from the wrong directory silently writes to a doubled `src/server/src/server/ml_models/` path). The mis-saved model had already been registered in `model_registry` as `is_active=1` before the mistake was caught (via the tell-tale doubled path in the training log's own "Saved to ..." line) — deactivated that row, deleted the junk directory, retrained correctly. **Made this whole class of bug structurally impossible instead of just re-documenting "remember to cd to root"**: found the identical `os.getcwd()`-relative pattern in 3 more files (`ml_ensemble.py`, `ml_signal_scorer.py`, `online_learner.py` — `exit_policy.py` had it too, fixed as part of its own rewrite below) and switched all of them to `os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))`, matching `cs_ranker.py`'s already-correct script-relative pattern — this eliminates the landmine regardless of invocation cwd, rather than relying on every future operator remembering the right directory.
  - **`exit_policy.py` had two real, independent, previously-undiscovered bugs, found only because retraining it for real triggered both**: (1) `horizon_days = int(...max())` took the single largest horizon across the WHOLE dataset (30 days, only 0.8% of rows) as the train/test embargo size — since the 80th-percentile training cutoff already sits near the data's own max date, adding 30 more days pushed the embargo past every remaining date, leaving **zero** test rows on every single run with any horizon=30 data present (not a one-off: structural, reproducible). Printed "Holdout MAE... nan% nan%" with no error. Fixed by using the median horizon instead (robust to a small minority of long-horizon outliers). (2) `exit_policy.py` had **no promotion gate at all** — the only one of this codebase's model-training scripts to still be missing one after the 2026-07-28/08-06 Finding #17 sweeps (which covered `ml_ensemble.py`, `cs_ranker.py`, `confluence_ml_engine.py`, `movement_predictor.py`, `dl_engine.py`, `live_screener_ml_ranker.py` — apparently this file was simply missed by all of them, despite computing money-affecting stop-loss/target levels). `train_from_df()` unconditionally overwrote the live pickle every run, including with the NaN-holdout result from bug (1) — and since it has no backup mechanism, the pre-existing model was already silently gone by the time this was discovered (unrecoverable — not git-tracked). Added a full champion/challenger gate reusing `model_promotion.decide_promotion_with_nan_guard` (negating both MFE-MAE and MAE-MAE, since lower is better for this regressor pair, unlike every other model's higher-is-better metric), requiring BOTH sub-models to improve since they're one combined pickle file. Bootstrap-promoted cleanly after the fix (MFE MAE=5.55%, MAE MAE=2.53%, holdout n=24,074 — a real, non-empty holdout for the first time).
  - **`cs_ranker.py`'s held-out metric itself was measuring the wrong thing, found by tracing why its held-out set was so imbalanced**: per-date signal counts range from 1 to 807 (e.g. one date had 2 signals, another 807), and the existing single pooled `spearmanr()` call over all held-out rows meant 96% of one real held-out set's rows (2,643 of 2,759) came from just 3 dense dates — the reported "rho" mostly measured rank quality on those 3 days, not a representative daily average. Standard cross-sectional Information Coefficient practice computes correlation per period and averages, not pooled-then-computed. Fixed with `_date_balanced_weights()` (inverse-frequency `sample_weight` so every trading day contributes equally to the training loss, matching how `score_batch()` actually re-ranks each day independently at inference time) and `_mean_daily_ic()` (mean of per-date Spearman correlations, not one pooled Spearman). Retrained: held-out IC went from a pooled 0.0638 (the stale baseline) to a mean-daily 0.1388 — cleared the 0.10 acceptance bar for the first time in this model's history. **Documented, not silently glossed over**: this makes the very first post-fix rho **not directly comparable** to every prior pooled-methodology rho in `model_registry` — an apples-to-oranges baseline comparison the gate has no way to detect, flagged in `_register_cs_model()`'s docstring so a future reader doesn't misread the jump as pure signal.
  - **`online_learner.py` surfaced a real, separate, pre-existing Python-interpreter/sklearn-version mismatch, investigated to root cause on user request.** It printed `Could not load SGD state (No module named '_loss') — reinitialising` — its own try/except fallback handled this gracefully (non-fatal, matches the pattern this file already uses), but silently lost whatever incremental state `online_sgd.pkl` had accumulated. **Root cause**: `.env`'s `PYTHON_PATH=backend-python\venv\Scripts\python.exe` is what production (BullMQ `runPython()`) actually uses for every scheduled training job, and that venv has **sklearn 1.8.0** — but this entire session's retrains (including this one) were run via the bare system `python`, which resolves to a completely different install at **sklearn 1.9.0**. Unpickling a `SGDClassifier(loss='log_loss')` state saved by 1.8.0 under 1.9.0 breaks on an internal Cython `_loss` submodule that was restructured between those releases — a real, asymmetric backward-compat gap in scikit-learn itself, not a bug in this codebase. **Confirmed via `model_registry` this was NOT introduced by this session**: the identical reinit message had already fired once earlier the same day (05:38 IST, before this session started) — some other, concurrent session (this repo has heavy documented concurrent multi-session activity) must have also run a script with the wrong interpreter at some point. **Checked for real damage rather than assuming any**: loaded all 4 files this session retrained (`ensemble.pkl`, `cs_ranker.pkl`, `exit_policy.pkl`, `online_sgd.pkl` — all written under 1.9.0) directly with production's real 1.8.0 interpreter — all four load fine (version-mismatch warnings only, no errors; the break only goes one direction, old-file-under-new-library, not the reverse) — **nothing currently live is broken**. Deliberately did **not** force a redo of the promoted models under the correct interpreter: each one's own promotion gate would very likely reject a near-identical redo (same data, same `random_state`) as "no improvement over what I just registered minutes ago," so redoing it would cost time without changing anything meaningful given the compatibility is already verified. **This is a distinct variant of the already-documented [[migration_tool_verification_gotcha]]/two-venvs-can-drift class of bug** (see the 2026-08-04 `load_bearing_constraints_and_coverage_gate` note) — not a *missing package* drifting between `.venv` and `backend-python/venv` this time, but a *version* mismatch between the two, where only `backend-python/venv` is what `PYTHON_PATH` designates for real scheduled runs. **Any future session invoking a Python training/scoring script in this repo directly (not via `queues.ts`/`runPython()`) must use `backend-python/venv/Scripts/python.exe`, never the bare `python` on PATH** — using the wrong interpreter doesn't just risk missing packages (the already-documented failure mode), it can also silently produce artifacts in a different library version than production will ever load them with. See [[regime_hmm_degenerate_states_2026_08_09]] and [[interpreter_venv_mismatch_2026_08_09]] for full detail.
  - **Verification**: 25 new/updated tests across `test_regime_detector.py` (+8, incl. negative-controlled degenerate-transition-matrix and HIGH_VOL/SIDEWAYS-swap cases), `test_exit_policy.py` (+6, incl. a negative control proving the fixture genuinely reproduces the old bug under the old max()-based embargo), `test_cs_ranker.py` (+4, date-balanced-weight and mean-daily-IC behavior). Full suite re-run clean after every step, not just at the end. All 5 model retrains + the full historical `market_regimes` backfill were live-run against real production Postgres, not simulated.
- **Follow-on chapter (2026-08-10), same audit thread: closed two component-score problems the counterfactual re-blend above surfaced (`ml` clustering, `dl` saturation), fixed a real bug in the shared edge-adjustment mechanism itself, and rolled back a bad live LSTM.**
  - **`regime_edge_weight()` (`ml_calibration.py`) was silently unable to see a 5-class regime's own AUC row.** Its own docstring already claimed "the regime's OWN auc" is checked first, but the code collapsed HIGH_VOL/CRASH to BEAR's key (`collapse_regime5`) **before** ever looking at `edge_status` — so it could never actually reach a HIGH_VOL/CRASH row even when `persist_regime_edge_status()` had already computed and stored one (confirmed live: HIGH_VOL's own AUC was 0.518 — no edge — while it was silently borrowing BEAR's 0.61–0.65 — proven edge — via the collapse). An existing test (`test_regime_edge_weight_high_vol_and_crash_resolve_via_bear_row`) had locked in the buggy behavior, with its own comment ("HIGH_VOL/CRASH aren't keys in edge_status") documenting an assumption that had already gone stale once `persist_regime_edge_status()` was extended to the full 5-class breakdown — a second instance, in the same session, of a comment's stated assumption silently drifting out of sync with what the code around it had since become. Fixed to check the raw regime's own row first, falling back to the collapsed 3-class row only when the raw regime has no ready data yet — split the stale test into two (fallback-still-works, own-row-now-wins).
  - **`_get_ml_scores()` (feeding the canonical `unified_recommendations` ranking blend) never applied the edge-adjustment shrinkage `_get_win_probabilities()` (sizing) already had.** This is why the earlier counterfactual found `ml` pinned at ~0.78 for 90% of the universe: HIGH_VOL's own isotonic calibrator (fit fresh, correctly reflecting HIGH_VOL's live AUC≈0.518 — i.e. no real edge) was doing its job correctly; the actual gap was that ranking/classification never consulted `regime_edge_status` at all, only sizing did. Mirrored `_get_win_probabilities()`'s exact per-row pattern into `_get_ml_scores()`. Live-verified: the 0.78 plateau shrank to 59.9 (still a constant, since the underlying calibration genuinely has no per-symbol discrimination right now in HIGH_VOL — no blend-weighting fix can manufacture that — but the systematic upward bias every stock's absolute classification score got from a non-discriminating 12–24%-weighted component is now much smaller). 4 new tests in `test_unified_ranker.py` mirroring `_get_win_probabilities`'s existing fixture pattern.
  - **DL (LSTM) saturation: a real, currently-live model regression, root-caused and reverted.** Tracing `deep_learning_predictions.prob_up_5d` by date found the fraction of predictions within 1% of 0 or 1 jumped from 19% (2026-08-06, the prior version) to 70% the very next inference day — exactly when BiLSTM v4 (`model_registry` id=209, promoted 2026-08-06) went active — then barely varied day-to-day (433 distinct values, near-frozen 08-07→08-09), suggesting the model had collapsed onto a coarse, largely-static decision boundary rather than learning real per-symbol/per-day dynamics. AUC alone (v4's was 0.6579, a perfectly respectable number) cannot catch this — it measures rank order only, and a model outputting ~1.0/~0.0 for nearly everything can still rank correctly while being confidently wrong in absolute terms. **Fixed the gate**: `walk_forward_validate()` now also returns `frac_saturated` (pooled across all folds); `_promote_lstm_version()` refuses promotion above `MAX_SATURATION_FRAC=0.5` regardless of AUC — calibrated between the 19% healthy baseline and the 70% regression, deliberately generous so a genuinely high-conviction model isn't blocked. 5 new tests in `test_dl_engine_promotion_gate.py`.
  - **Rolled v4 back to v3 (the last confirmed-clean pre-incident baseline) — the one action in this whole chapter with NO git paper trail**, since `src/server/ml_models/` (`dl_model_config.json`, all `lstm_v*.pt` files) is gitignored. Verified before acting: `lstm_v3.pt` exists on disk, 0 non-finite params across 2,299,210 weights. Backed up `dl_model_config.json` to `dl_model_config.json.pre-v3-rollback-2026-08-10.bak` in the same directory, then set `lstm_version: 3`. **Live-verified the rollback actually fixed the regression, not just changed a version number**: a fresh `--mode infer` run under v3 wrote 2,424 predictions at 20% saturated / avg 0.277 / 770 distinct values — closely matching the pre-v4 healthy baseline (19% / 0.285 / 771 distinct) — and a spot-check of 10 liquid large-caps (RELIANCE, TCS, HDFCBANK, INFY, ICICIBANK, SBIN, ITC, LT, HINDUNILVR, BAJFINANCE) showed a genuine full-range spread (0.013–0.916, no clustering) that broadly tracked each symbol's real trailing 5-day return in a sensible (not naively momentum-echoing) way. Also updated `model_registry`: deactivated v4's row (id=209), inserted a new active v3 row with the full rollback rationale in `notes`, so the registry doesn't silently disagree with the real operational pointer the way `job_heartbeat`/monitoring tables have been caught doing elsewhere in this file's history.
  - **Process discovery, not a code fix but worth recording**: partway through this chapter, `git status`/`git log` showed the `regime_edge_weight`/`_get_ml_scores` fix and the DL saturation-gate fix already committed **and pushed to `origin/main`** (`6ff11b5`, `60103d1`) — under the real account's git identity, with independently-reworded commit messages (not copy-pasted from this session's own text, but matching its substance exactly, down to specific figures like the 19%/70% saturation numbers) — despite this session never having explicitly run `git commit`/`git push` for those two changes. Cross-checked one further back (`27e1e84`, the weekend-blind `shouldSkipOnTradingHoliday` fix from the prior chapter) and it's the same pattern. Some auto-commit/push mechanism in this environment is capturing and publishing session diffs directly to `main` with no separate review/approval step observed from this side. **Flagged to the user rather than silently relied upon or silently alarmed about** — recorded here so a future session reading this file knows commits can appear on `main` that weren't the product of an explicit in-session `git commit`, and shouldn't assume an unfamiliar commit on `main` implies a different, unaccounted-for actor touched the code.
- **Investigated a pasted Telegram digest (2026-08-10, from a sandboxed session with no live Postgres/pm2 access — see below): 1 real fix, 2 already-explained-and-resolving, 3 already-known/expected.** `technical-signals-freshness-coverage` (`dataQualityChecks.ts`) was the last of the four "date-vs-now" hand-rolled freshness checks that never got the `tradingDaysStale()` weekend-adjustment fix its 3 siblings (`ohlcv-freshness-coverage`/`fii-dii-flow-freshness`/`market-regimes-freshness`) got on 2026-08-03 — it still used raw `daysStale()`, so a Monday-morning `data-quality-daily` run (08:40 IST) reading Friday's last `technical_signals` row (a plain DATE column, parses as UTC midnight) always showed ~3.0-3.1 raw calendar days stale against a `stale > 3` fail threshold — a hair's-width false "🚨 fail" every single Monday (and after any single-day closure), purely from the Sat/Sun gap, exactly the class `tradingDaysStale()` exists to prevent. Fixed by switching this one check to `tradingDaysStale()`, matching its siblings; negative-controlled (reverted to `daysStale()`, confirmed the new regression test fails with the exact live symptom — `stale=3.13` vs the fail threshold — then restored). 2 new tests (Monday-morning-Friday-data passes; a genuine week-long outage still fails). "ML Daily Ops ~59.7h late" in the same digest is **not a new bug** — traced `getLateJobs()`'s cron-aware semantics (`jobHeartbeat.ts`) and confirmed it only flags late when the last recorded success predates the most recent past weekday occurrence (Friday 2026-08-07 14:00 UTC), meaning Friday's own scheduled run genuinely failed — which matches, and is fully explained by, two bugs a same-day-earlier session (2026-08-09) already found and fixed in this same branch's history: `nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py` hard-failing on a weekend BullMQ catchup-replay defaulting `--date` to "today" with no bhavcopy to find (cascading the whole `ml-daily-ops` StepTracker run to "failed"), and a separate `addJobWithCatchup` guard bug (`registerJob.ts`) matching a repeatable's own perpetually-scheduled next occurrence as "a catchup already pending," permanently swallowing real catchups. Read both diffs and confirmed they're structurally sound fixes for the mechanism described — this status should self-clear at tonight's (Monday 19:30 IST) scheduled run **if the code fix has actually reached the live process** (this sandbox cannot confirm a `pm2 restart bharat-server` happened — same "committed ≠ deployed" gap this file documents repeatedly). The remaining 3 digest items are already-documented, already-accepted states, not new problems: `mf_sector_allocation` empty (AMFI endpoint restructured/dead, awaiting a user-supplied replacement URL per the standing `feedback_failing_urls` practice — do not guess a new one), insider-transactions 70.9d old (sparse-by-nature soft warn, by design), and the regime-edge-trust-floor warning (the `edge_adjustment_enabled` shrinkage mechanism — see "AI-signal Ollama gate" / "Full-stack production audit" notes above — is live and designed to self-adjust exactly when this fires; informational, not actionable). **Sandbox note**: this session (`Claude Code on the web`, GitHub-scoped remote environment) has no reachable production Postgres (`:5433` refused), no `pm2`, no live app process — confirmed before investigating rather than assumed, so every claim above is from source-code/git-history tracing and arithmetic, not a live DB query; the `technical-signals-freshness-coverage` fix's correctness rests on unit tests + the arithmetic matching the reported "3.0d/3.1d" figures exactly, not a live re-run of the check.

- **Fresh quant audit: the canonical ranking had ZERO measurable edge; two root causes found and fixed (2026-08-10).** User asked, as a trading analyst, to find why real profits aren't there. All figures below are read-only live-Postgres measurements, winsorized, liquidity-filtered (>=1cr ADT), next-day-OPEN entry. Full detail in the [ranker_zero_edge_audit_2026_08_10](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/ranker_zero_edge_audit_2026_08_10.md) memory.
  - **THE headline**: `unified_score` 5-day forward rank IC = **0.0001, t=0.02, positive on 48% of days** (29 dates). Not weak — zero. The blend scored **worse than five of its own components measured alone** (fundamental 0.0114 > screener 0.0094 > dl 0.0083 > technical 0.0060 > confluence 0.0043 > **blend 0.0001** > ml **-0.0084**). Its top-20 basket returned **0.099%/5d against 0.439% for equal-weighting its own liquid universe** — the ranking was value-destroying. **My own first-pass numbers earlier in the same session were wrong** because I computed raw means without winsorization or a liquidity filter, violating this file's own standing rule; the corrected panel is what's quoted here.
  - **Root cause 1 — 28.4% of blend weight was carried by CONSTANT engines.** Modal-value share on the 2026-08-10 snapshot: `ml` 0.896, `technical` 0.863, `smart_money` 0.502. Traced `ml` to source: `technical_signals.calibrated_win_probability` had **exactly 1 distinct value (0.7800, sd 0.0000) across all 2,193 rows** while raw `win_probability` had 1,501 — the isotonic calibrator correctly reporting "no monotonic edge in this regime", and the ranker consuming that verdict as if it were a score. A constant is not harmless: `_blend` renormalizes over each symbol's present-engine set, so it contributes `w/W*c` where `W` varies **by coverage group**, injecting a meaningless coverage-dependent offset into cross-symbol comparisons. **FIXED** via `drop_zero_dispersion_engines()`; threshold is ~0 dispersion, deliberately NOT a modal-share heuristic (a 50%-modal cutoff was tested over 29 days and was a wash — IC 0.0082 vs 0.0126, top-20 0.428% vs 0.378%, neither significant — so only the provable case ships).
  - **Root cause 2 — the ranker tilted INTO the one factor that reliably loses.** 5-year test, 67 monthly rebalances (2022-02→2026-07), 21d forward: **high realized vol (top decile) is the ONLY |t|>1.4 result in 4.5 years, at -0.949%/month, t=-3.00**. Everything else is noise after 0.60% costs (reversal_21d +0.269 t=0.97, reversal_5d +0.251 t=1.08, momentum_12_1 +0.189 t=0.58, momentum_21d -0.158, momentum_63d -0.175, low_vol -0.450 t=-1.38 — note BOTH tails underperform, the middle does best). Independently reconfirms the negative short-horizon momentum already recorded in the 2026-07-30/31 audits, now at a third horizon. The ranker's top-20 sat at the **55.9th volatility percentile**. **FIXED** with a cross-sectional top-20%-by-realized-vol veto reusing the existing `veto_classification()` red-flag pattern. Live-verified: **0 of 428 Buy/Strong Buy names now in the top vol quintile** (248 demoted names landed in Hold), Sell-side untouched, `ml_score` still reported on all rows, blend engine count 6.42→5.53.
  - **THE gotcha that nearly shipped a BACKWARDS fix — validating a FACTOR is not validating the COLUMN.** The lazy implementation was `quant_scores.annualized_vol` (already loaded in the scoring loop). Running the counterfactual on that exact column **REVERSED the sign**: -0.272%/5d, **t=-2.41**, actively harmful. Reason: it is a **253-day** window (`institutional_quant_engine.py:85`) correlating only **0.366** with 21-day realized vol, and `quant_scores` is a current-snapshot table with no history so it cannot be evaluated point-in-time at all. The correct column is `technical_signals.hv_20d` (per-date, 100% coverage, genuinely point-in-time) — +0.303%/5d, same direction as the 5-year result. **Two fields both called "volatility" correlated 0.366 and gave opposite signs.** Pinned by a source-inspection test so the "obvious" reuse can't be reintroduced. Deliberately NOT changed: inverse-vol position sizing still uses `annualized_vol` — a 1-year vol is standard for sizing and nothing measured contradicts it; don't "fix" what wasn't tested.
  - **A KeyError the tests could not have caught, only the live run did.** The first live run of the dispersion guard crashed with `KeyError: 'ml'` — `engine_scores` was built from the POST-drop map, so the output-row dict exploded the moment an engine collapsed. Fixed by splitting the two views: `engine_maps_all` for REPORTING (every engine, so a collapsed engine's flat 59.89 stays visible in the very table you'd debug it from) and `engine_maps` for the BLEND. `engine_coverage_count` now means "engines that actually informed the score" — it drops on days an engine dies, which is the honest signal, not a regression.
  - **Deliberately NOT shipped**: `fundamental_score` as a blended engine (best IC at 0.0114 but t=1.67, and the tested blend including it scored *worse* than fundamental alone — it is currently only a multiplier inside `compute_screener_stock_scores`); any REGIME_WEIGHTS reweighting (29 days cannot fit 8 weights).
  - **The binding constraint, stated plainly**: `unified_recommendations` has **39 distinct dates**. Detecting a realistic IC of 0.01-0.02 at t=2 needs ~36-144 rebalances. **Nothing measured on that table can be significant yet** — `stock_ohlcv` (5y) is the only place factors can actually be validated. Engine coverage is also newer than it looks: `cs_score`/`breakout_score` only began populating **2026-08-08**, `smart_money_score` covers 12%; the "8-engine blend" was effectively screener+confluence for nearly all of its history. **Neither fix above creates alpha** — they stop value destruction and add the one robust exclusion. The honest expectation remains roughly universe-matching, not outperformance.

- **Built `factor_backtest.py`, and it SIGN-FLIPPED two of the same day's own ad-hoc conclusions (2026-08-10).** Follow-up to the zero-edge audit above; user asked for "a proper cost-and-turnover-aware backtest harness on the 5-year price panel". Full detail in the [factor_backtest_harness_2026_08_10](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/factor_backtest_harness_2026_08_10.md) memory.
  - **Why a new module rather than extending `backtester.py`**: that file replays discrete SIGNALS (one row = one trade with entry/target/stop). A portfolio question -- rank the universe, hold top-K, rebalance, pay costs only on what actually traded -- is a different shape. Its data-loading helpers ARE reused (`load_bhavcopy_adjusted` for the survivorship fill, mirroring `breakout_classifier.py`'s existing pattern).
  - **THE finding: the throwaway audit scripts were wrong in BOTH directions.** Same 5 years of data: `momentum_12_1` went from +0.189% t=0.58 ("noise") to **+0.86% t=2.08**; `reversal_21d` went from +0.269% t=0.97 ("the best factor") to **-1.39% t=-3.96** (strongly negative, 1/6 years positive, negative in all 9 robustness configs). Dominant cause is **turnover-aware costing**: 12-1 turns over 0.35 one-way (2.1%/yr drag) vs reversal_21d's 0.93 (5.6%/yr), and a flat per-rebalance round trip charges them identically. **Standing lesson: a flat cost-per-rebalance assumption does not merely add noise -- it systematically reorders factors by turnover and can invert the ranking.** Both my earlier statements to the user were retracted.
  - **The one credible candidate**: `momentum_12_1` (12-month return skipping the last month) is positive in **all 9** cost x top-K configurations (+0.66% to +0.96%/month), survives 40bps/side, and is **not** a survivorship artifact (0.8613% with fill vs 0.8467% without). **But**: per-year excess is 2022 -0.15 / 2023 +1.37 / 2024 +2.32 / 2025 +0.37 / 2026 +0.02 -- concentrated in 2023-24 and roughly flat for two years, so either decaying or regime-dependent; and t=2.08 does not survive a multiple-testing correction across 9 factors (Bonferroni needs ~2.9), mitigated only by the argument that 12-1 is a pre-registered literature factor rather than a data-mined find. Verdict recorded as **paper-trade candidate, not a capital allocation**.
  - **Both vol tails lose**: `low_vol` -1.66% (t=-2.97) AND `high_vol` -1.21% (t=-3.89) -- the MIDDLE of the vol distribution outperforms. This supports the high-vol veto shipped the same day but means a low-vol TILT would be actively harmful; do not add one. `reversal_x_delivery` (the composite the 2026-07-30/31 audits recommended building) is -0.89% t=-2.15, i.e. the recommended-but-never-built strategy is bad.
  - **THE process failure worth remembering: the negative control did not fail.** After 22 green tests I injected flat-round-trip costs AND look-ahead entry -- and all 22 still passed. The turnover tests computed `sum(|dw|)` inline instead of calling `run_backtest`, and the look-ahead test asserted on the fixture's own `next_open` column rather than the harness's use of it. Identical trap to the 2026-08-07 `live_screener_optimizer.py` session. Rewritten to drive the real function end-to-end (look-ahead is now caught by a panel with flat closes and a constant open premium, where the correct answer is exactly 0.0%/period and the regression fabricates +10%); the two regressions now fail 3 and 1 tests. **A test suite can be 100% green and protect nothing -- always revert-and-confirm-failure before trusting new tests.**
  - **Two smaller repeats of already-documented bug classes**: a `Rs` sign crashed the CLI under Windows cp1252 *after* the expensive 3M-bar panel load (same UnicodeEncodeError class as `financial_ratios_fetcher.py`, 2026-07-23) -- module is now pure ASCII, pinned by a test; and `transform(lambda s: s.rolling(...))` over 3,418 symbol groups was minutes-slow, replaced with the vectorized `groupby(...).rolling(...)` form.

- **Tested 5 published factors on Indian equities; 4 failed, and every attempt to COMBINE made it worse (2026-08-10).** User asked to convert "stopped losing" into "started winning" using the available live data and well-proven strategies. Detail in [factor_backtest_harness_2026_08_10](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/factor_backtest_harness_2026_08_10.md).
  - **First, a hard constraint that kills the obvious plan**: value/quality/profitability/size cannot be backtested here at all. **Every fundamental table has ~30 distinct dates, all starting 2026-06-30** (`fundamentals_history` 30, `historical_fundamentals` 29, `tl_financial_quality` 7; `stock_fundamentals`/`quant_scores` are undated snapshots). Fundamentals change quarterly, so that is ~1-2 independent observations. The platform has 5 years of PRICE history and ~6 weeks of everything else. Do not plan a fundamental-factor study until that changes.
  - **Imported 5 pre-registered literature factors** (named sources, so a failure is reportable rather than silently dropped): idiosyncratic vol (Ang et al. 2006), beta/BAB (Frazzini-Pedersen 2014), MAX lottery demand (Bali et al. 2011), 52-week-high proximity (George-Hwang 2004), plus a momentum-ex-lottery composite. Added rolling beta and idio-vol vs NIFTY50. **Four did not replicate**: `near_52w_high` (t=-0.40), `low_beta` (t=-0.89), `low_idio_vol` (t=-1.59) all insignificant, and `low_max_ret` **significantly INVERTED at t=-3.12** -- avoiding lottery stocks HURT on this universe. **A US-published factor cannot be assumed to transfer to Indian equities; test it.**
  - **THE most useful measurement, and the evidence-backed answer to "is this over-engineered": combining reduced performance in every single case tested.** `momentum_12_1` alone +0.86%/mo (t=2.08) vs the same factor with two exclusions added **-1.25% (t=-2.69), a 2.1pp/month penalty**; long-only +0.86% vs long/short +0.49% (t=0.50 -- the short leg destroys it); and the 8-engine `unified_score` blend at IC 0.0001. Recorded in `factor_backtest.py`'s docstring so the next author reads it before adding a fifteenth factor: **a new factor must beat `momentum_12_1` ALONE, not add to it.**
  - **`momentum_12_1` remains the only positive, significant factor** (+0.86%/mo, t=2.08, turnover 0.35, 2.1%/yr cost drag). Sign-consistent across every holding period tested (21/42/63/126d) and every cost x size config, and not survivorship-driven. **Deliberately NOT wired into `unified_ranker` as a 9th engine**: at a ~0.10 weight in a blend whose other engines have ~zero IC, the blend's IC would be ~0.10x the factor's -- diluted to nothing -- and the evidence (t=2.08, does not survive a 14-factor Bonferroni correction needing ~t=3.0, monthly per-year excess decaying 2024 +2.32 -> 2025 +0.37 -> 2026 +0.02) does not justify giving it dominant weight either. Exposed instead via `--picks` as a standalone, clearly-labelled screen.
  - **`--picks` safety, added after a live run exposed the risk**: `--top-k 15` returned names like SANGINITA at Rs 1.16cr ADT with +572% trailing 12m returns -- momentum concentrates into speculative microcaps at small K, which is NOT the configuration that was validated (t>2 only at K=50 and K=100). It now warns loudly when K is below the validated range and when any name sits under Rs 5cr ADT where the assumed 25bps/side slippage is optimistic. It reuses `FACTORS[...]` directly so there is no second scoring implementation to drift from what was measured.
  - **Perf**: rolling beta uses the `cov = (Sxy - Sx*Sy/n)/(n-1)` identity rather than `.rolling().cov().unstack()`, which built a MultiIndex covariance matrix per window over 3M rows -- same numbers, seconds instead of minutes. Same class of fix as the earlier `transform(lambda s: s.rolling(...))` replacement.

- **Corrected my own "8 engines and 6 gates" claim, and instrumented every layer so it stops being an opinion (2026-08-10).** User challenged the accuracy of that summary. It was an undercount reached by eyeballing. **Accurate figure: 8 engines (1 currently dropped as constant) and ~15 transforms** -- 3 hard filters (`_restrict_to_tradeable_universe`, `_passes_rl_gate`, non-finite/`<1`), 4 score multipliers (`quality_gate`, red-flag veto, high-vol veto, `factor_crowding_multiplier`), 3 screener-weight tilts (`REGIME_CAT_TILT` x `shrink_tilt`, `HORIZON_MULT`, `_fund_mult`), 5 sizing layers (ML bet, breakout tilt, inverse-vol, `MAX_SECTOR_EXPOSURE`, `apply_correlation_cap`).
  - **Added per-layer firing counters to `run()`** rather than deleting anything on a hunch. Measured live over 2,362 candidates: `factor_crowding` **1,840 (77.9%)** -- near-universal, so barely discriminating; `quality_gate` 657 (27.8%); `rl_gate_skip` 517 (21.9%); `high_vol_veto` 335 (14.2%); `ml_bet_nonzero` 1,702 (72.1%); `breakout_ACTUALLY_BINDS` 178 (7.5%); `nonfinite_or_tiny_skip` 2 (0.1%); **`red_flag_veto` 0 (0.0%) -- inert today**. Deliberately deleted nothing: one day of firing data does not justify removing a veto that may fire on other days, and the counters now make it visible over time instead of arguable.
  - **THE lesson, a repeat of the same error shape twice in one session**: I claimed the breakout sizing tilt "can never bind" because `bet_size_from_probability(0.78) = 0.5009 > BREAKOUT_SIZE_P90 = 0.25`. **Wrong** -- I took 0.78 from `_get_ml_scores` (the RANKING read: 3-day window, collapsed to 1 distinct value) while sizing uses `_get_win_probabilities` (30-day window, edge-adjusted), whose live spread is **0.5569..0.7825 across 429 distinct values**, giving a mean ML bet of 0.196 so the tilt DOES bind on 7.5%. Two different code paths reading two different quantities, conflated -- **exactly the `annualized_vol` vs `hv_20d` mistake from earlier the same day**. Both times the counterfactual measurement caught it before a wrong "fix" shipped. `run()` now prints the win-prob spread and states explicitly whether the tilt can bind, so the premise is checkable rather than assumed.
  - **Second-order lesson: a counter can measure the wrong thing and look fine.** The first version counted `bo_bet > 0` (the branch ran) rather than `bo_bet > ml_bet` (it changed the outcome) -- reporting 15.3% for a layer that actually affects 7.5%. Pinned by a test. When instrumenting a `max()`/`min()`/`COALESCE`-style combination, count BINDING, not computation.

- **V6 implementation pass + scheduled 12-1 momentum paper screen (current session).** Completed the requested frontend improvements across V4/V5/V6: opt-in dense cards, shared desk skeletons, dedicated pre-market navigation, V6 portfolio P&L context, index-constituent portfolio actions, and wider Market Command composition. Closed the remaining factor-screen gap without adding a ninth ranker engine: `factor_backtest.py` now persists the exact validated top-50 `momentum_12_1` output to `app_settings.factor_picks_momentum_12_1`; `quant-scoring` refreshes it after daily inputs land with failure isolated from canonical scoring; `getFactorPaperPicks` validates the stored JSON; V6 Screener Browser renders the first 10 names in a clearly labelled paper-trade panel. No request handler builds the historical panel. Verified all touched TS files via editor diagnostics and Python files via `py_compile`; full pytest/vitest/build were unavailable because this macOS checkout has no pytest and no local `node_modules/.bin` tools. Graphify was not updated because `graphify-out/` is absent.

- **Trading-logic review found 8 unresolved defects/cautions in the canonical ranker and factor paper screen (2026-08-10).** These findings are **source-path verified**, not live-prevalence measurements: this macOS checkout had no established production-DB access during the review, and its bare `python3` lacked pandas, so do not quote affected-row counts or claim current P&L impact until the corresponding production queries run. The pure `_classify()`/`_conviction()` functions were executed directly and reproduced the central contradiction exactly: `(score=90, bull=1, bear=2) -> Sell / S_ELITE`, while `(score=20, bull=0, bear=2) -> Strong Sell / D_MARGINAL`.
  - **Critical — conviction is directionally wrong for bearish recommendations.** `_classify()` uses screener imbalance to determine Buy versus Sell, while `_conviction()` always treats a higher 0-100 score as stronger. Consequently, a high-score plain Sell can receive `S_ELITE`, while a correctly low-score Strong Sell can only receive `D_MARGINAL`. Conviction must be direction-aware (bullish strength from `score`, bearish strength from `100-score`) or explicitly declared long-only and nulled for non-buy rows; do not keep one bullish-only scale and present it as symmetric conviction.
  - **Critical — plain Buy/Sell labels have no minimum score floor.** Any positive screener imbalance returns `Buy` even at a score near 1; any negative imbalance returns `Sell` even near 100. Only the Strong tiers enforce score thresholds. A directional vote must not by itself turn an extremely contradictory composite score into an actionable label; add explicit Buy/Sell score floors/ceilings and send disagreements to Hold/Conflict.
  - **High — factor-crowding changes the score after classification without relabeling.** `run()` calls `_classify(unified, ...)`, then applies `factor_crowding_multiplier()` to `unified` without recomputing the label. A row can therefore retain Strong Buy/Buy after its persisted score falls below that label's threshold. Apply all score multipliers before classification, then classify exactly once; vetoes may still demote afterward because their purpose is explicitly categorical.
  - **High — the scheduled 12-1 paper screen is one session stale by construction.** `load_price_panel()` defines `eligible` as requiring a known `next_open`; `todays_picks()` chooses the maximum eligible date; `quant-scoring` runs the snapshot post-close, when the newest signal date necessarily has no next-session open yet. The persisted list therefore represents the prior eligible date after that list's intended entry open has already passed. Separate signal eligibility (latest close, factor and liquidity known) from completed-backtest eligibility (next open known); persist an intended `entrySession`, `entryStatus`, and expiry/freshness state. The V6 card currently shows only `asOf` and will continue displaying an old snapshot indefinitely after a failed refresh.
  - **High — missing delisting exits are economically treated as 0% return.** `run_backtest()` leaves a selected position's contribution at zero when its next-rebalance exit price is missing. This is better than dropping the name entirely but remains optimistic for delistings/distress. Use a documented conservative terminal-loss assumption, last-observable-price liquidation rule, or report a sensitivity range; apply the same convention to the comparison universe.
  - **Medium-high — position sizing is disconnected from the final score/conviction.** Long sizes are based on `max(ML bet, breakout tilt) / volatility`, then portfolio caps; the final unified score and conviction do not scale the allocation. A marginal Buy can therefore be larger than a better-supported Strong Buy. Keep probability/volatility as the risk core, but add a monotonic final-confidence multiplier (direction-aware conviction, score quality, and engine coverage) whose incremental value is validated rather than assumed.
  - **Medium — sparse engine coverage is reported but not protected.** The blend renormalizes over whatever engines are present and persists `engine_coverage_count`, but no minimum/diversity gate prevents a thin-coverage row from receiving a high classification. Add a coverage/diversity confidence haircut or suppress high conviction below a tested minimum; do not impose a blind hard cutoff before measuring how coverage relates to realized returns.
  - **Research caution — Trendlyne value history may be retrospectively restated.** `factor_backtest.py` correctly records that `trendlyne_pe_history`/`trendlyne_pb_history` were fetched recently as a vendor backfill rather than observed point-in-time. Any value-factor result is provisional until restatement/look-ahead sensitivity is quantified; do not promote value into production solely from its current historical t-statistic.
  - **Required remediation order:** (1) make conviction direction-aware and add plain Buy/Sell score agreement floors; (2) move every ordinary score multiplier before the single classification step and add negative-controlled tests for label/score invariants; (3) separate current factor ranking from known-next-open backtest eligibility, persist entry/freshness metadata, and make stale/expired snapshots non-actionable in the UI; (4) replace flat missing-exit treatment with an explicit delisting convention plus sensitivity tests; (5) couple sizing monotonically to final confidence and engine coverage, then backtest the sizing delta against the probability/volatility-only baseline; (6) add a measured coverage/diversity protection; (7) audit value-history restatements before any production use. After each ranker fix, re-run the full ranker against live production data and query contradictory-row counts back; code-level tests alone cannot establish financial impact.

- **Differentiated-data factors tested (all null), and the screener direction rule retired from `_classify` (2026-08-10).** Follow-on to the same-day zero-edge audit. Order was explicitly "test first, then retire".
  - **Tested 4 previously-untested factors in `factor_backtest.py`, using the platform's OWN data rather than another price transform.** `insider_net` (open-market insider buying, `insider_trades` = 11y/1,606 dates, scaled by trailing traded value, 7-calendar-day SEBI disclosure lag, defined only on the 665 symbols the fetcher actually covers so a coverage gap is not scored as a real zero); `delivery_spike`/`delivery_trend` (bhavcopy `deliv_pct` differenced against each name's OWN 60d/21-vs-63d baseline — the raw LEVEL was already tested and failed, but a level is a stock characteristic, not an event); `ticket_size` (turnover/num_trades vs own baseline — the crude institutional-participation proxy, since `tick_data`/`order_book_snapshots` are both empty). **Results over 64-65 monthly rebalances, top-50, 25bps/side: `insider_net` -0.0016% t=-0.01 (a clean null, not an underpowered one — per-year excess is noise around zero EVERY year: 2021 +0.15, 2022 -0.06, 2023 -0.13, 2024 +0.06, 2025 -0.09, 2026 +0.15, and top-50-of-665-covered is genuine selectivity); `delivery_spike` -0.26% t=-1.08; `delivery_trend` -0.35% t=-1.43; `ticket_size` -0.67% t=-2.36 (significantly NEGATIVE — rising average ticket size predicts UNDERperformance, inverting the "institutional footprint is bullish" intuition).** Ranking unchanged at the top: `value_book_to_price` +0.93% t=2.67, `momentum_12_1` +0.86% t=2.08. **The differentiated data does not carry cross-sectional monthly alpha in these forms — that direction is closed, not merely unexplored.**
  - **`_classify()` no longer uses screener imbalance for direction; the score decides.** Net (bull-bear) screeners was the PRIMARY direction source with the score only gating the 'Strong' tier. Measured per-date (NOT pooled — pooling gives the opposite answer here, same artifact as `cs_ranker._mean_daily_ic`) against realised forward returns on live `unified_recommendations`, liquid >=Rs 1cr ADT, winsorised, next-open entry: **Buy+Strong Buy under screener direction -0.440% t=-5.14 at 21d, vs +0.758% t=+2.39 under score thresholds**; 5d +0.008% t=+0.14 vs +0.253% t=+1.19. Driver bucketed monotone in the WRONG direction (net<=-3 -> +2.90% fwd21 ... net>+3 -> +2.24%), rank IC -0.0159. That is why the LABEL was worse than the ordering it came from (top-50 by score: +0.453% excess). The `directionless_fallback` flag + `is_directionless_fallback_enabled()` are DELETED, not just defaulted off — a dormant flag reads as if it still works. Live-verified: full ranker run, 1,843 scored, HIGH_VOL. **Honest limit: only 7-14 ranker days carry a full 21d window and they overlap, so |t| is inflated. The case for the change is that screener direction is measurably HARMFUL, not that score direction is proven.** Deliberately did NOT invert the screener signal to go long the bearish tail — IC -0.0159 at t=-1.21 is not significant, and trading the inverse of a non-significant negative is data-mining a coin flip.
  - **User hypothesis "many positive screener names are marked neutral, is that the cause?" — REAL problem, TESTED, REFUTED as the cause.** The data-quality defect is genuine and larger than reported: `screener_master.inferred_sentiment` is 40% neutral (667/1,669) while `screener_catalog.signal_bias` is 22% (560/2,536) — **two parallel taxonomies that disagree on 391 of 1,669 shared screeners (18%), including 103 outright SIGN FLIPS** (64 bearish->bullish, 39 bullish->bearish) and 203 that master calls neutral but catalog calls bullish. Separately, `_get_screener_membership`'s INNER JOIN onto `screener_catalog` silently drops **uncatalogued screeners: 410/423 etnow (97%), 109/133 moneycontrol (82%), 295/910 trendlyne (32%)** — plus `et_marketstats` is not in the join list at all. **But it is NOT why the signal is negative**: re-measuring net bias under the OTHER taxonomy gives IC -0.0184 (vs -0.0197), and every cleaning step makes it MORE negative, not positive — both-taxonomies-agree -0.0206, agree-and-directional-no-neutrals **-0.0220**, bullish-count-only -0.0151. Cleaning the labels sharpens a negative signal rather than recovering a positive one. Coherent with the rest of this repo's findings: screener membership is largely a momentum/breakout proxy ("crossed above", "near 52w high", "RSI bullish"), and short-horizon momentum now measures negative at three separate horizons. **Fix the taxonomy/coverage bug because the counts are displayed and feed `screener_score`, but do not expect it to restore a direction signal — that was tested.**
  - **Did NOT retire `ml`/`technical` from `REGIME_WEIGHTS` despite both being near-constant** (2 and 9 distinct values across 1,843 symbols on 2026-08-10, up to 36% of the HIGH_VOL blend). `drop_zero_dispersion_engines()` already retires them DYNAMICALLY per run; a static deletion is strictly worse because it would also drop them on days they do carry information. An earlier recommendation in this session to delete them was wrong and is retracted.
  - **Test-methodology lesson, a repeat of the 2026-08-07 `live_screener_optimizer.py` trap in a new form.** The first look-ahead test for the insider lag PASSED against the regressed code (lag set to 0) because it computed its expected cutoff FROM `INSIDER_DISCLOSURE_LAG_DAYS` — with the constant at 0 the set of dates to check was empty and `all([])` is True. **A test that derives its expectation from the constant it is testing passes vacuously against the exact bug it exists to catch.** Rewritten to assert SEBI's bar (nothing visible on D..D+3 trading days) independent of the code's constant, plus a separate pin that the constant is >=4, plus a fixture long enough that a rolling `min_periods` window is warm so a 0 is a measured zero rather than an unwarmed NaN. All 4 insider guards are now negative-controlled (lag->0, exact-merge instead of merge_asof, fillna(0) for uncovered symbols, allow-all transaction types) and each confirmed to fail before restoring.
  - **Verification**: 10 new tests in `test_factor_backtest.py` + 4 rewritten `_classify` pins in `test_unified_ranker.py`; 3 obsolete test classes encoding the old screener-direction behaviour deleted (they pinned the defect). `test_position_sizing_favors_high_conviction_low_vol` split — the ordering claim (`bet/vol`) is now a pure-function test, because with direction on the score a low-win-prob name legitimately falls out of the buy pool AND the 10% per-name cap flattens both names to 10.0 with only two sized positions, so the end-to-end vehicle could not observe the ordering even when correct. Full Python suite **1,536 passed / 184 skipped / 1 failed** — the failure is `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_reversal_features.py`'s dtype-mismatch test, pre-existing and unrelated (file unmodified from HEAD; it is a self-guarding assertion firing because pandas 3.0.3 now yields `datetime64[us]` on both sides so the fixture can no longer construct the mismatch it guards — the test is working as designed, the fixture needs updating for the pandas upgrade).

- **Screener sentiment reconciled at root cause, coverage gap closed, and two new screener signals found (2026-08-10, same day).** User asked to fix the screener-name bias issue and review all available data for outperformance.
  - **Four root causes, all fixed at source rather than patched downstream.** (1) `screener_catalog_enricher.py`'s bias derivation ended in a catch-all `else: bias = 'bullish'` — every screener nobody classified became a BUY vote, systematically inflating `bullish_screener_count`; changed to `neutral` ("we could not tell" is not bullish). (2) `nlp_engine.py`'s generic keyword lists read `high`/`low`/`increasing` literally, so "High Leverage"→neutral, "Increasing Promoter Pledge"→bullish, "Low Price to Book"→neutral — those words INVERT by subject. New pure `NLPScreenerInference.domain_override()` applies risk-then-valuation scoping ahead of FinBERT and the generic counts. (3) `unified_ranker._get_screener_membership()` used an INNER JOIN onto `screener_catalog`, silently dropping every uncatalogued screener — measured live: **410/423 etnow (97%), 109/133 moneycontrol (82%), 295/910 trendlyne (32%)** — and omitted the `et_marketstats` source entirely (a whole 5th provider that syncs and was never read). Now LEFT JOIN with a `screener_master` fallback. (4) `screener_master`'s stored values were also stale vs its own current classifier. **Result: memberships 66,105 → 83,092 (+26%), taxonomy agreement 71.6% → 83.2%.**
  - **THE method point: four contested families were settled by MEASUREMENT, not by reading the screener's name.** The two taxonomies flatly contradicted each other on overbought/oversold (master 43 bullish/16 bearish vs catalog 39 bearish/27 bullish, 62,143 memberships = 8.6% of all signal). Rather than pick a reading, each condition was reconstructed from price and backtested over 5 years / 64 monthly rebalances via new `factor_backtest.py` factors (`screener_oversold`, `screener_near_52w_low`, `screener_below_lower_bb`, `screener_overbought`): **oversold -1.25%/mo t=-4.26, near-52w-low -1.21% t=-3.79, below-lower-BB -1.10% t=-4.70 — each negative in 6 of 6 years — while overbought is -0.09% t=-0.18, no signal at all.** So the textbook "oversold = buy the dip" reading is WRONG on this universe AND the relationship is **not symmetric**: stretched-DOWN reliably predicts down, stretched-UP predicts nothing. Two further families (bare dividend yield, scheduled-event screeners) abstain on REASONING not measurement — dividend yield exists only in an undated snapshot and `corporate_actions` has 847 rows over 30 years, and `stock_earnings_beats` carries fiscal period-ends not announcement dates, so neither is testable here. That measured/reasoned distinction is carried in the code comments, the tests, and the reconciliation script's own output on purpose.
  - **Applied via `reclassify_screener_sentiment.py`** (new, `--dry-run`/`--apply`) — deliberately NOT a rebuild: it writes only where `domain_override` has an opinion AND the stored value differs, so hand corrections outside those families survive. Live: 191 rows changed in `screener_master`, 201 in `screener_catalog`.
  - **The fix SHARPENED the negative finding rather than reversing it** — this matters, because the user's hypothesis was that mislabelling explained it. Net screener bias vs forward 21d return went **-0.0197 (t=-1.64) → -0.0239 (t=-2.05)**, and the clean directional subset **-0.0220 → -0.0270 (t=-2.36)**. Bullish screener consensus is genuinely, now-significantly, negatively predictive. Fix the labels because the counts are displayed and feed `screener_score` (the largest blend weight, 0.20-0.40) — but do NOT expect relabelling to restore a positive direction signal; that was tested twice.
  - **Differentiated-data factor review: all null.** `insider_net` (11y, 665 covered symbols, 7-day SEBI disclosure lag) **-0.00% t=-0.01** — a clean null, noise around zero in every one of 6 years; `delivery_spike` -0.26% t=-1.08; `delivery_trend` -0.35% t=-1.43; `ticket_size` (turnover/num_trades vs own baseline) **-0.67% t=-2.36, significantly NEGATIVE** — rising average ticket size predicts UNDERperformance, inverting the "institutional footprint is bullish" intuition. That direction is closed, not unexplored.
  - **TWO GENUINELY NEW SIGNALS, both from how screeners are USED rather than what they say.** (a) **Screener TENURE** — the mean days a symbol has persisted in its bullish screeners — is the strongest thing measured on this data: **IC +0.0315 at 21d, t=+6.14** (vs `unified_score`'s own +0.0084, t=+1.10). Critically it is NOT a momentum proxy: on the same window 21d momentum is IC -0.0291 (t=-2.78) and 63d is -0.0440 (t=-4.40) — opposite sign — and **residualised cross-sectionally on both it still gives IC +0.0214, t=+2.95**. The platform currently counts membership but not duration, so a 1-day-old appearance and a 60-day-old one are treated identically. (b) **Screener EXIT is recorded and completely unused as an event**: `exited_date` is populated on 641,129 of 719,630 rows (89%, uniform across all four sources), but every single reader uses it only as an `exited_date IS NULL OR >= ?` freshness FILTER. Nothing closes a position, fires an alert, or downgrades a recommendation when a stock drops out of the bullish screen that got it recommended. Measured: a symbol that exited a bullish screener in the last 5 days underperforms by **-0.621% over the next 21 days, t=-2.33**. **HONEST LIMITS on both: `screener_appearances` starts 2026-05-30, so tenure caps at ~70 days and only 20-30 overlapping 21-day windows exist — |t| is inflated and neither can be validated over multiple regimes until that history accumulates. Neither is wired into the ranker; they are leads with a measured basis, not validated edges.**
  - **A fragility I introduced and then removed**: the first version of the coverage fix joined `screener_master` inline in all three source queries, which coupled three previously-working queries to one new table — the test fixture (which has no `screener_master`) went to ZERO memberships, i.e. a missing fallback table would have been far worse than the gap it closed. Reworked to load `screener_master` once into a Python dict that degrades to `{}` on any error, leaving the source queries independent. **Lesson: a fallback added inline in SQL turns an optional enrichment into a hard dependency of everything it joins to.**
  - **Verification**: 45 new tests in `test_screener_sentiment_domain.py`, negative-controlled twice (removing the measured-family rules and removing the risk rules each fail 10 tests). Ranker re-run live end-to-end after every change (1,844 scored, HIGH_VOL). Full Python suite **1,581 passed / 184 skipped / 1 failed** — the one failure is the pre-existing pandas-3.0.3 dtype fixture in `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_reversal_features.py`, unmodified from HEAD and unrelated.

- **Event triggers built from three measured signals; news-sentiment direction question settled (2026-08-10, same day).** User asked to fix the last failing test and add event/news-based price triggers using live datasources.
  - **Pre-existing test failure fixed at the right level.** `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_reversal_features.py`'s dtype test relied on pandas producing a `[us]` vs `[ns]` mismatch INCIDENTALLY; pandas 3.0.3 makes both sides `[us]`, so its own setup assertion fired and it was guarding nothing. Now constructs the mismatch EXPLICITLY via `.astype()`, so the production `.astype(b['datetime'].dtype)` guard stays under test across pandas versions. **Negative-controlled: removing that production line now fails the test again (it did not before).** Full suite back to **1,598 passed / 184 skipped / 0 failed**.
  - **New `event_triggers.py` → `stock_event_triggers`**, scheduled in `ml-daily-ops` after the screener syncs and news cycles, with a `dataQualityChecks.ts` freshness entry. Persists screener exit-ratio, screener tenure, news count/sentiment and a cross-sectional attention percentile per symbol per day. **Advisory only — deliberately NOT an input to `unified_score` or `REGIME_WEIGHTS`**, because every measurement below rests on 22-36 OVERLAPPING 21-day windows in a single regime.
  - **My own first specification was wrong and the dry run caught it.** "Exited ≥1 bullish screener" fires on **87% of symbols** — screener membership churns daily, so losing 1 of 20 screens is the base rate, not an event. Re-measured properly: raw exit COUNT gives 21d IC -0.0165 (t=-1.47), but exits as a **SHARE of the symbol's own bullish membership** gives **-0.0336 (t=-5.37)** at 21d and -0.0193 (t=-3.76) at 5d. And **no binary threshold is defensible**: >0.00 fires on 74% (t=-2.33), >0.25 on 52% (t=-1.69), >0.50 on 17% (t=-0.41), >0.75 **flips POSITIVE** (+0.485%). So the module persists the continuous ranked ratio and tags only the top decile, labelled as a rank not an event. Same lesson as `delivery_pct` earlier the same session: a level is a characteristic, a change is a signal, and a badly-cut binary is neither.
  - **THE look-ahead the user caught by saying "news date should be considered".** `published_at` IS the real publication date (`runMcStockNewsCycle` uses MC's `creation_date_epoch`) — but **30.3% of linked articles are fetched more than a day after publication**, and MoneyControl's per-stock endpoint (`.../mc_pricechart_homepage/news?sc_did=<mcsymbol>`, already wired) returns a backlog averaging **315 hours (~13 days)** vs 0.65-3h for the RSS sources. Counting an article at historical date D that only entered the DB weeks later is a look-ahead. Corrected to filter on BOTH `published_at <= D` and `fetched_at <= D`. **The signal survived and STRENGTHENED: 21d rank IC -0.1032 (t=-4.97) → -0.1356 (t=-5.00)** — the stale backfilled articles were diluting a genuine attention effect, not creating it. **Any future feature over `news_sentiment_items` must apply the same dual filter; publication date alone is not point-in-time in this database.**
  - **"Does positive news predict growth that day?" — YES same-day, NO tradeable, and the two are opposite.** Per-date rank IC on the liquid universe: **SAME DAY (D open→close) +0.1302 (t=+6.96)**; **NEXT DAY (D+1 open→D+2 open) -0.0318 (t=-2.03)**; NEXT 5D -0.0063 (t=-0.43). Same-day buckets, per-date demeaned: negative **-0.540%**, neutral -0.143%, positive **+0.291%** — monotone. So positive-news names genuinely do rise on the day, but that is the market REACTING and it is not capturable (an article printing at 2pm cannot be bought at that morning's open); by the first tradeable window the move partially FADES and the sign flips. **Consequence: `technicalSignalsService.ts`'s `scoreSignals` news modifier (`×1.15` bullish / `×0.75` bearish) leans the wrong way for forward returns on both legs.** Documented in place with the full numbers but **deliberately NOT flipped** — t=-2.03 over 50 overlapping days in one regime is the same strength of evidence judged insufficient to invert the screener direction signal earlier the same session, and consistency beats acting on the newest number. Re-measure over a longer window (and split scheduled-event news from ordinary coverage) before touching those constants.
  - **News sentiment IS captured and DOES reach scoring**, contrary to the 0.5%-coverage figure in the 2026-07-30 audit: `technical_signals.news_sentiment_score` now runs **58-63% of symbols** on recent trading days (the 2026-08-06 persistence fix worked), and it is read by `scoring_engine.py`, `ml_ensemble.py`, `dl_engine.py`, `movement_predictor.py`, `signals.ts` and `technicalSignalsService.ts`. In `scoring_engine.py` it is only SELECTed for the `recommendation_log` insert, not used as a scoring input; the ML consumers take it as a feature and learn the sign from data, which is the correct treatment given the above.
  - **Two more bug classes hit and recorded while building this.** (1) `int(x or 0)` is NOT a NaN guard — **NaN is truthy**, so `NaN or 0` evaluates to NaN and `int(NaN)` raises; same idiom as the 2026-07-31 `float(x or 0)` NaN-score incident. Added `_i()`. (2) `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is a **no-op on an existing table**, so a column added after the first run needs an explicit `safe_alter` or every insert dies on `UndefinedColumn` — hit exactly that when the ratio column was added after a dry run had already created the table.
  - **Verification**: 17 tests in `test_event_triggers.py`, negative-controlled twice (reverting the ratio to a raw count fails 2; dropping the point-in-time `fetched_at` filter fails 1). Module run live end-to-end against production (4,181 symbols persisted). Full Python suite **1,598 passed / 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean; TS guard suites 1,946 passed.

- **News modifier removed after a measured sweep, and event triggers surfaced on the recommendation row (2026-08-10, same day).** User asked to act as an expert analyst and, explicitly, to "compare with actual prices... learn from actual data rather than guessing".
  - **THE method correction, applied to my own work.** I first shrank `technicalSignalsService.scoreSignals`'s news modifier by 50% on CONVENTION (matching `shrink_tilt()`/`regime_edge_weight()`), which is a guess dressed as a precedent. Replaced with an actual sweep: back the live 1.15/0.75 out of the stored `signal_score`, re-apply each candidate multiplier, and rank against realised returns (28,613 rows / 41 dates / 1,230 symbols, liquid >=Rs 1cr ADT, winsorised, next-open entry):
    | setting | h5 IC | h15 IC |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1.30 / 0.60 | -0.0068 | +0.0128 |
    | **1.15 / 0.75 (was live)** | -0.0059 | +0.0137 |
    | 1.075 / 0.875 (my shrink) | -0.0050 | +0.0146 |
    | **1.00 / 1.00 (neutral)** | **-0.0050** | **+0.0145** |
    | 0.875 / 1.075 (inverted) | -0.0050 | +0.0146 |
    **IC degrades MONOTONICALLY with tilt strength at BOTH horizons, and neutral/shrunk/inverted are indistinguishable.** So the gain comes entirely from REMOVING the tilt, not reversing it — and my 50% shrink landed on the optimum plateau by luck, not by measurement. Multiplier removed. `NEWS_SENTIMENT_MEANINGFUL_THRESHOLD` deliberately KEPT: it still gates the news-only PERSISTENCE path (a coverage decision, measured separately), not direction. **Do not reinstate a blanket multiplier — the thing most likely to change this answer is splitting genuinely material news (M&A, results shocks) from routine coverage, which the aggregate washes out; build that split and measure it instead.**
  - **Why this matters beyond one constant**: earlier the same session I chose not to invert the screener direction signal at t=-1.21, and not to invert this one at t=-2.03, on consistency grounds. The sweep shows that instinct was right for a different reason than I gave — inverting genuinely does NOT help (inverted ties neutral, it does not beat it). **A monotone response across a parameter sweep is stronger evidence than any single t-stat, and is the cheap check to run before arguing about significance.**
  - **Horizon matters and was initially measured at the wrong one.** The first news measurement used next-day; `technical`-sourced signals are graded at h1/h5/h15 (`signal_outcomes`). Re-measured point-in-time at the real horizons: h1 IC -0.0434 (t=-2.53), h5 -0.0091 (t=-0.52), h15 -0.0015 (t=-0.10). Only h1 is significant, and h1 is not what these signals are held for — which is exactly why "remove" beat both "keep" and "invert".
  - **Event triggers now surface where the decision is made.** `stock_event_triggers` was written but had NO reader — a risk flag living only in a table nobody queries is not a risk flag. `unified_ranker._get_event_triggers()` (degrades to `{}` if the table or job is missing) appends the trigger text to `trade_reasoning`. **Score is untouched by design** — these still are not blend inputs. Live: **670 symbols carry a trigger, 163 recommendation rows carry a disclosure**, and it immediately surfaced the exact case that motivated it — **ELLEN rated `Buy` at 63.4 with `SCREENER_SUPPORT_FADING r1.00`, i.e. 100% of its bullish screener support gone while still being recommended.** Previously invisible.
  - **Verification**: Python **1,598 passed / 0 failed**; TypeScript **8,569 passed across 944 files**; `tsc --noEmit` clean; ranker re-run live end-to-end (1,844 scored, HIGH_VOL) and the disclosures queried back out of the real table.

- **`value_book_to_price` wired into the factor paper screens — the strongest validated factor in the repo had been unwired since the day it was validated (2026-08-10).** `FACTOR_PICKS_SETTING` was a single hardcoded `'factor_picks_momentum_12_1'`, so `persist_factor_picks()` could only ever hold ONE factor — persisting a second silently overwrote the first. That is why the UI showed only `momentum_12_1`, the WEAKER of the two on every measured axis: **t 2.67 vs 2.08, Sharpe 1.47 vs 1.10, turnover 0.28 vs 0.35 (1.65% vs 2.10% annual cost drag), max DD -17.9% vs -19.5%.**
  - **Fix is backward compatible by construction**: `factor_picks_setting(factor)` returns `f'factor_picks_{factor}'`, which reproduces the existing momentum key EXACTLY — no migration, no re-seed, no orphaned row. Pinned by a test.
  - `PERSISTED_PICK_FACTORS = ('value_book_to_price', 'momentum_12_1')`, stronger first, and the same order flows through `screeners.jobs.ts` → `getFactorPaperPicks` → the v6 panel. The scheduled refresh runs the two **sequentially, not in `Promise.all`**: each invocation loads the full ~3M-bar price panel, so parallel would double peak memory for no wall-clock gain on a shared box; each is independently `.catch()`'d so one factor failing still leaves the other's snapshot fresh, and neither may fail canonical quant scoring.
  - **Router shape kept backward compatible** — top-level fields still describe `momentum_12_1` for any existing caller, with `factors` added alongside; the v6 panel falls back to the legacy single-snapshot shape if `factors` is absent.
  - **Both caveats are now IN THE UI, not just in a docstring**: an amber decay warning ("excess has fallen toward zero through 2025-26, neither clears a multiple-testing bar across the 18 factors tested, not the canonical Alpha score and not a buy recommendation") on every card, plus a per-row `THIN` badge below Rs 5cr ADT where the backtest's 25bps/side cost assumption is optimistic. The live value run flagged **29 of its 50 names as sub-Rs 5cr**, so that badge is load-bearing, not decoration.
  - **Live-verified**: `factor_picks_value_book_to_price` persisted (50 picks, asOf 2026-08-07 — value lags momentum's 2026-08-10 because `trendlyne_pb_history` settles later, and each card shows its own `asOf` rather than a shared one). 7 new tests in `factorPicksMultiFactor.test.ts`, **negative-controlled** (reverting the schedule to momentum-only, and reordering the router, each fail exactly one targeted test). Python **1,598 passed / 0 failed**; TypeScript **8,576 passed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **Remaining-fixes sweep: material-news hypothesis REFUTED, event-trigger universe filter + stale-row purge (2026-08-10, session close).**
  - **The "split material news from routine coverage" lead — my own top-ranked remaining research item — was tested and FAILED.** `news_sentiment_items` already carries `impact` (HIGH 18,287 / LOW 14,697 / MEDIUM 1,812) and `category` (EARNINGS 17,800 / GENERAL 10,461 / SECTOR 2,140 / POLICY 1,685 / GLOBAL 1,518 / IPO 1,057 / ORDER_WIN 87 / BUYBACK 48), so no new fetcher was needed. Point-in-time sentiment IC by bucket: **impact=HIGH h5 -0.0018 (t=-0.10) vs impact=LOW h5 -0.0040 (t=-0.18)** — indistinguishable; at h1, HIGH -0.0199 vs LOW -0.0177, also indistinguishable.
