By category at h5: EARNINGS -0.0072, GENERAL -0.0062, SECTOR -0.0201, all nothing. **`impact` does not separate tradeable from routine news at all**, so the removal of the `scoreSignals` news multiplier is now better-supported, not weaker. **Do not re-open this with the existing fields.** The genuinely event-like categories that might still differ (ORDER_WIN 53 linked rows, BUYBACK 23) are far too small for a cross-sectional daily IC and would need a dedicated corporate-event feed, not a re-slice of this table.
  - **One real finding from the same sweep**: `ai_scored=1` (LLM-scored) news gives **h1 IC -0.0647, t=-3.84** vs keyword-scored -0.0232 (t=-1.18) — measuring the tone more accurately produces a *stronger contrarian* next-day signal, consistent with the overreaction/fade story. It collapses to -0.0118 (t=-0.60) by h5, so it is a 1-day effect only and not usable by signals graded at h5/h15. Recorded because it is the one place better NLP demonstrably bought something, just not at a horizon this platform trades.
  - **`event_triggers.py` universe filter**: 1,884 of 4,181 rows (45%) were symbols absent from `nse_stocks`, including the raw numeric id `13510368` this repo has purged from `unified_recommendations` twice. Now mirrors `unified_ranker._restrict_to_tradeable_universe` and degrades unfiltered with a warning if `nse_stocks` is unavailable. Live: 4,181 → 2,297 symbols, **0 not-in-master**.
  - **Stale-row purge, and it was needed immediately.** Adding the filter exposed that the upsert only touches rows it PRODUCES — the 1,884 now-excluded symbols kept their rows and stayed visible to every consumer. Same defect already fixed twice in this repo (`unified_recommendations`, `intraday_outcome_resolver`); it recurs because an `ON CONFLICT` upsert is the natural thing to write and the deletion half is invisible until a gate narrows the output. **Any table written as "today's full recomputation" needs a purge of rows the run did not produce, not just an upsert.** Live: purged 1,884; ranker disclosures dropped 670 → 208 symbols accordingly.
  - **Item deliberately NOT actioned: wiring tenure/exit as ML features.** `stock_event_triggers` accumulating daily IS the mechanism for letting them earn a weight; `factor_edge.py` needs 20 distinct dates and has 1. Adding them to `technical_signals` now would give a 30-overlapping-day signal a permanent seat before it has been validated, which is precisely the pattern this repo keeps having to unwind. Re-check once the table has ~2 months.
  - **Verification**: Python **1,598 passed / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, ranker re-run live end-to-end (1,842 scored, HIGH_VOL), filter and purge both queried back out of production.
- **All 8 trading-logic-review findings fixed and live-verified against production (2026-08-10).** The review above was source-path verified only; this pass fixed each defect and then measured the before/after on the real `unified_recommendations` table, which is what turned three of them from "structurally possible" into "happening right now, at this scale". Live BEFORE state on the latest snapshot: **12 `Buy` rows at scores 13.5-24.7** and 151 more at 25-45 (finding #2 in the flesh); **1 `Sell` at `A_HIGH`** (score 67.1) plus 56 `Sell` at `B_MEDIUM` (45-62), while **every single correct `Strong Sell` was capped at `C_LOW`** — conviction ran exactly backwards on the short side (finding #1); and 231 `Hold` rows carrying an `A_HIGH`/`B_MEDIUM` tier despite having no direction at all.
  - **#1/#2 (conviction direction + agreement floor).** New `_directional_strength(score, classification)` is the single source of truth for "how strongly does the score support the direction THIS row claims" — bullish reads `score`, bearish reads `100-score`, Hold has none. `_conviction` now **requires** `classification` (deliberately no default: a default silently reinstates the bullish-only reading, and the one caller that would have inherited it, `intraday_ranker.py`, is fixed at the same time rather than left on a compatibility shim). `DIRECTIONAL_AGREEMENT_FLOOR = 45.0` is **one** number mirrored around 50 (Buy needs `>= floor`, Sell needs `<= 100-floor`) so the long and short sides cannot drift apart — an asymmetry between them is the exact bug class being fixed, so it is now structurally impossible rather than a convention two constants have to keep agreeing on.
  - **#3 (ordering).** Every ordinary score multiplier — quality gate, red-flag, high-vol, factor-crowding — now runs BEFORE the single `_classify` call, and nothing touches `unified` after it; the two categorical vetoes apply their multiplier there and their `veto_classification()` demotion after, which is the only label change permitted post-classification. **This was not cosmetic: `Strong Buy` fell 55 -> 19 live.** Factor-crowding fires on 78% of names, so 36 rows had been carrying a `Strong Buy` label earned at a pre-discount score they no longer had by the time the row was persisted — and `_conviction` was reading the post-multiplier score while the label came from the pre-multiplier one, so the two columns were describing different numbers on every discounted row.
  - **#6/#7 collapsed into ONE function rather than two mechanisms.** `size_confidence_multiplier(strength, coverage)` is bounded `[0.5, 1.0]` and monotonic in both inputs, so it can only ever SHRINK a position: the part of it that has not been backtested can reduce risk but can never inflate a position above the validated `bet/vol` core. That property is what makes it safe to ship before the sizing-delta backtest the review asks for. Coverage enters the same function, which is the "haircut, not a blind cutoff" the review specifies for #7. Live: fires on 78.0% of rows, counted as `size_confidence_haircut` alongside the existing layer counters so it is measurable rather than assumed.
  - **#4 (staleness).** `load_price_panel` now emits TWO eligibility masks: `signal_eligible` (factor + liquidity known at the close — true on the newest bar) and `eligible` (also needs the next open, so **never** true on the newest bar). `todays_picks` selects on the former, `run_backtest` keeps using the latter. Conflating them is the whole defect: a post-close job could only ever return the previous session, whose entry open had traded hours earlier. `picks_entry_state()` now travels with the payload as `entryStatus`/`entrySession`, the zod schema **defaults a pre-fix snapshot to `entry_passed`** (degrade pessimistic, never silently fresh), and the V6 card renders an expired list dimmed and labelled EXPIRED instead of showing an `asOf` date that reads identically to a live one. Live-verified with the real scheduled command: `asOf 2026-08-10, entryStatus pending_next_open` — today's close, entry still ahead.
  - **#5 (missing exits).** Strategy and benchmark now use ONE figure (`MISSING_EXIT_PCT = -100.0`, sweepable via `--missing-exit-pct`), and `missing_exits` is reported in every summary so the assumption's weight is visible. The deeper problem was not that 0% was optimistic, it was that **two adjacent statements used two different conventions for the same event**: the strategy booked the delisted name flat while `.dropna()` removed it from the comparison universe two lines later — i.e. the benchmark was quietly survivorship-filtered while the strategy was not. The write-off is deliberately applied AFTER `RETURN_CLAMP_PCT`, since clamping a -100% write-off to -50% would restore the optimism through the back door.
  - **#8 (value restatement).** `PROVISIONAL_FACTORS` names the four value factors; `--persist-picks` refuses them without `--allow-provisional`, and `todays_picks` warns on any read. Blocks the specific failure the review names (a backfilled series' t-statistic being taken at face value) without pretending the sensitivity has been quantified.
  - **A metric that changed meaning mid-verification, worth remembering.** The query used to find the bug — `classification IN ('Sell','Strong Sell') AND conviction_level IN ('S_ELITE','A_HIGH')` — returned 1 before the fix and **399 after**, which reads like a catastrophic regression and is in fact the fix working: on a direction-aware scale, `S_ELITE` on a Sell now means *strongly bearish*, which is correct. Re-asking it properly (recompute `_conviction(score, classification)` for all 1,843 live rows and diff) gives **0 disagreements**, with S_ELITE shorts topping out at score 20.0 and B_MEDIUM shorts starting at 35.1 — a clean mirror of the long ladder. **When a fix changes what a column MEANS, the pre-fix detection query does not survive it; re-derive the check from the new invariant instead of reading the old number.**
  - **Verification**: 23 new tests across `test_ranker_direction_invariants.py` and `test_factor_backtest_conventions.py`, every one **negative-controlled** — reverting each of the 7 source changes in turn was confirmed to fail the specific named tests (`_conviction` bullish-only -> 4 fail, Buy floor -> 2, Sell ceiling -> 3, coverage term -> 2, missing-exit write-off -> 2, benchmark `dropna` -> 1, `eligible` selection -> 2), then restored. 4 pre-existing tests encoded the old behaviour and were updated with the reason recorded inline, not silently flipped. Full suites: Python 1,570 passed (1 pre-existing unrelated failure — `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_reversal_features.py`'s `[us]`/`[ns]` fixture can no longer reproduce the dtype mismatch it guards under this pandas version, and says so in its own assertion message), TypeScript 8,572 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Live post-fix invariants on production: 0 Buys below the floor, 0 Sells above the ceiling, 0 Holds carrying a conviction tier, 0 sized non-Buy rows, 0 conviction/direction disagreements across all 1,843 rows.
  - **Follow-up: the one pre-existing failure flagged above is also fixed.** `test_live_screener_ml_ranker_reversal_features.py`'s `[us]`-vs-`[ns]` merge_asof test built its dtype mismatch by *relying on pandas to infer* one resolution for a scalar broadcast and another for `pd.to_datetime` — then asserted the two differed as a setup check. Which resolution pandas infers is a version-dependent detail; when it changed (both sides now come out `[us]`) that setup assert fired and **the test had silently stopped exercising the guard it exists to protect**. Rewritten to pin both sides explicitly with `.astype("datetime64[<unit>, UTC]")` and parametrized over both orderings, so the condition is constructed rather than hoped for; the real scalar-broadcast shape is kept as documentation of `score()`'s actual construction. Negative-controlled: removing `_asof_reversal_for_date`'s `.astype(b['datetime'].dtype)` now fails both parametrizations with a `MergeError`, which it could not do before. Full Python suite: **1,572 passed, 0 failed**. **Standing lesson: a test that depends on a library's inferred default to manufacture its own precondition will pass, then silently stop testing anything, and only a guard assert makes that visible at all — construct the condition explicitly.**

- **Signal-accuracy review: the RL gate excluded 54% of the universe on a bare sign test, and has NO measured edge in any form (2026-08-10).** Ran the new `/signal-accuracy-review` command (`.claude/commands/signal-accuracy-review.md`, added same session — encodes the measurement rules and recurring bug classes from this file so a future pass doesn't re-derive them). Full detail in the [rl_gate_significance_2026_08_10](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/rl_gate_significance_2026_08_10.md) memory. Summary:
  - **THE finding.** `unified_ranker._passes_rl_gate` tested `avg_r < 0` — a bare SIGN test. The 2026-08-06 KECL fix added `MIN_RL_GATE_SAMPLES=5` (a *count* floor) but no *significance* test, so the defect survived in a new form: a symbol averaging **−0.17% over 22 resolved outcomes (t=−0.19)** was removed from the ranked universe exactly as hard as one losing 8% a trade. Live: **519 of 958 eligible symbols (54%) gated out, 359 (69%) on statistical noise.** Confirmed cost, not theoretical — **SBCL** sat outside the universe from 2026-07-10 on a −0.174%/22-sample average, then was the **top liquid gainer on 2026-08-10 at +19.08%** (found via the standing top-gainers reverse-engineering practice, tracing an absent symbol end-to-end to its exact drop point).
  - **The counterfactual says the gate earns nothing.** Point-in-time (only outcomes *resolved* before each evaluation date), 37 dates, liquid ≥₹1cr ADT, winsorised, next-open entry, 5d forward, excluded-minus-kept: **all exclusions +0.098% (t=1.22, excluded won 51% of dates); t≤−2 "evidence" exclusions +0.148% (t=1.23); noise exclusions +0.073% (t=0.77).** Excluded symbols do not underperform in *either* form — the premise (a poor trailing record predicts poor forward returns) is unsupported by this platform's own data. Fixed with `RL_GATE_MAX_T = -2.0` (exclude only on a *significant* negative): **519 → 160 exclusions, 359 recovered**, new excluded set a strict **subset** of the old so it can only widen the universe and never newly excludes. Deliberately did NOT delete the gate — t=1.23 is not evidence it helps, but removing a risk control on a non-significant result is the same over-reach inverted.
  - **Two traps, both generalising.** (1) **`STDDEV` is Postgres-only** — the first version used SQL `STDDEV`, which in the SQLite dev/test fallback fails the whole query, returns `{}` from `_get_rl_gate_map`, and **silently disables the gate entirely** rather than erroring; now derived from portable `SUM`/`SUM(x²)`/`COUNT`. Same class as the documented `date('now')`/multi-word-cast dialect bugs, and caught only by running the existing tests. (2) **`sd == 0` is NOT "cannot establish significance"** — a perfectly consistent negative is *maximally* significant (t→−∞); my first version treated it as untestable and let it pass, which would have disabled the gate for its single most clear-cut case. Caught by *reading* an existing fixture (5 samples all at −8.0), not by running it.
  - **A pooling artifact I nearly shipped as a finding.** Six hand-picked dates pooled showed excluded +0.798% vs kept +0.418% — one step from reporting "the gate is INVERTED". Per-date across 37 dates: +0.098%, t=1.22, 51% of dates. **Third recorded time pooling flipped/inflated a conclusion in this repo** (see `cs_ranker._mean_daily_ic`, the screener-direction measurement). Standing rule reaffirmed: per-date then average, never pooled — and when a dramatic pooled number disagrees with per-date numbers, it is the pooled number that is wrong.
  - **Branch-state finding, important and separate.** `origin/main` (`0df59df`) still has the **bullish-only `_conviction(score)`** and applies **3 of 4 score multipliers AFTER `_classify`** (`_classify` at line 1864; `RED_FLAG_VETO_MULT` 1871, `HIGH_VOL_VETO_MULT` 1881, `crowd_mult` 1890) — so a Strong Buy can be persisted with a score below its own label threshold, and the bullish-only conviction then reads that post-multiplier score backwards on every Sell. All six direction/label/sizing/ordering fixes exist **only** on `merge/pr61-into-main` (`7a11ddc`, pushed to `origin/fix/trading-logic-review-8-findings`). Production runs that branch and is fine; `origin/main` is not. **Merge PR61 before trusting any "already fixed" claim about ranker direction/conviction/ordering read from a `main` checkout.**
  - **Accuracy state, measured.** Per-date 5d excess vs liquid universe across 32 usable ranker dates: Strong Buy +0.46% (t=1.21) > Buy +0.11% (t=1.27) > Hold −0.19% (t=−3.41) — bullish ladder monotone and correctly ordered, nothing significant. Bearish side historically carries **zero** information (Sell +0.008%/t=0.11, Strong Sell +0.007%/t=0.07), though on 08-10 alone the new score-based direction gave Sell −0.42% vs universe −0.10%, the right sign. `unified_score` per-date rank IC +0.0104 (t=1.30, 30 dates). Zero NaN in `win_probability`/`calibrated_win_probability`/`breakout_probability` over 30 days, so `intraday_ranker.py:278`'s `float(x or 0)` on a model-output column is **latent, not live** (and intraday emission is gated closed) — flagged, not fixed.
  - **Measurement caveat worth carrying forward: `unified_recommendations.computed_at` is a bare TEXT date with no time component**, and the upsert key is `(symbol, computed_at)` — so a manual same-day re-run silently overwrites that day's 07:30 cron row. Recent dates in any backtest of this table may reflect post-close re-runs, which would flatter them. Cannot be detected from the data; assume it for dates this file records as having been manually re-ranked.
  - **Verification**: commit `10cc327` on `merge/pr61-into-main`. 210 ranker tests (incl. PR61's own `test_ranker_direction_invariants.py`) + full 349-test `src/server/__tests__/` suite pass on the merged tree; **negative-controlled** (reverting the significance test fails the new insignificant-negative test, on both the worktree and the PR61 tree); patch trial-applied non-destructively before landing; live-run against production Postgres reproduces 519→160 / 359 recovered / SBCL recovered / subset property. No `pm2 restart` needed — `runPython()` re-reads `.py` per spawn, so the next 07:30 ranker run picks it up.
  - **Same session, open-item closeout.** (1) **PR #51 (dependabot "Bump esbuild 0.25.12 → 0.28.2") CLOSED, not merged.** Its title names a transitive dep; the actual diff against current main is `vite ^6.2.3 → ^8.2.1` (TWO majors) and `vitest ^3.2.7 → ^4.1.10` (one major) — the exact upgrade this repo already declined deliberately on 2026-07-23 and 2026-08-04 (vitest 4 risks the suite's custom `singleFork` pool config; dev-tooling only, never shipped). Decisive: **`npm audit` on current main reports 0 vulnerabilities at every severity** — the advisory is already resolved via the existing `overrides.tsx.esbuild: ^0.28.1` (lockfile resolves esbuild 0.28.2), so the PR carried zero remaining security value against a real toolchain-major risk. Its `build-test` failures were also NOT caused by the bump: `bedrockService.test.ts` (5/5) and `pgClient.test.ts` (2/6) fail at its stale 2026-08-09 base but **both pass on current main** (verified locally, 132 tests green) — they were fixed by work merged since. **Before treating any dependabot PR here as a routine patch bump, diff its package.json against current main and re-run `npm audit`** — the title can understate the change by two major versions and the advisory may already be closed.
  - (2) **The Buy floor is an ABSOLUTE cut on a non-stationary score scale — made observable, deliberately NOT recalibrated.** Live: `DIRECTIONLESS_BUY_FLOOR=70` selected **15.6% of the universe on 08-09 and 1.2% on 08-10** (612 → 22 actionable buys, a 96% collapse). **PR61 did not cause it** — hypothesis "factor-crowding now applies before `_classify`" was measured and **refuted** (fired on only 4.3% of rows; removing it changes the count 22 → 22). Per-engine diff found the real cause: `dl_score` avg **78.5 → 28.1** (LSTM v4→v3 rollback, v4 was 70% saturated) and `ml_score` avg **70.8 → 47.9** (the `_get_ml_scores` edge adjustment) — two independent, *correct* same-day fixes that each deflated an inflated engine, dropping `unified_score` avg 54.9 → 40.6 underneath an unchanged threshold. **Did not recalibrate the constant or switch to a percentile**, because measured per-date over the 30 dates with forward returns **no score cut has significant edge**: top-10 −0.242%/t=−0.57, top-20 +0.102%/t=0.33, top-50 −0.215%/t=−1.02, top-100 −0.001%/t=−0.01, top-200 +0.160%/t=1.75 — non-monotone, and the *very top* of the distribution is negative. Nothing supports 70, 60, or any percentile, so any change would be a guess. Added `_report_buy_floor_selectivity()` + `BUY_FLOOR_EXPECTED_SELECTIVITY=(0.03,0.40)` instead: pure reporting, never touches a score/label/size, logs the cleared fraction every run and warns when it leaves the band, pointing at engine drift rather than "the market changed". Live-verified against the real stored rows — silent at 32.7% (08-09), fires at 1.2% (08-10). **Standing lesson: an absolute threshold on a blended score is only as stable as every engine feeding it; fixing ONE engine silently re-tunes every absolute cut downstream, and the symptom shows up as a recommendation-volume collapse, not as an error.** Revisit the floor itself only when enough forward history exists to measure a cut that actually discriminates.
  - **A measurement I discarded rather than built on.** A `percent_rank()` sweep reported "235 names in the top 1%" of ~1068/date. Rather than reason around it I checked the tie structure — scores are 82-85% distinct with the largest tie at 0.2% of rows, so that sweep was simply wrong and was thrown away in favour of an unambiguous `row_number()` top-N cut. **A percentile/window result that implies impossible bucket sizes is a bug in the query, not a property of the data — verify bucket sizes before trusting any percentile-based calibration.**

- **Signal-logic accuracy review: 13,505 fabricated-label rows found in the canonical ranking table, purged; 2 latent NaN holes closed in `intraday_ranker.py` (2026-08-10).** Full detail in the [nan_residue_canonical_table_2026_08_10](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/nan_residue_canonical_table_2026_08_10.md) memory. All figures from read-only live-Postgres measurement (per-date, winsorised, ≥₹1cr ADT, next-day-OPEN entry, `is_suspect` excluded).
  - **THE finding — a source-level NaN fix does not clean the rows the bug already wrote.** `unified_ranker.run()` purges rows it did not produce, **but only for the `computed_at` it is currently writing**, so historical dates keep their poisoned rows forever. **13,505 rows** with a non-finite `unified_score` but a real `Buy`/`Sell`/`Hold` label (3,815/5,479/4,211) survived the 2026-07-31 `_finite_engine_map` fix, spanning 2026-07-02→07-30. **Aggregate poisoning read ~42% of rows/day, but the tradeable slice was ~100%**: joined against ≥₹1cr ADT, the surviving non-NaN rows were 4 liquid names of 821 on 07-21, 9 of 2,311 on 07-30, versus 960 on the clean 07-27 control. Mechanism: the NaN entered via `dl_score`, and DL coverage *is* the liquid universe — illiquid names had no `dl_score`, so `_blend` renormalised without it and stayed finite. **Always decompose a "% of rows affected" figure by liquidity before believing it.**
  - **It silently corrupted measurement, which is how it surfaced.** Per-date rank IC of `unified_score` measured **−0.0121 (t=−0.31)** before the purge and **+0.0107 (t=+1.34)** after — the point estimate *changed sign*. Neither is significant, so the standing "no measurable edge" conclusion is unchanged, but every number computed over this table before today is untrustworthy, **including this repo's own zero-edge audit**. Tell-tale: pandas `.corr()` drops NaN pairs, so poisoned dates returned suspiciously exact ICs (0.500, −0.548) over ~1,000 rows. Live consumer that would act on it: `high_flyer_retrospective.py` reads `MAX(computed_at) < day` matching `classification LIKE '%Buy%'`, so a `--backfill` into that window credits predictions the system never made, and it feeds learned precursor lifts.
  - **Fixed** via a new `data_integrity_repair.py --nan-recommendations` task (idempotent, dry-run aware). Deletes rather than nulls: a row with no valid score is not a recommendation, and a missing symbol on a historical date is honest where a fabricated `Buy` is not. Live-verified: 13,505 deleted, **0 non-finite rows remain**, and `MAX`/`STDDEV` are real numbers again on all 9 previously-poisoned dates.
  - **Two traps pinned by tests, both of which produce a silently-useless fix.** (1) **`x != x` does NOT detect NaN in Postgres** — Postgres deliberately defines `NaN = NaN` as TRUE so btree ordering is total, so the portable-looking IEEE self-inequality test matches *nothing* and reports "none found — clean". That exact wrong implementation is the negative control; do not "simplify" the cast-to-text form back into it. (2) **SQLite cannot represent NaN** (coerces to NULL on insert), so a SQLite-backed test passes against the *unfixed* code. The test uses a throwaway Postgres schema shadowed via `search_path`, and **must pin `USE_POSTGRES` in the fixture** — `use_postgres()` reads the env fresh per call, and the tests passed in isolation but FAILED in the full suite until pinned. Same env-pollution class already documented for `dbAsync.ts`.
  - **`intraday_ranker.py`: 2 remaining `float(x or 0)` holes on model-output columns closed** (`breakout_probability`, `sentiment_score`). NaN is truthy, so `float(nan or 0)` is `nan` — one non-finite `sentiment_score` would make the *mean* NaN for every symbol tagged on that article, not merely skew it. Guards **skip rather than coerce to 0**, matching `_finite_engine_map`'s "treat as absent" semantics: coercing would fabricate the *worst possible* score. Breakout now falls through to an older finite row rather than letting a NaN newest row consume the symbol's slot. Latent, not live (0 NaN across 13,162 `technical_signals` rows; both emission gates closed at 0 resolved trades) — 6 negative-controlled tests are the only thing standing between a future NaN and a repeat.
  - **Verified already-correct, not re-litigated** (so a future pass doesn't redo it): all 12 model files carry a promotion gate — `regime_detector` via `MIN_SELF_TRANSITION=0.5` and `online_learner` via `ONLINE_REGRESSION_TOLERANCE`, both of which a naive grep reports as missing; DL saturation is 0.203 on v3 versus 0.706 on the rolled-back v4, so the gate and rollback are live; `event_triggers.py` still carries the dual `published_at`+`fetched_at` point-in-time filter; no `%s` SQL placeholders, no multi-word `::casts`, no `shuffle=True` on time-series CV. **PR61's invariants hold on live data** (0 Buys below floor, 0 Sells above ceiling, 0 sized/geometry non-Buy rows, 0 `risk_reward<1`), and direction-aware conviction is confirmed working — `Sell/A_HIGH` sits at scores 20–30 and `Strong Sell/S_ELITE` at 3.5–20, a clean mirror of the long ladder. My own first read flagged "1,347 Holds carrying a conviction tier" as a violation; they all carry `D_MARGINAL`, which is correct.
  - **The RL-gate significance fix (commit `10cc327`) is correct in code but had never reached the persisted table.** Traced live end-to-end: 7 of the 8 absent top-movers pass every gate with full 7-engine coverage (only DMCC legitimately excludes at t=−2.64), and current code would score **2,202** symbols against the **1,842** actually persisted for 08-10 — the last run that wrote that snapshot predates the 22:31 IST commit. **SBCL, the very symbol CLAUDE.md cites as recovered (+19.08%, t=−0.19), is still absent from the live table.** Self-resolving at the next 07:30 cron since `runPython` re-reads `.py` per spawn; no fix applied, but do not read "live-verified" on a ranker change as meaning the *data* reflects it — re-query the table.
  - **Measurement caveat worth carrying forward:** `unified_recommendations.computed_at` is a bare TEXT date, so the **08-10 snapshot is a post-close manual re-run, not the 07:30 pre-market row** — grading the 08-10 session against it would be look-ahead. The honest pre-move signal for a session is the *previous* date's snapshot. Under that (pre-PR61) logic the 15 worst losers of 2026-08-10 carried 7 `Buy` labels, 2 of them `Strong Buy`/`A_HIGH` — consistent with the already-retired screener-direction rule, not a new finding.
  - **Also found, not fixed:** `flyer_classifier.py` is scheduled nowhere, so `technical_signals.flyer_probability` is populated on exactly one date (2,193 rows on 08-07, zero on every other day) — an unscheduled model writing an ML feature column. `delivery_trend_30d` remains 0% covered on every date despite `nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py` already pulling official delivery data (a wiring gap CLAUDE.md already flags).
  - **Verification**: 11 new tests, each negative-controlled by reverting the fix and confirming the exact expected failures (2 fail on the broken Postgres NaN test, 5 on the restored `or 0` idiom) before restoring. Full Python suite **1,642 passed / 184 skipped / 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean; no TS touched so no `pm2 restart` needed, no migration involved. Working tree contained only this session's 4 files — no concurrent-session interference.

- **Follow-up (2026-08-10/11): the two items the accuracy review left open are closed — `unified_recommendations` is now gradeable, and `flyer_classifier.py` was verdicted rather than scheduled.**
  - **THE canonical table was ungradeable, and the fix is a column not a key change.** `computed_at` is a bare TEXT DATE and the upsert key is `(symbol, computed_at)`, so a manual same-day re-run silently replaced that morning's 07:30 IST cron row with **no trace at all** — a genuine pre-market signal and a post-close re-run were indistinguishable, which means grading any session against its own date's row could silently be look-ahead. New `generated_at TIMESTAMPTZ` (migration `1786800000000_unified-recommendations-generated-at.sql`, **applied via `npm run migrate:up`**, mirrored into `db.ts` as `runMigration('078_...')` for the SQLite fallback) records the wall-clock instant of the producing run. **Deliberately NOT part of the key** — putting it there would create multiple snapshots per day and change what "the latest snapshot" means for every consumer of the canonical table. Deliberately **overwritten** on conflict: the row IS the later run's output, so keeping the first run's timestamp would label a post-close re-run as pre-market, i.e. the exact defect. Existing rows left NULL on purpose — their true generation time is unknown, and backfilling `computed_at::timestamptz` would fabricate a precise-looking 00:00 that measurement code would then trust. **The intended filter is in the column COMMENT so nobody re-derives it: `generated_at < computed_at 03:45 UTC` (NSE opens 09:15 IST = 03:45 UTC).** Live-verified: a full ranker run stamped all 2,201 rows with one timestamp, and the detector correctly classifies **all 2,201 as contaminated** (it genuinely was a post-close re-run) where previously that was unknowable.
  - **That same run also finally applied the RL-gate significance fix to the DATA** — 1,842 → **2,201 rows scored**, with SBCL/PIXTRANS/JGCHEM recovered as `Hold` (passing a gate is not being bullish — exactly the "validate the fix doesn't over-correct" check the practice memory requires) and DMCC correctly still excluded at t=−2.64.
  - **`flyer_classifier.py`: the answer was NOT to schedule it.** It is advisory-only by design, awaiting demonstrated edge — and the author had already left a read-only go/no-go script. Running it (`scripts/flyer_return_alpha_backtest.py`, 766,199 walk-forward out-of-sample rows over 475 dates) returned a decisive **NO EDGE, significantly negative**: cross-sectional Spearman IC **−0.0410 (t=−9.02, 473 days)**, top-20 basket vs universe **−0.2004%/day (t=−5.36)** at 0.15% cost, and negative even at **zero** cost (−0.0504%/day). **Its purged-OOF AUC is 0.8097 and held-out test AUC 0.8087 — an AUC that reads as excellent and is a trap**: it measures who flies, not when, reproducing exactly the incidence-not-timing failure the 2026-07-10 walk-forward already found in its heuristic predecessor. Verdict written into the file's own docstring next to the AUC (so the AUC can never be read alone), and persisted to `app_settings.flyer_classifier_backtest`. **Left unscheduled on purpose, pinned by a test** that fails if it ever appears in `queues.ts`/`jobs/*.ts`. *Method note: the backtest's log lines are prefixed `[Breakout]` because it reuses `breakout_classifier.evaluate_purged_cv`; confirm it consumes `flyer_classifier.load_labeled_features` before doubting which model was scored.*
  - **The real bug behind the flyer symptom — `NEVER_FILL` enumerated only 3 of the 7 model-output columns.** `densify_feature_matrix.NEVER_FILL` exists precisely so a stale model output is never carried forward and presented as a prediction that was never made, but it listed only `win_probability`/`calibrated_win_probability`/`breakout_probability`. **`flyer_probability`, `movement_probability`, `pead_score` and `cs_score` were all unprotected** — so a day any of those producers failed, its last prediction would be smeared forward up to `MAX_FILL_DAYS` and read as fresh. `flyer_probability` was the live candidate (populated on exactly ONE date, and now known to be anti-predictive). **Verified zero behavioural change before adding**: `movement_probability` and `cs_score` are already at 100% coverage from their own daily jobs and `pead_score`'s ~76% is its real producer coverage (PEAD only applies to names with a recent earnings print), so there was nothing for the filler to have been doing — this is a guard, not a correction. `cs_score` mattered most to check, since it feeds the ranker's `cs` engine and shrinking its coverage would have changed rankings.
  - **Verification**: 13 new tests, **each of the 4 fixes negative-controlled** (drop `generated_at` from the conflict clause → 1 fail; strip the 4 columns from `NEVER_FILL` → 4 fails; remove the flyer verdict → 1 fail; simulate scheduling flyer → 1 fail), then restored. Fixed genuine fixture drift in `test_unified_ranker.py` (its SQLite table predated the column — 13 failures that were fixture staleness, not a defect). Full Python suite **1,655 passed / 184 skipped / 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run schema:regen` + `schema:drift` → **"Schema clean"** (193 tables); `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy the `db.ts` change, restarted online with no migration errors.

- **Phase 1 of a full-stack audit: the memory/rule architecture was already built and was never committed; the gate meant to enforce it read prose (2026-08-11).** Task was "rewrite CLAUDE.md for progressive disclosure, split rules into modules, add verification hooks." All three already existed in the working tree. The repeat-failure cause was elsewhere, in five defects.
  - **`git ls-files .claude` returned exactly 2 files** (`launch.json`, `settings.json`). `.claude/rules/` (4 files), `.claude/hooks/` (2), `.claude/commands/` (1) and `docs/session-log.md` were untracked and **not gitignored** — just never committed, along with the finished 1152→114-line `CLAUDE.md` rewrite. So the committed `CLAUDE.md` routed every session to four rule files that **did not exist in any clone, CI run, cloud session, or worktree**; **there are 12 worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` each carrying its own divergent `CLAUDE.md`** — 26, 809, 863, 941, 990, 992, 992, 992, 993, 1008, 1110 lines, not one of them the committed 114-line version, and none of them containing `.claude/rules/` at all. A session started in any worktree reads a different set of project instructions. **This is the single largest contributor to rediscovering the same constraints — the architecture was real but local.** Committed as `ab36f3e`. The worktree copies were deliberately left alone: each belongs to an in-progress concurrent session, and overwriting their instructions mid-task is exactly the interference this repo's own commit-by-explicit-path convention exists to prevent.
  - **CI never ran the ranker's own tests.** `pytest src/server/tests/` was the job; `src/server/__tests__/` holds **14 `.py` files / 354 tests** — the entire `unified_ranker` (6 files), `ml_ensemble`, `factor_backtest` (2), `outcome_resolver`, `ranker_direction_invariants`, `event_triggers`, `relative_strength`, `screener_sentiment_domain` suite. Verified there is **no `pytest.ini`/`pyproject.toml`/`setup.cfg` `testpaths` and no root `conftest.py`**, so nothing collected them: the regression tests written to lock in the last four merged ranker fixes (NaN residue, RL-gate significance, ranker direction, unrankable `unified_score`) were enforced only by a human remembering. All 354 pass; combined invocation is **1,655 passed / 198 skipped** despite 3 duplicate basenames across the two dirs. CI and `CLAUDE.md`'s Definition of Done now carry the **identical command string** so they cannot drift silently again.
  - **THE enforcement defect: `verify-gate.mjs` regexed the raw transcript for `/pytest/`, `/vitest/`, `/tsc --noEmit/`.** That tests whether a command was *mentioned*, not whether it *passed* — the sentence "I ran pytest, everything passes" satisfied a Stop hook whose entire purpose is catching exactly that claim. **Demonstrated, not assumed**: ran the old logic against a prose-only transcript, it returns ALLOW. Rewritten to pair each `tool_use` block against its `tool_result` and require `is_error !== true`. Tests were initially a standalone assert script, which **broke the vitest suite** (vitest globs `**/*.test.mjs`); converted to a real vitest suite instead of renaming it out of the way — so the gate is now itself gated by `npm test` rather than by someone remembering to run it, which is the same failure mode one level up.
  - **Documented ≠ enforced, quantified.** `recurring-bugs.md` carries recurrence counts (11, 10, 6, 4, 4, 3, 2) that were **all recorded after the class was already documented there**; only 3 of ~20 classes had an executable check. Added two: `x != x` as a NaN test **in SQL context only** (Postgres defines `NaN = NaN` TRUE, so it silently reports clean), and multi-word `::double precision` casts. **Both matched zero real occurrences repo-wide when added — they are regression guards, not a backlog.** Both guards are load-bearing and were negative-controlled: dropping the SQL-context requirement flags the ~10 *legitimate* Python-level `f != f` NaN checks, and dropping the comment/docstring skip flags the two comments that warn against these very patterns.
  - **A third candidate check was measured and rejected.** `float(x or 0)` on a possibly-NaN column is a real class, but it matches **50 sites** repo-wide and inspection showed most are legitimate `None`→0 on DB aggregates (`float(hit_rate or 0)`, `float(r['amount'] or 0.0)`). **A check that is ~90% false positive gets ignored within a week and then protects nothing while looking like it does** — which is the exact failure this phase exists to fix. Left unautomated, with the reason written into the script so it isn't re-proposed. `recurring-bugs.md` now marks (🤖) which classes are machine-enforced and which rely on being read.
  - **Fixed the checker's own false positive**, since a checker CI blocks on cannot cry wolf: `feature_coverage_gate.py:64`'s `""" % int(lookback_days)` is Python interpolation, not a placeholder bug, and the line-start-only guard missed that shape. Kept the widening narrow to the triple-quote spelling on purpose — matching any closing quote would swallow a genuine placeholder in a query containing a quoted `'%s'` LIKE pattern. **412 files now scan clean; 28 checker tests pass.**
  - **Memory index consolidated 109 files → 32** (index 19,450 → 5,162 bytes, 106 → 32 entries — it is loaded in full every session, so it was ~5k tokens of mostly-irrelevant one-liners defeating the progressive disclosure it sits on top of). One day, 2026-08-10, had produced **seven** separate files. **Merged by concatenation, never summary** — each of the 89 retired entries survives verbatim as a `##` section, verified by word count (80,651 → 80,642 across bodies; the 9-word delta is stripped frontmatter). Wikilinks repointed programmatically: **0 broken by the merge**, and the **32 that were already broken before it** (kebab-case variants, plus 5 targets like `project_architecture` that never existed) were also resolved or de-linked. Usable sample is 30 dates at 1d, 26 at 5d, **10 at 21d** — the monthly horizon is barely measurable at all.
  - **THE finding — 26,375 of 85,442 rows (31%) are recommendations on instruments the platform has no price data for.** 2,498 distinct symbols appear nowhere in `stock_ohlcv` on any date, **more ghost symbols than the 2,450 real ones**, and **3,774 carry an actionable Buy/Sell label** rather than Hold. Present on every single date (640-2,287/date), so this is structural, not an incident. They are genuine NSE microcaps (REXPRO, KONSTELEC, SWADPOL, 4THDIM, LATIMMETAL) outside the price universe — *not* the 2026-07-23 URL-as-symbol corruption, which was checked and ruled out (0 url-ish symbols, 0 lowercase, all match `^[A-Z0-9&*-]+$`). Score degeneracy is visible on them: 29.56 and 32.32 repeat across unrelated symbols, the signature of component engines returning a constant where they have no data. These rows can never be graded, never be traded, and consume ranking slots.
  - **Four of 32 snapshots are stamped to non-trading days** — 2026-07-05 (Sun), 07-12 (Sun), 07-25 (Sat), 08-09 (Sun), **9,096 rows** dated to days the market was shut. This is the `date.today()`-as-write-target class that `.claude/rules/recurring-bugs.md` already documents at 11 recurrences, still live in the ranker's own `computed_at`.

On the tail measure, the share of the 50 biggest losers carrying a Sell runs **-14.9pp below the universe base rate (t=-9.45, 24 dates)**. Both statistics agree in sign; the tail number is dramatic and the mean number is 8bp, and **both are reported because they answer different questions**. Named, 2026-08-10 graded against the 08-07 snapshot: of the top 12 gainers **4 were rated Sell** (COMSYN +17.5%, DALMIASUG +9.8%, SIMPLEXINF +9.5%, THOMASCOTT +9.4%) and 2 absent (SBCL +19.1%, DMCC +10.3% — SBCL still carried `technical_signals` features, so this is the RL-gate exclusion, and the recovery recorded on 08-10 had not reached the 08-07 snapshot that graded this session); of the 12 biggest losers **7 were rated Buy** (GREENLAM -12.0%, ATULAUTO -11.3%, AMBIKCO -10.8%, ADVENZYMES -8.7%, BHARATFORG -8.2%, SOTL -7.9%, RAIN -7.4%). One genuine hit: JGCHEM, Strong Buy / A_HIGH, +10.6%.
  - **No factor group is missing, and my own first-pass conclusion that several were had to be discarded.** ~40 features across all six groups the audit named, per-date AUC for discriminating next-session top-50 gainers. First pass looked like a discovery — `hv_20d` 0.667, `breakout_probability` 0.659, `bb_width` 0.622, `rs_vs_sector_21d` 0.565, all p<0.001. **The symmetric control killed it: every one predicts the top-50 LOSERS at least as well** (0.704, 0.705, 0.662, 0.628 respectively), and the direction AUC (gainers vs losers, isolating sign from magnitude) is **below 0.5 for all of them**, several significantly (t=-2.26 to -3.79). They carry magnitude, not sign. `breakout_probability` reproduces the `flyer_classifier` trap exactly. **Re-weighting these cannot help, and a one-tailed AUC will keep insisting it can** — added to `.claude/rules/measurement.md` as a standing rule with the three numbers any factor test must report. AUC implementation was verified against synthetic data (1.0 / 0.0 / ~0.5) before trusting the sign.
  - **The setup screeners themselves are inverted at 1 day.** Next-day open-to-close excess vs universe, per-date: **Gap Up >=2% -0.465pp (t=-8.80)** — the most significantly negative thing measured in this session — Breakout above 20d-high -0.185pp (t=-2.69), volume shocker >=3x -0.122pp (t=-1.67), Open=High -0.071pp (t=-1.85); **Gap Down <=-2% is +0.329pp (t=+3.70)** and Open=Low is null (+0.052pp, t=+1.41). Consistent with the documented "short-horizon momentum is negative at three horizons": this market mean-reverts at 1 day and the continuation screens are the wrong sign.
  - **One lead, explicitly not a finding.** `delivery_pct` is the only directionally-positive feature (direction AUC 0.565, t=+3.65, p=0.001, 25 dates). It is 1 of ~40 tested, the sample is small, and `measurement.md` already records `delivery_spike`/`delivery_trend` as dead. Settle with `factor_backtest.py`, do not add a weight.
  - **Verification**: all figures from read-only queries against live Postgres (:5433) in this session, per-date and re-derived; a `text = date` join failure on `technical_signals.date` (TEXT) vs `stock_ohlcv.date` (DATE) was hit and fixed mid-analysis — the "column type assumed from db.ts" trap the rules file documents. No code changed, nothing deployed; `.claude/rules/measurement.md` gained the both-tails rule.

- **Phase 3: purged 34,629 unreachable rows from the canonical table, fixed the weekend `computed_at` at source, and deliberately left the bearish path alone (2026-08-11).** Full detail in [plumbing_repairs_2026_08_11](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/plumbing_repairs_2026_08_11.md). Scope was chosen from measurement rather than the original brief — the brief said "rewrite the signal weighting", and Phase 2 had already shown weights are not the problem.
  - **Two of the four proposed fixes were already fixed at source, which corrects Phase 2's own report.** (a) The ghost rows were **not** "structural, not an incident" as recorded — `_restrict_to_tradeable_universe` (commit `b786b98`, 2026-07-31) already requires membership in both `nse_stocks` and recent `stock_ohlcv`, and **every `computed_at` from 07-31 onward was verified clean**. They were residue. (b) The 44% coverage gap was measured over the contaminated window; post-fix coverage runs 58.7% → **91.6%** (08-10) and is self-healing as the RL-gate significance fix propagates. Neither needed new prevention.
  - **29,014 ghost rows purged** (2,500 symbols with no `stock_ohlcv` bar on any date, 3,774 carrying a Buy/Sell label, spanning 2026-06-01..07-30 — the range ending exactly at the source fix, as predicted). New idempotent `data_integrity_repair.py --ghost-recommendations`. This is the **third occurrence** of "a source fix does not clean what the writer already wrote", after the 13,505 NaN rows and the three purge-gap tables. The delete predicate is deliberately *narrower* than the live filter — it removes a symbol only if `stock_ohlcv` has no bar for it on **any** date, not merely none in 30 days, so a delisting or a feed gap can never be mistaken for a ghost (pinned by a test that keeps a symbol priced only in 2021).
  - **5,615 rows on four non-trading-day snapshots purged, and the source fixed.** New `as_of.logical_session_date()` — `logical_trading_date()` rolled forward off a weekend — wired into `unified_ranker.run()` in place of `date.today()`. **Weekends only, deliberately**: `market_holidays` is built from observed gaps and stops at **2026-04-14**, so it cannot answer whether a future weekday is a holiday, and the asymmetry is what makes this safe — a holiday-dated snapshot is superseded by that holiday's own early-batch run hours later, a weekend-dated one never is. Deliberately **not** `logical_write_floor()`: that returns the last *completed* session, so a Monday 07:30 pre-market run would label its output Friday and overwrite Friday's snapshot. Verified all four weekend snapshots were immediately followed by a real trading-day snapshot with at least as many rows before deleting anything.
  - **THE outcome is the two fixes deliberately NOT made.** (1) **The bearish path was left untouched.** Phase 2 reported Sell as inverted (+0.080pp, t=+2.25, 23 dates); re-measured on post-fix dates only it is **+0.019pp, t=0.32, p=0.76 over 6 dates** — the full-window result is dominated by the pre-08-01 contaminated period. Six dates cannot support a change to live signal logic; gating the Sell path on that would be acting on noise. (2) **Coverage was left alone** — already self-healing, see above.
  - **The purge revealed a hole rather than creating one.** After it, 2026-07-21..07-30 read **0.3–0.5% coverage of the liquid universe**. Those dates genuinely ranked almost nothing tradeable; the ghost rows had been making the table *look* full. **A row count is not coverage.**
  - **Process note worth keeping: never `git checkout <file>` to undo a negative control on uncommitted work.** It reverts to HEAD and silently deletes the very function under test — cost one re-write here. Save the file's text and restore from that. Also: weekend membership is computed in Python, not `EXTRACT(DOW ...)`, because `computed_at` is TEXT and that function is Postgres-only — on the SQLite fallback the query fails and the caller silently gets nothing.
  - **Verification**: 27 new tests, **both fixes negative-controlled** (removing the weekend roll fails 5; widening the ghost predicate to the live 30-day filter fails 2). Full Python suite **1,682 passed / 198 skipped / 0 failed**. Both repairs dry-run, then executed against live Postgres and **queried back**: 0 ghosts, 0 weekend snapshots, 53,015 rows / 2,374 symbols / 36 dates, all priced, all real sessions. No `.ts` touched so no `pm2 restart`; `runPython()` re-reads `.py` per spawn, so the ranker picks up the date fix on its next run.

- **Screener-filter direction review: no individual screener gives clear direction, the population is significantly negative, and the sentiment labels are inverted (2026-08-11).** Read-only against live Postgres, panel spec throughout, entry at the session AFTER the screener flagged the symbol (open_{D+1} -> close), >=Rs 1cr ADT, `is_suspect` excluded, per-date then averaged.
  - **1,563 screeners across 4 providers, 720,824 appearances, 50 dates. 552 cleared the sample bar (>=10 dates, >=5 liquid members/date) at 1d. 51 were nominally significant at p<0.05 against 28 expected by chance -- and ZERO survived Benjamini-Hochberg FDR or Bonferroni.** That is the answer to "does any filter give clear direction": at a corrected bar, none does. At 5d two survived nominally (`moneycontrol/371 "Downward Momentum - Price & Volume"`, `moneycontrol/178 "Ben Graham Undervalued"`), but **5d returns on consecutive dates overlap 80%**, and Newey-West correction drops their t-stats from -5.27/-5.10 to **-3.70/-3.35** against the ~4.2 Bonferroni requires. Neither is established. mc/371 is the most coherent candidate -- a screen named "Downward Momentum" whose members underperform 0.57pp/5d and skew to the losing tail (4.7% in the day's bottom-50 vs 2.9% in the top-50) -- it works as labelled, as a SHORT signal, but not to a corrected bar.
  - **The population does have clear direction, and it is negative.** Mean per-screener 1d excess **-0.0596pp (t=-6.46, n=552)**; only 231/552 positive. Three of four sources individually significant: et_marketstats -0.19 (t=-4.73), trendlyne -0.048 (t=-4.51), etnow -0.076 (t=-2.51); moneycontrol insignificant. **Appearing on a screener is on average a mildly bad sign** -- consistent with the documented consensus IC of -0.027 (t=-2.36), now confirmed one screener at a time rather than in aggregate.
  - **THE actionable finding: the sentiment labels are significantly INVERTED, and the mechanism is visible in the schema.** Bullish-labelled screeners return **-0.1204pp (t=-6.62)**; bearish-labelled are indistinguishable from zero (-0.0138, t=-0.96). **bullish minus bearish = -0.1066pp, t=-4.61, p<0.001** -- a working label system needs that difference significantly POSITIVE. Not a mystery: `screener_master.classified_by` is `keyword` for **all 1,669** rows (avg `category_confidence` 0.712), so `inferred_sentiment` is pattern-matched off the screener's *name* and has never been validated against an outcome. The bearish tag is noise; the bullish tag is actively wrong.
  - **Two categories survive Bonferroni across the 10 tested, both negative**: `technical` (-0.042, t=-3.38 at 1d) and `technical_momentum` (-0.136, t=-3.21 at 1d; **-0.673, t=-3.29 at 5d**, the most negative category found). Nothing with a positive sign reached significance at either horizon -- closest was `technical_trend` at 5d (+0.090, t=+1.99, p=0.051), which is *negative* at 1d. Same short-horizon-momentum-is-negative result the factor panel and the Gap-Up/breakout setups independently produced this session.
  - **`screener_master.tier` is degenerate and cannot prioritise anything**: 1,527 of 1,669 rows are `D`, 112 `Unranked`, 29 `C`, **1 `B`, and no `A` at all**. Every one of the 552 screeners that met the sample bar was tier D, so tier carries no information for exactly the screeners there is enough data to judge. Same shape as the constant-engine problem `drop_zero_dispersion_engines()` already handles in the ranker.
  - **Two schema traps hit while measuring:** `screener_appearances.source` is lowercase while `screener_master.source` is title-case (join case-insensitively or silently get zero rows), and `screener_appearances.screener_id` is TEXT holding slugs like `1h-adx-bearish-divergence` as well as numeric ids -- comparing it to an int matches nothing, silently. Also re-hit the documented literal-`%` trap in my own harness: psycopg2 interpolates whenever params is not None, so `LIKE` with a percent-wildcard blows up if an empty tuple is passed instead of None.
  - **Recorded in `.claude/rules/measurement.md`** so the 1,563-screener sweep is not re-run without a reason, including the overlap-correction requirement. No code changed; this was a measurement pass.

- **Data audit: which of the platform's data actually suits a directional test, and the one factor it settled (2026-08-11).** Profiled all 60 symbol+date tables in the live database for real history depth, then used the deepest relevant one to close the open lead from the reverse audit.
  - **Of 60 symbol+date tables, only 9 are testable, 13 are marginal, and 35 cannot be tested at all.** The bar was stated before measuring, not fitted after: >=100 distinct dates AND >=6 months span. The calendar constraint `measurement.md` already recorded for fundamentals/analyst/ownership/earnings is **far wider than those four categories** -- 35 tables start at ~2026-06-30 with under 40 distinct dates. That is a wall-clock problem, not an engineering one; no modelling fixes it.
  - **The deep data, ranked.** `trendlyne_pb_history` and `trendlyne_pe_history` are by a wide margin the deepest factor data the platform holds -- **3,028 distinct dates spanning 12.6 years (2013-12-24 -> 2026-08-11), ~4.1M rows and ~2,420 symbols each** -- and they are exactly what underpins `value_book_to_price`, the one provisional positive factor on record. Then `insider_trades` (1,635 dates / 11y, already measured a clean null), `stock_ohlcv` (1,394 / 5.6y), `nse_universe_history` (1,387 / 5.6y), **`macro_asset_prices` (1,242 dates / 4.7y, 81 assets, never factor-tested)**, **`feature_store` (398 dates / 19 months, 2,424 symbols, a ready-made technical panel, never factor-tested)**, `stock_delivery_data` (286 / 14 months), `news_symbol_link` (188 dates but sparse across 15y).
  - **THE result: `delivery_pct` is the strongest directional factor measured in this codebase, and the audit is what made it measurable.** Phase 2 flagged it as a lead on 25 dates of `technical_signals` and explicitly refused to act on it; `stock_delivery_data` carries **275 usable dates**. Measured with the panel spec (next-day OPEN entry, >=Rs 1cr ADT, `is_suspect` excluded, per-date then averaged): direction AUC **0.541 at 1d (t=+8.85)** and **0.546 at 5d (t=+10.99)**; top-minus-bottom quintile **+0.1917pp/day (t=+7.82)** and **+0.4812pp/5d (t=+9.94)**. The 1d figure uses non-overlapping windows, so unlike the screener 5d numbers it needs no HAC correction.
  - **It passes the both-tails test in the strongest possible form**, which is why this one is credible where `hv_20d`/`breakout_probability` were not: AUC vs the winning tail is **0.444** and vs the losing tail **0.403** -- *both below 0.5*, i.e. high-delivery names avoid both tails because they are the calm ones -- while the gainer-vs-loser AUC is **above** 0.5. A volatility proxy shows the opposite signature (both tail AUCs high, direction AUC ~0.5). This does not contradict the documented "delivery_spike / delivery_trend are dead (t=-1.08/-1.43)": those are *derived* features (spike vs own average, trend); this is the raw level.
  - **Deliberately NOT wired into anything.** +0.19pp/day gross against ~15bps round-trip costs makes a daily rebalance marginal at best; the 5d construction carries a fifth of the turnover and is the more plausible form. The next step is `factor_backtest.py` (turnover-aware, survivorship-free), not a weight in the ranker. **Caveat that cannot be closed from the data**: 88% of `stock_delivery_data` was backfilled, so `updated_at` cannot prove point-in-time availability for historical rows -- what it does show is that the live path writes trade-date D after the 15:30 IST close (11 of 12 same-day write-hour buckets are post-16:30 IST, all from 2026-06 onward), which is tradeable at D+1's open.
  - **Two data defects found in passing.** `stock_delivery_data.trades` is a byte-for-byte duplicate of `delivery_qty` in **100% of 664,006 rows** -- the trade-count column is being fed delivery quantity, so any consumer reading `trades` is reading the wrong quantity. And `mc_earnings_forecast`'s date column holds non-ISO values like `'Mar 2024'`, so it will not cast to a date at all (it was the only one of the 60 tables that failed profiling outright).
  - Recorded in `.claude/rules/measurement.md` -- both the testability inventory and the `delivery_pct` result, the latter added to the already-tested factor table so it is not re-derived. No code changed; this was a measurement pass.

- **Screener timing challenged and tested; feature_store and macro screened; urls.txt re-explored (2026-08-11).** Follow-ups to the data audit, all read-only measurement.
  - **The screeners are DESCRIPTIVE, not predictive - the "these are intraday lists" hypothesis is right about what they are, and it does not rescue them as signals.** Per-date excess vs the same-day universe: **same day (prev-close to close) +0.500pp, t=+12.70**; `technical_momentum` screeners **+0.990pp, t=+9.24**. Next session: **+0.003pp, t=+0.13** overall, **-0.106pp, t=-2.03** for momentum screeners. A screener lists a stock *because* it moved, so the same-day gain is the cause of the listing, not a capturable return - entering at that day's open would require knowing at 09:15 which names get flagged at 11:00. The forward test therefore remains the correct one for tradeability, and its answer is unchanged.
  - **The genuinely open version of the question cannot be tested at all with current data.** `screener_appearances.appeared_date` is TIMESTAMPTZ but every one of 720,824 rows is stamped `00:00:00` - **exactly one distinct time** - so the capture moment is never recorded and "enter at flag time, exit at close" is unmeasurable. The price side is ready: `intraday_ohlcv` holds 15-minute bars for 2,353 symbols over **215 days**. The fix is a NEW column, not a change to the existing one, because `appeared_date` sits in the PK `(screener_id, symbol, appeared_date)` and putting a real time there would break dedup for repeat appearances. Until then, treat "intraday screener edge" as unmeasured rather than disproven.
  - **`feature_store` adds no new positive factor, and confirms 5-day mean reversion hard.** 18 pre-registered columns not already in `factor_backtest`'s FACTORS, 396 dates, Bonferroni across the 16 that had data. **10 clear it and every trend/overbought reading is negative**: `vwap_dist_pct` -1.487pp/5d (t=-14.06), stochastics -0.73 (t=-13.6), `williams_r` -0.71 (t=-13.0), `cci` -0.60 (t=-10.3), `di_plus` -0.69 (t=-11.2), plus `atr_pct`, `obv_slope`, `mtf_alignment_score`. The single positive is **`di_minus` +0.463pp (t=+7.70)** - the *downtrend* indicator - i.e. the same 5-day mean reversion the Gap-Down-beats-Gap-Up result showed. `debt_to_equity` and `piotroski_f` clear Bonferroni positive but at +0.03pp/5d are economically trivial, with direction-AUC ~0.502 (no tail discrimination).
  - **`macro_asset_prices` is not a cross-sectional factor and was not tested as one.** Only 8 of its symbols have real history (CRUDE, GOLD, DXY, US10Y, SP500, INDIAVIX, NIFTY50, NSEBANK, ~1,190 days from 2021-11); the rest have 32 days. Eight assets cannot rank 1,500 stocks - this is regime/conditioning data, and testing it as a stock-selection factor would be a category error. Its stock-level exposures exist (`crude_corr_90d` etc. in `technical_signals`) but that table has only 64 dates.
  - **urls.txt is ~99% mined after four prior passes; only 2 of ~25 hosts have no fetcher.** `oxide.sensibull.com` (7 URLs incl. a month-parameterised `fii_dii_daily?year_month=` and `iv_chart/NIFTY` - history-bearing, so genuinely interesting) returns **HTTP 403 "access denied"** on every endpoint tried; reported rather than worked around, per the standing preference. `etinsights.indiatimes.com` returns **HTTP 200** and advertises `dataAvailabilityInYears: 21` back to Jan 2005 - but the payload is *summary statistics* (probabilityGain/Decline, movement-distribution buckets, consecutive-streak episode lists), not a time series. Computed over the whole history, so at any historical date it embeds future data: **look-ahead by construction**, and derived from prices already held in `stock_ohlcv`. **Not a usable new factor source.**
  - **Strategic note following from the data audit**: a brand-new datasource starts with zero history, and this platform's binding constraint is history depth (35 of 60 tables are untestable for exactly that reason). A new source cannot help for 6-12 months. The only new sources worth chasing are ones that serve *history* - which is what made the month-parameterised sensibull FII/DII endpoint the pick of urls.txt, and why its 403 is the thing worth resolving.

- **Phase 4: the four defects found today now have automated guards, and the measurement harness checks its own benchmark (2026-08-11).** The audit asked for a verification loop plus scheduled execution. Most of it already existed, so this closed the gaps rather than building a parallel system.
  - **What already existed, found before building anything**: `signalAccuracyDigest.ts` runs at the tail of ml-daily-ops and pushes recall + wrong-direction calls to Telegram, fed by `high_flyer_retrospective.py` nightly; `dataQualityChecks.ts` runs 62 checks on a daily cron with alerting; `factor_backtest.py` exists precisely so factor claims are reproducible. **Scheduled execution was already solved.** The real gap was that **not one of today's four defects would have been caught by any of it** -- every table was fresh and full throughout, just full of the wrong rows, and freshness is all those checks measure.
  - **Four new checks in `dataQualityChecks.ts`, each pinned to a defect that was live today**: ghost symbols (29,014 rows naming instruments with no `stock_ohlcv` history), non-trading-day snapshots (9,096 rows on Sat/Sun), liquid-universe coverage (ran **0.3%** on seven July dates while the existing row-count check read healthy), and `stock_delivery_data.trades = delivery_qty` (664,006 rows). Each carries the exact repair command in its failure detail. **All four SQL bodies validated against live Postgres** (0 / 0 / 1387-of-1519 / 0) and **negative-controlled in vitest against the real broken values** -- 1534/8 for the coverage check, 29014 for ghosts, 664006 for delivery.
  - **THE guard that matters most: `factor_backtest.py` now sanity-checks its own benchmark.** Every figure it reports is an excess over the universe, so a broken universe silently rescales every verdict -- and it printed **-4.66%/month, i.e. -99.9% over 5.5 years for a market that roughly tripled**, sitting unchallenged beside authoritative-looking t-stats for a day. `_summarize` now annualises the universe and sets `benchmark_sane`; `_print` **refuses to issue a verdict at all** outside -40%..+80% and names exit pricing as the first thing to check. Deliberately symmetric: an implausibly *good* benchmark is equally a broken harness. Live after the fix: **+10.38%/yr, guard silent, verdict given.** 5 tests, including that a verdict is withheld on an insane benchmark ("a verdict on a void benchmark is worse than no verdict").
  - **`tsc` earned its place**: it rejected `category: 'data'`, which is not in the check union. Worth recording because the earlier `npx tsc --noEmit | tail` invocations in this session were reporting **tail's** exit code, not tsc's -- the empty output was the real signal. Do not trust `$?` after a pipe.
  - **Reported, not fixed -- a live-network test gates `npm test`.** `src/server/__tests__/mcapiProxy.test.ts` fetches MoneyControl's premarket endpoint and asserts HTTP 200. It passed earlier in this session and fails now; a direct curl to that endpoint returns **422**, and the file is unmodified by this work. So the tracked test suite (and CI) fails whenever a third party has a hiccup. The Python side already solved this -- `@pytest.mark.live_datasource`, skipped by default, never in CI, per `data-sources.md` -- but the TypeScript side has no equivalent gate. Belongs to whoever owns that test.
  - **Verification**: pytest **1,697 passed / 208 skipped**; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (checked bare, not through a pipe); `schema:drift` clean at 198 tables; recurring-bug checker clean over 428 files; `vitest --dir src` 853 passed with the single upstream-dependent failure above.

- **Gated the live MoneyControl proxy test; `npm test` is green and 10x faster (2026-08-11).** Closing the item Phase 4 reported but did not fix.
  - **`mcapiProxy.test.ts` boots a local proxy and fetches MoneyControl's real API in all 28 cases**, so when `/mcapi/v1/premarket/get-global-marketdata` began returning 400/422 upstream the suite went red with nothing in this repo changed. Gated behind `RUN_LIVE_DATASOURCE_TESTS=1` via `describe.runIf` -- **deliberately the same env var as the Python side's `@pytest.mark.live_datasource`** rather than a second TypeScript-only flag, so one switch runs every live check in the repo in both languages. **A gate, not a deletion**: opted in, all 28 still execute (27 pass, 1 fails on the genuine upstream 422), and it remains the only thing that catches the proxy's path rewrites drifting from MoneyControl's real routes.
  - **Also excluded `.claude/worktrees/**` from vitest**, which turned out to be the larger problem. Other concurrent sessions keep checkouts of this same repo there, so vitest was collecting a stale duplicate of every test file in each -- **1,010 test files instead of ~96**, most of the wall-clock, plus failures from other people's in-progress work. **CI never saw it** because the directory is gitignored, and that made it worse rather than better: the local suite disagreed with CI, and a local red that is always present is a local red you stop reading. Note vitest replaces its defaults wholesale when `exclude` is set, so `node_modules`/`dist`/`build` are restated deliberately.
  - **Result: 95 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed, in 45s instead of 489s.** `tsc` exit 0.
  - **`data-sources.md`'s live-datasource mandate now covers TypeScript explicitly**, with the `describe.runIf` snippet, so the next live TS test is gated by default rather than discovered when it breaks a build. The standing reason is worth repeating: **a suite that fails on someone else's outage stops being a signal** -- the pressure becomes to ignore red CI rather than to fix it.
- **Investigated a pasted StockAI data-integrity + job-health digest (2026-08-11) from a sandbox with no live Postgres access.** Two code-level bugs found and fixed by reading, negative-controlled, and confirmed correct on a real SQLite/`translateSql()` round-trip; two other reported items (`technical_signals.signal_score` stuck at one value, Market Regime Detector stale since 2026-07-05) were investigated but **left unfixed** -- no plausible root cause survived code-only review, and this session had no way to query the actual production DB to reverse-engineer against real data, per `measurement.md`'s own standing rule against guessing. Flagging both for the next session with DB access rather than shipping a speculative fix.
  - **Fixed: `getSystemStatus()` (`monitor.router.ts`) could never report `runState: 'failed'` for a MONITOR_SCRIPTS entry driven by its normal BullMQ schedule -- only ever `'stale'`.** `rawState` (the only input the failed/stale branch checked) comes from `app_settings.monitor_<id>`, written *only* by `triggerScript`'s manual "run now" tRPC path; a script's real scheduled failure lands in `job_heartbeat` via `recordHeartbeat()`/`updateMonitorState()` instead -- a completely different store this branch never read, even though `job_heartbeat.last_error` was already being surfaced separately as the `error` field. So a genuinely failed cron run (e.g. `regime_detector.py --mode update` throwing `RuntimeError` because `hmm_regime.pkl` is missing) always downgraded to a bare "stale" label in the daily Telegram digest, hiding the real, already-captured exception. Fixed by also selecting `job_heartbeat.last_status` and trusting it as evidence of `'failed'` only when its `last_run_at` is at least as recent as the DB-freshness `lastRunAt` (i.e. the failure is why nothing fresher landed, not a stale failure a later heartbeat-less success has since superseded). 3 new tests in `monitorSystemStatus.test.ts` (surfaces a real heartbeat failure as `'failed'` with the real error message; still reports `'stale'` with zero heartbeat evidence; a stale prior failure predating the last known-good data does NOT mask as `'failed'`) -- negative-controlled by reverting the router change and confirming the new failure-surfacing test genuinely fails first.
  - **Fixed: `technical-signals-freshness-coverage`'s win_probability coverage ratio included TODAY's still-in-progress rows in its denominator, every single weekday between the 8:30am-4pm IST scan and the evening `ml-ensemble-score` run that actually scores them.** `checkAndAlertDataQuality` polls this check every 15 minutes all day, so `WHERE date >= date('now','-3 days')` (unchanged) meant coverage was measuring "yesterday (scored) + today (not scored yet)" for most of every trading day -- reading ~50% with nothing actually broken (matches the observed "2185 rows" then "4373 rows" growth across the same digest run, and the suspiciously exact ~50% split). Same underlying shape as the `stale`-side fix already applied to this exact check on 2026-08-10 (Monday-morning-vs-Friday's-data), just never migrated to the coverage ratio. Fixed by computing `total`/`scored` over the most recently **completed** trading day (`date = (SELECT MAX(date) ... WHERE date < date('now'))`) while `last_date` stays unbounded so a genuine multi-day outage still fails via the existing staleness branch. Verified end-to-end against a real in-memory `better-sqlite3` DB (10 completed-day rows at 80% coverage + 20 same-day unscored rows correctly reports 80%, not the 26.7% the old query would have blended) and against `translateSql()`'s Postgres output. The pure `evaluate()` tests were already correct (this file's own design keeps SQL untested there by intent) and needed no change.
  - **Investigated, not fixed -- `technical_signals.signal_score` reported identical across all rows in the 3-day window.** `detectSignals()`/`scoreSignals()` read clean: signal detection is a sparse set of specific bullish technical setups (breakout, golden cross, MACD crossover, ...), and a row with zero of them but "meaningful" news gets persisted at `signal_score=0` *by design* (`isMeaningfulNewsSentiment`, 2026-08-06 fix, see `newsSentimentPersistenceGate.test.ts`) -- so `distinct_scores<=1` is technically satisfiable without a bug if literally every real technical setup happened to be absent platform-wide for 3 straight sessions, which is possible but not verifiable from code alone. No exception path, recent diff (`73071bc`'s news-modifier removal doesn't change which signals fire, only the multiplier on non-news scores), or SQL/schema issue was found that would force this. Needs a live query of `technical_signals.signals_json`/`signal_score` for the actual 3 dates to tell "real quiet market + broad news gate" from "detectSignals() silently broken" apart.
  - **Investigated, not fixed -- Market Regime Detector (`market_regimes.computed_at`) reported stale at 2026-07-05, contradicting two entries already in this log.** The 2026-08-05 entry (line ~455) live-queried `market_regimes` as fresh through 2026-08-04; the 2026-08-09 entry (degenerate-HMM-states fix, `59a767e`) claims a full historical backfill through 2026-08-07 "live-run against production Postgres." Read the diff: `59a767e` only touches `train_hmm()`/`_assign_state_labels()` (manual/on-demand only -- `regime_detector.py --mode train` is never scheduled anywhere in `queues.ts`/`dl.jobs.ts`); the daily cron path (`dl-regime-daily`, `--mode update` -> `update_regime()` -> `_label_day()`) is textually unchanged by that commit and unconditionally stamps `computed_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` on every successful write, so nothing in this diff explains a frozen July 5 timestamp persisting past an Aug 9 backfill that (per its own log entry) should have moved it to ~Aug 9. Either the Aug 9 backfill didn't actually land on the same production DB this monitoring reads, or the daily cron has been failing (`update_regime()` raises `RuntimeError` if `hmm_regime.pkl` is missing) since before 2026-08-05's check and that check's own live-query claim doesn't hold up -- this sandbox has no Postgres access (`POSTGRES_URL` points at `127.0.0.1:5433`, connection refused here) to tell which. Next session with DB access: `SELECT date, computed_at FROM market_regimes ORDER BY computed_at DESC LIMIT 5` settles it in one query.
  - **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full `vitest run` 859 passed / 28 skipped / 0 failed (95 files) including the 3 new tests, negative-controlled per `recurring-bugs.md`'s testing discipline. No `.py` files touched, so no pytest run. No live-DB verification was possible in this sandbox (`psql` connection refused) -- flagged per file rather than silently claimed done.


- **The technical signal scan had been dead for two sessions behind a green heartbeat; root-caused, fixed, both days backfilled (2026-08-11).** Started from the daily data-integrity report's `technical_signals signal_score variance` failure ("signal score is identical across all 4373 rows -- looks stuck at a default"), which was the only monitor that noticed anything.
  - **The check's wording pointed the wrong way, and the DB said so immediately.** `signal_score` was not stuck at a default -- it was *absent*. Rows per date by `signal_type`: every trading day through 2026-08-07 carried both a `GRID` block (~2,170 rows, written 19:30 UTC by `backfill_technical_features.py`'s `run_full_universe_today`, `signal_score` hardcoded 0) *and* a scan block written 06:00-10:00 UTC. **On 08-10 and 08-11 the scan block was simply not there** -- 2,185 and 2,188 rows, all `GRID`, all score 0, `signals_json` NULL for every row. So "identical across all rows" meant "the only writer left is the one that writes a constant".
  - **Root cause: `quant_scores` has no `date` column, and a query added 2026-08-07 ordered by it.** The `rank_composite` pre-fetch in `technicalSignalsService.ts` wrapped the table in `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY symbol ORDER BY date DESC)`. `quant_scores` is `PRIMARY KEY (symbol)` -- 2,424 rows, 2,424 symbols, one row each -- so the latest-per-symbol window was **never needed in the first place**, and the column it ordered by does not exist. Postgres raised `column "date" does not exist` on every scan, **after** the scan had done all its work: the logs read `[SIGNALS] Found 114 setups` immediately followed by `[SIGNALS] Scan failed`, 13 times on 08-11 alone. Every setup was computed and then thrown away at the write.
  - **A second, silent instance of the identical bug in `signals.ts`**, from the same 2026-08-07 dead-column sweep: `ORDER BY qs.date DESC` inside a scalar subquery feeding `recommendation_log.quant_score`, wrapped in `.catch(() => null)`. It never logged anything; it just wrote NULL into the column the sweep had added it to populate. Found by grepping every `quant_scores` reader rather than fixing only the path the alert named.
  - **Why nothing paged for two days -- the more important half.** The `technical-scan` heartbeat read `success` throughout. The queue's market-hours guard returns early outside the session, and that early return fell through to the `completed` handler, which stamped `updateMonitorState('technical-scan', 'success')`. So the 16:00 and 19:10 post-close runs **overwrote the 13 real failures from earlier the same day**, every day. The job digest duly reported 148 healthy jobs. Fixed by returning `{ skipped: true }` and having the `completed` handler decline to stamp success on it: **a skip is not a success.** This is the "success heartbeat on a step that silently wrote nothing" class that `measurement.md` already warns about, in a new location.
  - **Verification, live.** Negative-controlled at the SQL level against production Postgres -- the pre-fix query raises `UndefinedColumn`, the fixed one returns rows for RELIANCE/INFY/HDFCBANK. Then `pm2 restart bharat-server` (the fix is `.ts`; committed is not deployed) and ran the scan for real: **275 records upserted for 08-11**, `signal_score` now 8 distinct values spanning -4..6 where it had been a single 0. Re-ran with `{ date: '2026-08-10' }` -- the scan's point-in-time path bounds win-rates, weights, OHLCV and news by `scanDate`, so the lost session is recoverable rather than gone -- **396 records, 10 distinct scores, -3..6**. Both failing data-quality checks now pass on their own data. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; vitest 95 files / 856 tests passed.
  - **No new test.** The failure is "SQL names a column that does not exist", which only a real Postgres catches, and the guard that actually caught it -- the `signal_score` variance check -- already exists and did its job in under 48 hours. What was missing was not a test but an honest heartbeat, and that is what changed.

- **Cleared the rest of the daily report: two of the four "failures" were the monitoring lying, not the jobs (2026-08-11, same session).** Data-quality now reads **80/83 with zero failures**, down from 4 open items.
  - **Strategy Optimizer "stale, last Aug 03" was a false alarm produced by its own promotion gate working correctly.** `getLastRunAt('strategy-optimizer')` probes `MAX(snapshot_at) FROM screener_weight_history`, but the optimiser **deliberately writes nothing** when the optimised weights lose to baseline on held-out data -- which is exactly what happened on 2026-08-09 (holdout 0.4139 vs baseline 0.4437) and 2026-08-10 (0.4317 vs 0.4640). The job ran clean both weeks. **Every correctly-rejected run made the monitor report it staler**, so the better the gate performed the more broken the job looked. Fixed in `monitor.router.ts`: `lastRunAt` is now the **latest** of the output-table probe, the stored `_ran_at`, and `job_heartbeat.last_success_at`, instead of `dbLastRunAt ?? storedRanAt`. The heartbeat matters most -- `updateMonitorState()` writes it on every scheduled run, while `monitor_<id>_ran_at` is only stamped by the manual trigger path, so for a cron-driven script the heartbeat is the **only** non-output-table evidence that exists. Verified live: strategy-optimizer now reads `success / 2026-08-09`, previously `stale / 2026-08-03`. **This is general, not a special case** -- it fixes the class for every gated script whose output table a healthy run may legitimately not write.
